The default functional form, pending the validation program, is the
bounded geometric aggregate of the five factors:

Confidence_(G) = A^(β)_(A) · F^(β)_(F) · L^(β)_(L) · D^(β)_(D) ·
C^(β)_(C), ∑_(k) β_(k) = 1

The geometric form is chosen because it is pessimistic: any factor that
collapses to zero collapses the whole score, so three perfectly agreeing
sources that are one second from staleness do not produce a publishable
price. A linear weighted mean is the perturbation alternative (Section
23.6). The exponents, the factor transforms and the threshold C_min
(validation-stage default 0.60) are Risk-Council parameters within
constitutional envelopes, published with each methodology version. The
Modification Specification states the same function with five arguments,
naming the last factor Coverage rather than SourceCoverage [MS §55]; the
COO-20 naming is adopted as normative here, and the two formulations are
otherwise identical.

The consequence rule is constitutional (Invariant I9): if Confidence_G
falls below C_min, the affected calculation enters degraded mode rather
than allowing bad data to rewrite the basket [MS §55]. The score flows
to the state machine of Section 17.7, into the daily state vector as
OracleConfidence_t (Section 18.3), into the crisis-score inputs (Section
5.5) and into the daily publication (Section 24.3), so every consumer of
the gold price can see exactly how much trust it carried.

17.7 Oracle States and Degradation Modes

The oracle layer tracks state at two levels: per-source state,
describing each provider feed, and the aggregate oracle state,
describing the layer as a whole [MS §56, BP §9.7]. Each source may be in
one of five states:

s_(i) ∈ {VALID, DEGRADED, STALE, OUTLIER, HALTED}

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Source State            Meaning                 Treatment in
                                                  Aggregation
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  VALID                   Passed all four checks  Included.
                          of Section 17.3 in the  
                          current round.          

  DEGRADED                Fresh enough to be      Included, but drags the
                          usable but weak —       Freshness and
                          missed heartbeat, wide  SourceCoverage factors
                          confidence interval, or down; warning emitted.
                          decaying reliability.   

  STALE                   Timestamp older than    Discarded for the
                          the staleness threshold round.
                          (Section 17.3.1).       

  OUTLIER                 Valid but beyond the    Discarded for the
                          2.5% deviation breaker  round.
                          (Section 17.3.3).       

  HALTED                  Dead — age beyond twice Discarded; alert
                          staleness (Section      escalated to risk
                          17.8) — or explicitly   states (Chapter 21).
                          halted by governance.   
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

The system needs a minimum independent-source quorum at all times [MS
§56], and the aggregate state summarizes whether it has one:

OracleState ∈ {NORMAL, DEGRADED, FAILED}

-   NORMAL — the quorum is met, Confidence_G is at or above C_min, and
    no unresolved correlation warnings stand.

-   DEGRADED — the quorum is met but Confidence_G < C_min (or coverage
    has collapsed to a single source family). Affected calculations use
    last-known-good values and may not rewrite the basket [MS §55];
    minting and rebalancing pause per the fallback rules below.

-   FAILED — the quorum is lost (fewer than two valid independent
    feeds). All operations, including redemption, pause until feeds are
    restored.

The state machine is intentionally asymmetric: entering DEGRADED is
automatic on any confidence breach, but leaving it requires confidence
to recover above C_min on a subsequent round — the same
recovery-confirmation discipline the reserve rules apply to solvency
(Chapter 14). Degradation is a risk state (Chapter 21), not an error to
be retried silently. The fallback behavior by scenario is carried from
the v1.2 blueprint [BP §9.7]:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Scenario                            Action
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  One feed stale                      Discard it; use the other two.

  Two feeds stale                     Pause minting and rebalancing; use
                                      last known good price for viewing.

  All three feeds stale               Pause ALL operations (including
                                      redemption) until feeds are
                                      restored.

  One feed deviates > 2.5%            Discard it; use the other two.

  All feeds deviate > 2.5%            Use the geometric mean of the three
                                      as a conservative fallback, but
                                      flag ORACLE_DISPUTE.
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

P̄ = (P₁ · P₂ · P₃)^(1/3)

The geometric-mean fallback exists for the pathological case in which
all feeds are live but mutually inconsistent — a dispute, not an outage.
It is conservative because the geometric mean never exceeds the
arithmetic mean, so the fallback cannot inflate the aggregate above what
the sources jointly support; the ORACLE_DISPUTE flag forces escalation
into the risk states of Chapter 21 and blocks state-changing consumption
of the price. Degraded mode propagates predictably downstream: the index
uses last-known-good values and flags them (Chapter 9), MARP gates hold
execution (Chapter 11), reserve valuation applies conservative prices
(Chapters 13–14), minting pauses while redemption remains available in
DEGRADED (users can exit at the prevailing price — Chapter 19), and the
daily publication reports the degraded state rather than hiding it
(Section 24.3).

17.8 Staleness and Heartbeat Management

Each feed has a staleness threshold (its maximum age, the hard validity
bound of Section 17.3.1) and a heartbeat (its expected update frequency)
[BP §9.6]. The two are deliberately distinct instruments: staleness is a
constitutional binary gate — a feed older than its threshold is
discarded — while the heartbeat is an operational expectation against
which feed health is continuously monitored. A feed that keeps missing
its expected cadence while remaining within staleness is DEGRADED, not
yet STALE, so the state machine of Section 17.7 raises warnings before
the feed actually fails:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Feed Type               Staleness (max age)     Heartbeat (expected)
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  EUR/USD, GBP/USD, PAXG  60s                     15s

  JPY/USD, CNY/USD        120s                    30s

  VIX, DXY                120s                    60s
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

alive(i) ⇔ t_(now) - τ_(i) ≤ 2 θ_(stale,i)

The liveness rule above is the isFeedAlive() convention: a feed is
considered dead when its age exceeds twice the staleness threshold [BP
§9.6]. The factor of two separates DEGRADED territory (between the
heartbeat and staleness) from HALTED territory (beyond twice staleness),
giving the operators a full degradation interval in which to intervene.
Heartbeat misses also feed the Freshness factor of the confidence score
(Section 17.6): a feed publishing at three times its heartbeat drags
confidence down even while it is formally valid, which is precisely the
behavior a trust function should have. Staleness bounds are
constitutional constants (Section 17.10); heartbeats are Risk-Council
operational parameters, because expected update cadences are properties
of the providers, not of the monetary constitution.

17.9 Cross-Source Correlation Detection

To detect correlated failures, the protocol tracks the historical
deviation between sources [BP §9.9]. The rule: if two sources deviate by
more than 1.5% from each other for 3 consecutive updates, the protocol
flags SOURCE_CORRELATION_WARNING and temporarily switches to using the
median of all three — even if one is an outlier — to avoid
market-manipulation exploits:

δ_(ij,t) > 1.5% for 3 consecutive updates ⇒ SOURCE\_CORRELATION\_WARNING

The logic composes with the deviation breaker of Section 17.3.3 rather
than duplicating it. The breaker protects against one bad feed when the
other two agree; the correlation detector fires when agreement itself
breaks down persistently — the pattern produced either by two feeds
drifting into a shared bad source or by an attacker moving one market
that several feeds reference. Switching to the all-three median during
the warning is deliberately anti-fragile: it refuses to discard any
source while the sources dispute each other, so an attacker cannot shape
the aggregate by getting one honest feed outlier-censored. The
correlation warning counter resets to zero whenever the pair deviation
falls back inside the threshold.

Warnings feed three consumers: the source-independence check of Section
17.3.4 (a warned feed pair is treated as same-family until proven
otherwise), the SourceCoverage factor of the confidence score (Section
17.6), and the risk-state escalation path of Chapter 21, where
persistent warnings hold MARP rebalancing paused until the correlation
resolves. The 1.5% threshold and the 3-update persistence requirement
are Risk-Council parameters (4/7), with the initial values above; both
are published with the methodology version so that the detection rule is
reproducible.

17.10 Asset-Specific Oracle Addresses

The aggregator must be deployed with the correct oracle addresses for
the target chain [BP §9.5]. The v1.2 reference deployment targeted
Arbitrum Mainnet; the entries below are placeholder identifiers of the
deployment form, to be replaced with the correct addresses for the
chosen target chain at deployment time. Each row becomes one OracleFeed
configuration record — the Chainlink aggregator address, the Pyth feed
identifier, the Chronicle read address, plus the per-feed staleness
threshold and plausible-range bounds (the struct is carried in Listing
9):

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Asset / Pair      Chainlink Feed    Pyth Feed ID        Chronicle Feed
  ----------------- ----------------- ------------------- ------------------------
  EUR/USD           0x...EURUSD       0x...EURUSD_Pyth    0x...EURUSD_Chronicle

  GBP/USD           0x...GBPUSD       0x...GBPUSD_Pyth    0x...GBPUSD_Chronicle

  JPY/USD           0x...JPYUSD       0x...JPYUSD_Pyth    0x...JPYUSD_Chronicle

  CNY/USD (CNH)     0x...CNYUSD       0x...CNYUSD_Pyth    0x...CNYUSD_Chronicle

  PAXG (Gold)       0x...PAXG         0x...PAXG_Pyth      0x...PAXG_Chronicle

  GOLD_REF          0x...GOLDREF      0x...GOLDREF_Pyth   0x...GOLDREF_Chronicle
  (reference unit,                                        
  v2.0)                                                   

  VIX               0x...VIX          0x...VIX_Pyth       0x...VIX_Chronicle

  DXY               0x...DXY          0x...DXY_Pyth       0x...DXY_Chronicle
  --------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The two gold rows implement the reference/executable split of Section
17.4: GOLD_REF is the LBMA-basis reference-unit feed consumed by the
index and the canonical price, while PAXG is the tokenized-gold market
feed consumed by execution and the conservative minimum rule. Feed
identifiers are keyed by bytes32 (for example EUR/USD, PAXG, GOLD_REF)
so that the same aggregation path serves every row. Address
configuration changes are Risk-Council actions (setFeed in Listing 9)
and are emitted as events; a misconfigured or rotated address is
therefore an auditable governance fact, never a silent deployment
detail.

  Testnet deployments use simulated oracles and simulated assets; the
  full testnet address sets, chain identifiers and simulation parameters
  live in Appendix D and must never be mixed with mainnet configuration.
  The address table above is mainnet-form and carries no secrets — only
  identifiers to be resolved at deployment.

The developer-facing constants of the oracle layer, with their
governance classes, are [BP §9.11]:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Parameter               Value                   Governance
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  Chainlink Staleness     60s                     Constitutional
  (Major FX)                                      (immutable)

  Chainlink Staleness     120s                    Constitutional
  (Minor FX)                                      (immutable)

  Pyth Max Confidence     0.5%                    Risk Council (4/7)
  (Major FX)                                      

  Pyth Max Confidence     1.0%                    Risk Council (4/7)
  (Minor FX)                                      

  Deviation Circuit       2.5%                    Risk Council (4/7)
  Breaker                                         

  Correlation Warning     1.5%                    Risk Council (4/7)
  Threshold                                       

  Min Feeds Required      2                       Constitutional
  (quorum)                                        (immutable)

  Confidence threshold    0.60 (validation-stage) Risk Council (4/7),
  C_min (v2.0, §17.6)                             constitutional envelope
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

17.11 Contract State and Events

The aggregator's persistent state is deliberately small — configuration,
history, warning counters and fallback caches — so that the
trust-critical surface stays auditable [BP §9.10.1]:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Variable                Type                    Purpose
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  oracleFeeds             mapping(bytes32 =>      Per-feed configuration:
                          OracleFeed)             provider addresses,
                                                  staleness, confidence
                                                  cap, plausible range.

  priceHistory            mapping(bytes32 =>      Historical aggregates
                          uint256[])              per feed (feeds the
                                                  Deviation factor and
                                                  the research series).

  correlationWarnings     mapping(bytes32 =>      Consecutive
                          uint256)                cross-source deviation
                                                  counters (Section
                                                  17.9).

  lastKnownPrice          mapping(bytes32 =>      Last known good price
                          uint256)                for degraded-mode
                                                  fallback (Section
                                                  17.7).

  lastKnownTimestamp      mapping(bytes32 =>      Timestamp of the last
                          uint256)                known good price.

  oracleHealthy           bool                    Global health flag
                                                  maintained by the
                                                  health sweep.

  lastOracleCheck         uint256                 Timestamp of the last
                                                  health sweep.

  goldConfidence,         uint256, enum           The confidence score
  oracleState (v2.0)                              and aggregate state
                                                  machine (Sections
                                                  17.6–17.7).

  sourceStates, rounds    mappings                Per-source states and
  (v2.0)                                          last-round records —
                                                  the confidence inputs
                                                  (Section 17.6).
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

The event set is equally small and machine-parseable [BP §9.10.2],
because the transparency layer of Chapter 24 consumes these events
directly for dashboards and alerting:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Event                   Fields                  Emitted When
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  PriceUpdated            feedId, price,          Every published
                          timestamp, sourceCount  aggregate (Section
                                                  17.5).

  OracleWarning           feedId, reason,         Staleness, deviation
                          timestamp               breaker, quorum loss or
                                                  halted feed.

  OracleRestored          feedId, reason,         A feed or the
                          timestamp               confidence path returns
                                                  to NORMAL.

  CorrelationWarning      feedId, deviation,      Pair deviation > 1.5%
                          timestamp               for 3 consecutive
                                                  updates (Section 17.9).

  OracleDegraded (v2.0)   confidence, timestamp   Confidence_G falls
                                                  below C_min (Section
                                                  17.6).
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

The daily publication (Section 24.3) cites this event stream as the
evidence base for OracleConfidence_t, so the on-chain log and the
published state vector can never disagree without the discrepancy itself
being visible. Storage growth is bounded by design: priceHistory is
pruned on a governed retention window, and all mappings key on the fixed
feed identifier set rather than on user addresses, so state grows
linearly in time and feed count only.

17.12 Smart Contract Implementation — Oracle Aggregator

Listing 9 carries the BP §9.4 aggregation contract substantially
complete and cleaned: getPrice() (fetch, validate, quorum, deviation
breaker, aggregate), validatePrice() and validatePricePyth() (per-feed
checks), and getMedian() with its sort helper. The v2.0 additions are
the gold special handling of Section 17.4 (getGoldPrice with the
conservative minimum and the GOLD_REF/PAXG split), the Confidence_G
computation hook of Section 17.6 (updateGoldConfidence with the five
factors and the C_min state transition), the per-source and aggregate
state machines of Section 17.7, and the correlation detection of Section
17.9. The provider adapters are interface shims: production adapters
handle provider-specific decimal scaling, revert paths and multi-round
reads per the provider SDKs, without altering the aggregation logic.

Listing 9 — Multi-source oracle aggregator: getPrice, validatePrice,
getMedian (adapts BP §9.4)

  // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT

  pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

  /// @title OracleAggregator — three-source consensus with confidence
  scoring.

  /// @dev Adapts BP §9.4 (getPrice / validatePrice / getMedian); adds
  the gold

  /// reference-unit handling of §17.4, the confidence mathematics of
  §17.6

  /// and the state machine of §17.7. Provider adapters are interface
  shims.

  contract OracleAggregator {

  // ============================================================

  // 1. FEED CONFIGURATION (§17.10)

  // ============================================================

  struct OracleFeed {

  address chainlink; // Chainlink aggregator (push)

  bytes32 pyth; // Pyth feed id (pull)

  address chronicle; // Chronicle read address (verifiable)

  uint256 stalenessThreshold; // seconds (§17.3.1)

  uint256 maxConfidenceWidth; // Pyth interval cap, 1e18 scale (§17.3.2)

  uint256 minPrice; // plausible-range floor, 1e18 (§17.5)

  uint256 maxPrice; // plausible-range ceiling, 1e18

  }

  mapping(bytes32 => OracleFeed) public oracleFeeds;

  // ============================================================

  // 2. STATE (§17.11)

  // ============================================================

  mapping(bytes32 => uint256[]) public priceHistory; // aggregate
  history

  mapping(bytes32 => uint256) public correlationWarnings; // §17.9

  mapping(bytes32 => uint256) public lastKnownPrice; // §17.7 fallback

  mapping(bytes32 => uint256) public lastKnownTimestamp;

  bool public oracleHealthy = true;

  uint256 public lastOracleCheck;

  // Per-source states (MS §56 / §17.7)

  enum SourceState { VALID, DEGRADED, STALE, OUTLIER, HALTED }

  mapping(bytes32 => uint8[3]) public sourceStates; // 0=CL 1=Pyth
  2=Chronicle

  // Last aggregation round (confidence inputs, §17.6)

  struct RoundState {

  uint256[3] prices; // raw provider prices of the round

  uint256[3] timestamps; // provider timestamps

  uint256 median; // median of the valid prices

  uint8 validCount; // feeds surviving validation

  uint256 updatedAt;

  }

  mapping(bytes32 => RoundState) internal rounds;

  // Aggregate oracle state (§17.7)

  enum OracleState { NORMAL, DEGRADED, FAILED }

  OracleState public oracleState = OracleState.NORMAL;

  // ============================================================

  // 3. CONSTANTS, ROLES, EVENTS

  // ============================================================

  // Constitutional (Invariant I9): quorum and breakers

  uint256 public constant MIN_FEEDS_REQUIRED = 2; // oracle quorum

  uint256 public constant DEVIATION_BPS = 250; // 2.5% breaker

  uint256 public constant CORRELATION_THRESHOLD = 0.015e18; // 1.5%
  (§17.9)

  // Gold confidence (§17.6 / COO-20) — validation-stage defaults

  uint256 public constant C_MIN = 0.60e18;

  uint256 public goldConfidence; // 1e18 scale

  uint256 public liquidityInput = 0.90e18; // keeper-posted (§17.6)

  address public riskCouncil; // 4/7 Multi-Sig (feed configuration)

  address public keeper; // approved poster of liquidity inputs

  address public pythContract; // Pyth oracle contract (pull model)

  event PriceUpdated(bytes32 indexed feedId, uint256 price, uint256
  timestamp, uint8 sourceCount);

  event OracleWarning(bytes32 indexed feedId, string reason, uint256
  timestamp);

  event OracleRestored(bytes32 indexed feedId, string reason, uint256
  timestamp);

  event CorrelationWarning(bytes32 indexed feedId, uint256 deviation,
  uint256 timestamp);

  event OracleDegraded(uint256 confidence, uint256 timestamp); // §17.6

  constructor(address _riskCouncil, address _keeper, address _pyth) {

  riskCouncil = _riskCouncil;

  keeper = _keeper;

  pythContract = _pyth;

  }

  /// @notice Register or replace a feed configuration (§17.10).

  function setFeed(bytes32 feedId, OracleFeed calldata f) external {

  require(msg.sender == riskCouncil, "Only Risk Council");

  oracleFeeds[feedId] = f;

  }

  /// @notice Keeper-posted liquidity quality input for Confidence_G
  (§17.6).

  function setLiquidityInput(uint256 v) external {

  require(msg.sender == keeper, "Only keeper");

  require(v <= 1e18, "Out of range");

  liquidityInput = v;

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 4. PRICE AGGREGATION (adapts BP §9.4.1)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Returns the aggregated price for a feed, with all validation
  checks.

  /// @param feedId Asset identifier, e.g. "EUR/USD", "PAXG",
  "GOLD_REF".

  /// @return price The validated, aggregated price (18 decimals).

  function getPrice(bytes32 feedId) public returns (uint256) {

  // 1. Fetch prices from all three sources

  (uint256 priceCL, uint256 tsCL) = getChainlinkPrice(feedId);

  (uint256 pricePyth, uint256 tsPyth, uint256 confPyth) =
  getPythPrice(feedId);

  (uint256 priceChronicle, uint256 tsChronicle) =
  getChroniclePrice(feedId);

  // 2. Validate each feed (§17.5) and record per-source states

  bool validCL = validatePrice(priceCL, tsCL, feedId);

  bool validPyth = validatePricePyth(pricePyth, tsPyth, confPyth,
  feedId);

  bool validChronicle = validatePrice(priceChronicle, tsChronicle,
  feedId);

  sourceStates[feedId][0] = validCL ? uint8(SourceState.VALID) :
  uint8(SourceState.STALE);

  sourceStates[feedId][1] = validPyth ? uint8(SourceState.VALID) :
  uint8(SourceState.STALE);

  sourceStates[feedId][2] = validChronicle ? uint8(SourceState.VALID) :
  uint8(SourceState.STALE);

  // 3. Count valid feeds — constitutional quorum (Invariant I9)

  uint256 validCount = (validCL ? 1 : 0) + (validPyth ? 1 : 0) +
  (validChronicle ? 1 : 0);

  require(validCount >= MIN_FEEDS_REQUIRED, "Oracle: Insufficient valid
  feeds");

  // 4. Deviation circuit breaker (§17.3.3): discard > 2.5% from median

  uint256[3] memory candidates = [priceCL, pricePyth, priceChronicle];

  bool[3] memory valids = [validCL, validPyth, validChronicle];

  uint256 median = getMedianOf(candidates, valids);

  uint256 maxDeviation = (median * DEVIATION_BPS) / 10000; // 2.5%

  uint256 sum = 0;

  uint256 count = 0;

  for (uint256 i = 0; i < 3; i++) {

  if (!valids[i]) continue;

  uint256 diff = candidates[i] > median ? candidates[i] - median :
  median - candidates[i];

  if (diff <= maxDeviation) {

  sum += candidates[i];

  count++;

  } else {

  sourceStates[feedId][i] = uint8(SourceState.OUTLIER);

  emit OracleWarning(feedId, "Deviation breaker", block.timestamp);

  }

  }

  require(count >= MIN_FEEDS_REQUIRED, "Oracle: Too many outliers");

  // 5. Cache the round (confidence inputs, §17.6) and last-known-good

  RoundState memory r;

  r.prices = candidates;

  r.timestamps = [tsCL, tsPyth, tsChronicle];

  r.median = median;

  r.validCount = uint8(count);

  r.updatedAt = block.timestamp;

  rounds[feedId] = r;

  // 6. Publish the aggregate: filtered mean of surviving prices

  uint256 aggregated = sum / count;

  lastKnownPrice[feedId] = aggregated;

  lastKnownTimestamp[feedId] = block.timestamp;

  priceHistory[feedId].push(aggregated);

  emit PriceUpdated(feedId, aggregated, block.timestamp, uint8(count));

  // 7. Cross-source correlation detection (§17.9)

  checkCorrelation(feedId, (maxDeviation * 1e18) / median);

  return aggregated;

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 5. VALIDATION AND MEDIAN (adapts BP §9.4.2 / §9.4.3)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Validates a single price feed (BP §9.4.2).

  function validatePrice(uint256 price, uint256 timestamp, bytes32
  feedId)

  internal view returns (bool)

  {

  OracleFeed memory feed = oracleFeeds[feedId];

  // 1. Price must be > 0

  if (price == 0) return false;

  // 2. Timestamp must be fresh (§17.3.1)

  if (block.timestamp - timestamp > feed.stalenessThreshold) return
  false;

  // 3. Price must be within plausible range (sanity check, §17.5)

  if (price < feed.minPrice || price > feed.maxPrice) return false;

  return true;

  }

  /// @dev Pyth validation adds the confidence-interval check (§17.3.2).

  function validatePricePyth(uint256 price, uint256 timestamp, uint256
  confidence, bytes32 feedId)

  internal view returns (bool)

  {

  if (!validatePrice(price, timestamp, feedId)) return false;

  // Confidence width (same scale as price) within the per-asset cap

  if (price > 0 && (confidence * 1e18) / price >
  oracleFeeds[feedId].maxConfidenceWidth) {

  return false;

  }

  return true;

  }

  /// @dev Returns the median of an array of prices (BP §9.4.3).

  function getMedian(uint256[] memory prices) internal pure returns
  (uint256) {

  require(prices.length > 0, "Empty array");

  uint256[] memory sorted = sort(prices);

  return sorted[sorted.length / 2];

  }

  /// @dev Median of the valid candidates (fixed-length round form).

  function getMedianOf(uint256[3] memory c, bool[3] memory v)

  internal pure returns (uint256)

  {

  uint256 n = 0;

  for (uint256 i = 0; i < 3; i++) if (v[i]) n++;

  require(n > 0, "No valid prices");

  uint256[] memory arr = new uint256[](n);

  uint256 k = 0;

  for (uint256 i = 0; i < 3; i++) if (v[i]) arr[k++] = c[i];

  return getMedian(arr);

  }

  /// @dev Insertion sort; round arrays hold at most three elements.

  function sort(uint256[] memory arr) internal pure returns (uint256[]
  memory) {

  for (uint256 i = 1; i < arr.length; i++) {

  uint256 key = arr[i];

  uint256 j = i;

  while (j > 0 && arr[j - 1] > key) {

  arr[j] = arr[j - 1];

  j--;

  }

  arr[j] = key;

  }

  return arr;

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 6. GOLD SPECIAL HANDLING (§17.4 / BP §9.8)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Canonical gold price: conservative min of reference and
  executable.

  /// GOLD_REF is the LBMA-basis reference-unit feed; PAXG is the

  /// tokenized-gold market used for execution (Chapter 11).

  function getGoldPrice() public returns (uint256) {

  uint256 refPrice = getPrice("GOLD_REF");

  uint256 execPrice = getPrice("PAXG");

  updateGoldConfidence();

  return (refPrice < execPrice) ? refPrice : execPrice;

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 7. GOLD ORACLE CONFIDENCE (§17.6 / COO-20)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Confidence_G = f(SourceAgreement, Freshness, Liquidity,
  Deviation,

  /// SourceCoverage): bounded geometric aggregate of five factors in

  /// [0, 1e18], so a single collapsed factor collapses the score.

  function updateGoldConfidence() internal {

  bytes32 feedId = "GOLD_REF";

  uint256 agreement = computeSourceAgreement(feedId);

  uint256 freshness = computeFreshness(feedId);

  uint256 liquidity = liquidityInput; // keeper-posted

  uint256 deviation = computeDeviationFactor(feedId);

  uint256 coverage = computeCoverage(feedId);

  goldConfidence = mul18(mul18(mul18(mul18(agreement, freshness),

  liquidity), deviation), coverage);

  // State machine (§17.7): Confidence_G < C_min => OracleDegraded

  if (goldConfidence < C_MIN && oracleState != OracleState.DEGRADED) {

  oracleState = OracleState.DEGRADED;

  emit OracleDegraded(goldConfidence, block.timestamp);

  } else if (goldConfidence >= C_MIN && oracleState ==
  OracleState.DEGRADED) {

  oracleState = OracleState.NORMAL;

  emit OracleRestored("GOLD_REF", "Confidence recovered",
  block.timestamp);

  }

  }

  function mul18(uint256 a, uint256 b) internal pure returns (uint256) {

  return (a * b) / 1e18;

  }

  /// @dev (a) SourceAgreement: 1 - max deviation of valid sources from
  median.

  function computeSourceAgreement(bytes32 feedId) internal view returns
  (uint256) {

  RoundState memory r = rounds[feedId];

  if (r.validCount < 2 || r.median == 0) return 0;

  uint256 spread = 0;

  for (uint256 i = 0; i < 3; i++) {

  if (sourceStates[feedId][i] != uint8(SourceState.VALID)) continue;

  uint256 d = r.prices[i] > r.median ? r.prices[i] - r.median :
  r.median - r.prices[i];

  if (d > spread) spread = d;

  }

  uint256 rel = (spread * 1e18) / r.median;

  return rel >= 1e18 ? 0 : 1e18 - rel;

  }

  /// @dev (b) Freshness: linear decay of round age to the staleness
  bound.

  function computeFreshness(bytes32 feedId) internal view returns
  (uint256) {

  RoundState memory r = rounds[feedId];

  uint256 threshold = oracleFeeds[feedId].stalenessThreshold;

  if (threshold == 0 || r.updatedAt == 0) return 0;

  uint256 age = block.timestamp - r.updatedAt;

  return age >= threshold ? 0 : 1e18 - (age * 1e18) / threshold;

  }

  /// @dev (d) Deviation: latest aggregate move vs the 2.5% breaker
  band.

  function computeDeviationFactor(bytes32 feedId) internal view returns
  (uint256) {

  uint256[] memory h = priceHistory[feedId];

  if (h.length < 2) return 1e18;

  uint256 last = h[h.length - 2];

  uint256 cur = h[h.length - 1];

  if (last == 0) return 1e18;

  uint256 d = cur > last ? cur - last : last - cur;

  uint256 band = (last * DEVIATION_BPS) / 10000;

  if (d >= band) return 0;

  return 1e18 - (d * 1e18) / band;

  }

  /// @dev (e) SourceCoverage: valid feeds / configured feeds. Family

  /// diversity is enforced separately by the independence check
  §17.3.4.

  function computeCoverage(bytes32 feedId) internal view returns
  (uint256) {

  return (uint256(rounds[feedId].validCount) * 1e18) / 3;

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 8. STALENESS, CORRELATION AND HEALTH (§17.8, §17.9, §17.7)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Feed liveness: 2x staleness = dead (BP §9.6 / §17.8).

  function isFeedAlive(bytes32 feedId) public view returns (bool) {

  (, uint256 timestamp) = getChainlinkPrice(feedId);

  return block.timestamp - timestamp <=
  oracleFeeds[feedId].stalenessThreshold * 2;

  }

  /// @dev Cross-source correlation detection (BP §9.9 / §17.9).

  function checkCorrelation(bytes32 feedId, uint256 relDeviation)
  internal {

  if (relDeviation > CORRELATION_THRESHOLD) {

  correlationWarnings[feedId]++;

  if (correlationWarnings[feedId] >= 3) {

  emit CorrelationWarning(feedId, relDeviation, block.timestamp);

  // Circuit breaker: rebalancing pauses until the correlation

  // resolves (risk states, Chapter 21; MARP gates, Chapter 11).

  }

  } else {

  correlationWarnings[feedId] = 0;

  }

  }

  /// @notice Health sweep: any keeper may call; sets FAILED on quorum
  loss.

  function healthCheck(bytes32[] calldata feedIds) external {

  for (uint256 k = 0; k < feedIds.length; k++) {

  if (rounds[feedIds[k]].validCount < MIN_FEEDS_REQUIRED) {

  oracleState = OracleState.FAILED;

  oracleHealthy = false;

  emit OracleWarning(feedIds[k], "Quorum lost", block.timestamp);

  }

  }

  lastOracleCheck = block.timestamp;

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 9. PROVIDER ADAPTERS (interface shims; §17.2)

  // ============================================================

  function getChainlinkPrice(bytes32 feedId)

  internal view returns (uint256 price, uint256 timestamp)

  {

  (, int256 answer,, uint256 ts,) =

  AggregatorV3Interface(oracleFeeds[feedId].chainlink).latestRoundData();

  price = uint256(answer); // 8 decimals; production adapter scales to
  1e18

  timestamp = ts;

  }

  function getPythPrice(bytes32 feedId)

  internal view returns (uint256 price, uint256 timestamp, uint256
  confidence)

  {

  IPyth.Price memory p =
  IPyth(pythContract).getPrice(oracleFeeds[feedId].pyth);

  price = scaleTo18(uint256(uint64(p.price)), p.expo);

  timestamp = p.publishTime;

  confidence = scaleTo18(uint256(uint64(p.conf)), p.expo);

  }

  function getChroniclePrice(bytes32 feedId)

  internal view returns (uint256 price, uint256 timestamp)

  {

  (uint256 val, uint256 age) =
  IChronicle(oracleFeeds[feedId].chronicle).readWithAge();

  price = val; // 18 decimals

  timestamp = block.timestamp - age; // reconstruct publish time

  }

  /// @dev Decimal normalisation for exponent-carrying pull feeds.

  function scaleTo18(uint256 v, int32 expo) internal pure returns
  (uint256) {

  if (expo >= 0) return v * (10 ** uint256(uint32(expo)));

  return v / (10 ** uint256(uint32(-expo)));

  }

  }

  // ---- Provider adapter interfaces (production adapters handle
  scaling) ----

  interface AggregatorV3Interface {

  function latestRoundData()

  external view returns (uint80 roundId, int256 answer, uint256
  startedAt, uint256 updatedAt, uint80 answeredInRound);

  }

  interface IPyth {

  struct Price { int64 price; uint64 conf; int32 expo; uint64
  publishTime; }

  function getPrice(bytes32 id) external view returns (Price memory);

  }

  interface IChronicle {

  function readWithAge() external view returns (uint256 val, uint256
  age);

  }

  Fidelity notes: (i) getPrice, validatePrice, validatePricePyth and
  getMedian follow BP §9.4.1–§9.4.3 with the deviation constant
  expressed in basis points; BP's outlier-filtered mean return is
  preserved, and the median remains the deviation anchor (Section 17.5
  explains the relationship to the §17.2 consensus rule and the §17.4
  robust median). (ii) The gold special handling adapts BP §9.8's min
  rule to the GOLD_REF/PAXG split of Section 17.4. (iii) The confidence
  computation, the state machine, per-source states, round caching and
  the health sweep are v2.0 additions implementing Sections 17.6–17.7
  and COO-20; the geometric aggregate implements the default form of
  Section 17.6 with equal implicit exponents pending validation. (iv) BP
  §9.9's illustrative constant 1.5e18 is re-scaled to 0.015e18 so that
  the 1.5% rule of Section 17.9 is enforced on the 1e18
  relative-deviation scale. (v) C_min = 0.60 and liquidityInput = 0.90
  are validation-stage defaults (Risk-Council parameters within
  constitutional envelopes). (vi) Provider adapters are shims —
  production integrations resolve decimal scaling and revert paths per
  provider SDKs.

18. The Monetary Unit, Daily State Vector and Monitoring

18.1 The MTQ Reference Price and PAR

  This chapter defines the monetary unit: the USD-equivalent value of
  one MTQ Σ at any moment, the total USD-denominated liability the
  reserve must collateralise, and the daily state vector — the single
  published snapshot from which every protocol decision derives [BP §3,
  MS §64]. Section 18.1 is the enforcement point of Invariant I1: PAR =
  1.00 basket-unit is immutable (Section 2.6).

The Reference Price of 1 MTQ Σ at time t is the normalized value of the
adaptive reference index [BP §3.1]:

  P_(MTQ,t) = (I_(t))/(I_(base))

where I_t is the current reference index value (USD-equivalent; Chapter
9) and I_base is the index value at the genesis base date. The v1.2
blueprint wrote this as GFB_t / GFB_base over the fixed-quantity GFB
index; v2.0 substitutes the adaptive chain-linked reference index of
Chapter 9, so the formula's shape is unchanged while the construction
behind I_t is now the adaptive basket. I_base is fixed at the genesis
snapshot (Section 3.4), so P_{MTQ,genesis} = 1.00 exactly.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Index Level             Reference Price         Meaning
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  I_t = 1.12              1 MTQ = $1.12           The basket appreciated
                                                  12% against USD since
                                                  genesis.

  I_t = 0.92              1 MTQ = $0.92           The basket depreciated
                                                  8% against USD since
                                                  genesis.

  I_t = 1.00              1 MTQ = $1.00           The basket sits exactly
                                                  at its genesis value.
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

The constitutional invariant is that the contract does not contain any
function to set P_{MTQ,t} directly [BP §3.1]. The price is entirely
derived from on-chain oracle data via the index formula, which prevents
any manual manipulation of the token price; the only writes into the
price path are oracle aggregate updates, themselves protected by the
validation and quorum machinery of Chapter 17. In degraded oracle states
the price path uses last-known-good aggregates and is gated by the
sanity band of Section 18.4 before consumption.

PAR = 1.00 basket-unit is immutable — Invariant I1, enforced here and
hard-coded with no setter in Listing 1 (Section 2.7). PAR is the
denomination constant: one MTQ Σ is one unit of the reference basket at
genesis. PAR is not a peg and not a price promise — the reference price
moves with the index, not with a fixed 1:1 USD rate, which is the
Not-a-Dollar principle; the denomination is a normalization choice whose
user-facing consequences are specified in Section 3.5. Minting and
redemption are always priced against the reference price or NAV
respectively (Invariants I5 and I6): a user depositing X USDC receives
approximately X·(1 − f_mint)/P_{MTQ,t} tokens, and a user burning Y MTQ
receives approximately Y·P_{MTQ,t}·(1 − f_redeem) in value — fewer
tokens per USD when the index is high, reinforcing the purchasing-power
equivalence claim. The full fee mechanics, guards and edge cases are
specified in Chapter 19 and are not duplicated here.

18.2 The Liability and Solvency Identity

The total liability of the protocol is the total USD value of all
circulating MTQ Σ tokens [BP §3.2]:

L_(t) = S_(circ,t) × P_(MTQ,t)

where S_{circ,t} is the circulating supply — tokens held by users,
excluding the Genesis Reserve Account and protocol-owned locked
balances. The distinction between total and circulating supply is
critical. totalSupply() (ERC-20) includes the Genesis Reserve locked in
the protocol vault; circulatingSupply is used for liability
calculations. The Genesis Reserve is a protocol asset, not a user
liability: it is excluded from S_{circ,t} to prevent the protocol from
collateralising its own locked tokens [BP §3.2].

The solvency identity is the reserve ratio — net asset value over
liability:

RR_(t) = (V_(net,t))/(L_(t))

with V_{net,t} the haircut-adjusted net asset value (Section 14.1;
Invariant I4). The target operating point is RR_t ≥ 1.10 — the reserve
must always be at least 110% collateralised — with a stress floor of
1.05 and the constitutional hard floor of 1.00 (Invariant I2; the full
solvency mechanics, recovery rules and confirmation periods are carried
in Section 14.2). Both sides of the identity use canonical prices: the
numerator values reserve assets with their haircuts, including the
conservative gold minimum of Section 17.4, while the denominator prices
the unit through I_t. The identity is therefore oracle-honest by
construction — degraded oracle confidence lowers both sides consistently
rather than flattering one of them.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Identity                Formula                 Primary Enforcement
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  Reference price         P_{MTQ,t} = I_t /       Section 18.1;
                          I_{base}                getMTQPrice (Listing 1,
                                                  normative)

  Liability               L_t = S_{circ,t} ×      Section 18.2;
                          P_{MTQ,t}               getLiability

  Reserve ratio           RR_t = V_{net,t} / L_t  Section 14.2; Invariant
                                                  I2 (hard floor 1.00)

  Liquidity coverage      LCR_t = V_{liquid,t} /  Chapter 14;
                          D_{stress,t}            Risk-Council minimum
                                                  LCR_min
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

The LCR row completes the solvency picture on the liquidity dimension:
immediately liquid eligible assets must cover near-term stress
obligations at the Risk-Council minimum (Chapter 14). Together the four
identities of the table are the monetary unit's accounting spine — every
risk state, fee decision and emergency action of Chapters 21–22 reads
them, and the daily state vector (Section 18.3) publishes them.

18.3 The Full Daily State Vector

The Modification Specification requires a single, complete daily state
vector: every published state variable in one snapshot [MS §64]:

  X_(t) = [ W_(t), Risk_(t), CVaR_(t), MDD_(t), PPError_(t),
  Liquidity_(t), Coverage_(t), OracleConfidence_(t), CrisisScore_(t),
  Turnover_(t), Cost_(t) ]

The system then decides:

Action_(t) = f(X_(t)) ≠ f(Calendar)

Every automated decision — rebalance triggers, regime shifts, fee state,
crisis mode — is a function of the published state, never of the date.
This is the state-contingent design principle behind Invariant I11
(Section 2.6): daily calculation does not imply daily trading, and
acting on the vector requires an economically justified trigger under
the MARP rules of Chapter 10. The vector's fields, each published with
its own lineage, are:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Field                               Content and Meaning
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  W_t                                 The live smoothed weight vector
                                      (Section 8.4) with its methodology
                                      and data version hashes — the
                                      basket definition as published.
                                      Weights, not quantities, are the
                                      live state after genesis (Section
                                      3.4).

  Risk_t                              Blended multi-horizon volatilities
                                      per component and the shrunk
                                      correlation state (Sections 5.1,
                                      6.1–6.2). This is the risk lens
                                      through which every candidate model
                                      and the ensemble read the market.

  CVaR_t                              The 95% conditional value-at-risk
                                      of the current basket, with the 99%
                                      level monitored for severe
                                      scenarios (Section 6.6). Tail
                                      position, not average loss, is what
                                      the architecture protects.

  MDD_t                               Current drawdown from the running
                                      peak and the maximum drawdown
                                      statistic (Section 6.7).
                                      Purchasing-power protection is
                                      experienced as drawdown, so the
                                      vector tracks it explicitly.

  PPError_t                           Purchasing-power tracking error
                                      against the Global Purchasing Power
                                      index (Section 6.8) — the
                                      distinctive stability objective of
                                      the unit.

  Liquidity_t                         Per-component liquidity measures —
                                      spreads, depth, settlement
                                      availability — feeding the
                                      liquidity penalty of Section 7.4
                                      and the MARP execution gates of
                                      Chapter 11.

  Coverage_t                          Liquidity coverage of near-term
                                      obligations: the LCR identity and
                                      its components (Chapter 14).
                                      Coverage, not solvency alone,
                                      determines whether redemptions can
                                      be met under stress.

  OracleConfidence_t                  Per-feed oracle states and the gold
                                      confidence score Confidence_G
                                      (Section 17.6). Every price the
                                      vector rests on carries its own
                                      trust measurement.

  CrisisScore_t                       The crisis-risk score aggregating
                                      stress evidence (Section 5.5),
                                      which scales tail-risk weighting,
                                      widens no-trade thresholds and
                                      tightens execution gates.

  Turnover_t                          Realized and pending weight
                                      turnover since the previous
                                      publication (Section 7.3) — the
                                      movement the turnover penalty
                                      priced and MARP executed.

  Cost_t                              Realized and estimated execution
                                      costs from the MARP layer (Chapter
                                      11): spread, slippage, impact and
                                      fees attributable to the period's
                                      rebalancing.
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Beyond the MS §64 list, the daily publication of Section 24.3 extends
X_t with the normative anchors and governance state: the index level
I_t, the reference price P_{MTQ,t}, the liability L_t, NAV and RR_t, the
LCR, the regime probability vector (Section 5.4), the risk state
(Chapter 21), per-feed oracle states (Section 17.7), the current fee
state, and the methodology and data version hashes. The vector is
hash-committed on-chain by Listing 10 so that history is tamper-evident;
the full publication format, cadence and retention rules are specified
in Section 24.3.

The state vector is also the monitoring unit. Dashboards, alerting and
the research program's out-of-sample evaluation (Chapter 23) all consume
X_t snapshots, and because every element is published with lineage —
data versions, oracle confidences, methodology hashes — any anomaly in a
downstream decision is traceable to the specific inputs that caused it.
An auditor asking why the basket moved on a given day receives the
vector, the trigger evaluation against it, and the hashes under which
both can be recomputed.

18.4 Price Sanity Checks, Circuit Breakers and Event Emissions

To protect against oracle manipulation or extreme market dislocation,
the monetary-unit module enforces a price band on the reference price
[BP §3.5]:

0.50 ≤ P_(MTQ,t) ≤ 2.00

-   If P_{MTQ,t} falls outside this range, PAUSE_MINT and
    PAUSE_REBALANCE are automatically triggered.

-   Redemption remains allowed — users can exit at the prevailing price.

-   Governance (4-of-7 Multi-Sig) must manually resume operations after
    investigation.

The band is a last line of defense, not a trading rule. It is roughly
four times wider than the worst plausible annual move of a
major-currency-plus-gold basket, so a breach indicates feed corruption
or genuine dislocation requiring investigation, not ordinary volatility.
The asymmetry of the response is deliberate: minting (expansion of the
liability) halts immediately, while redemption (contraction) stays open,
so the failure mode cannot trap users — the same exit-first principle
that governs the degraded oracle states of Section 17.7. The
implementation guard, getMTQPriceWithGuard(), reverts any consuming call
whose price lies outside the band and is carried in Listing 10.

The module emits audit events [BP §3.6]. PriceUpdated fires whenever the
reference price changes by more than 0.5% since the last logged price —
a threshold chosen to avoid log spam while preserving a complete audit
trail of every economically meaningful move:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Event                   Fields                  Trigger
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  PriceUpdated            oldPrice, newPrice,     Reference price moves
                          timestamp               more than 0.5% since
                                                  the last logged price
                                                  (log-spam guard).

  PriceSanityBreach       price, tooLow,          P_{MTQ,t} leaves the
                          timestamp               0.50–2.00 band; minting
                                                  and rebalancing pause
                                                  automatically.

  OperationsResumed       by, timestamp           Risk Council (4/7)
                                                  manually resumes after
                                                  investigation.

  SupplyUpdated           total, circulating,     The circulating base
                          timestamp               changes (mint,
                                                  redemption, reserve
                                                  unlock).

  DailyStatePublished     epochDay, stateHash,    The daily state-vector
                          timestamp               digest is committed
                                                  (Section 18.3).
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

The sanity band composes with the oracle layer's own breakers. The
deviation breaker of Section 17.3.3 discards a bad feed before it
reaches the index; the correlation breaker of Section 17.9 pauses
rebalancing when feeds dispute each other; the confidence gate of
Section 17.6 blocks publication when the gold price is untrustworthy;
and this section's band catches whatever escapes all of them — the
scenario in which every feed is manipulated consistently, or the index
construction itself fails. In that final case the band halts expansion,
keeps exit open, and escalates to the risk states of Chapter 21 for
governance action.

18.5 Smart Contract Implementation — Monetary Unit and State Vector

BP §3.3 implemented the monetary unit inside the v1.2 core contract. In
the v2.0 consolidation, Listing 1 (Section 2.7) already carries the
normative core getters — getMTQPrice, getLiability, getNAV,
getReserveRatio and getLCR — together with the constitutional constants.
Listing 10 below is the extended monetary-unit module: the storage
constants of BP §3.3.1 (adapted to the v2.0 genesis snapshot), the
getter functions of BP §3.3.2 (re-declared for module self-containment),
the circulating-supply tracking of BP §3.3.3, the price sanity checks of
Section 18.4, the audit events, and the daily state-vector digest of
Section 18.3. Where the listings overlap, Listing 1 remains normative;
the deployment composes Listing 10 around it as the module that owns
supply tracking, sanity checks, events and publication.

Listing 10 — Monetary unit: price, liability, supply and sanity checks
(adapts BP §3.3)

  // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT

  pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

  /// @title MTQMonetaryUnit — extended monetary-unit module (adapts BP
  §3.3).

  /// @dev Listing 1 (§2.7) remains normative for the core getters
  getMTQPrice,

  /// getLiability, getNAV, getReserveRatio and getLCR. This module adds

  /// circulating-supply tracking (BP §3.3.3), the price sanity checks
  of

  /// §18.4, audit events (BP §3.6) and the daily state-vector digest of

  /// §18.3. It is abstract: getIndex and getNAV are provided by the
  index

  /// (Chapter 9) and reserve (Chapter 14) modules of the deployment.

  abstract contract MTQMonetaryUnit {

  // ============================================================

  // 1. STORAGE CONSTANTS (BP §3.3.1; genesis snapshot, §3.4)

  // ============================================================

  // Normalisation denominator for the reference price (set at genesis)

  uint256 public immutable INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR; // I_base, 1e18 scale

  // Genesis quantities q_i — v2.0 seven-component strategic-prior
  snapshot

  uint256 public constant Q_USD = 0.27e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_EUR = 0.20e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_JPY = 0.09e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_GBP = 0.08e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_CNY = 0.05e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_CHF = 0.05e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_GOLD = 0.26e18;

  // Base FX fixings (set at genesis, immutable; 8 decimals, Chainlink
  style)

  uint256 public constant BASE_EUR_USD = 1.0500e8;

  uint256 public constant BASE_GBP_USD = 1.2500e8;

  uint256 public constant BASE_JPY_USD = 0.0067e8;

  uint256 public constant BASE_CNY_USD = 0.1400e8;

  uint256 public constant BASE_CHF_USD = 1.1300e8;

  uint256 public constant BASE_GOLD_USD = 2500.00e8; // USD per
  reference unit

  // Price sanity band (§18.4) — immutable

  uint256 public constant PRICE_SAFETY_LOWER = 0.50e18;

  uint256 public constant PRICE_SAFETY_UPPER = 2.00e18;

  // Genesis Reserve — a protocol asset, not a user liability (§18.2)

  address public immutable GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS;

  // Roles

  address public immutable RISK_COUNCIL; // 4/7 — resume after breach

  address public immutable STATE_PUBLISHER; // §24.3 publication path

  // ============================================================

  // 2. CIRCULATING-SUPPLY TRACKING (BP §3.3.3)

  // ============================================================

  uint256 public totalSupply;

  uint256 public circulatingSupply; // cached exact value

  mapping(address => uint256) public balanceOf;

  event SupplyUpdated(uint256 total, uint256 circulating, uint256
  timestamp);

  /// @dev ERC-20 hook: keeps the cached circulating base exact. Invoked
  by

  /// the mint/redeem engine of Chapter 19 and by reserve unlocks.

  function _afterTokenTransfer(address from, address to, uint256 amount)
  internal {

  if (from == GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS && to != GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS)
  {

  circulatingSupply += amount; // reserve -> market

  } else if (from != GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS && to ==
  GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS) {

  circulatingSupply -= amount; // market -> reserve

  }

  emit SupplyUpdated(totalSupply, circulatingSupply, block.timestamp);

  }

  /// @dev Total supply minus the Genesis Reserve balance (BP §3.3.3).

  function getCirculatingSupply() public view returns (uint256) {

  return totalSupply - balanceOf[GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS];

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 3. REFERENCE PRICE AND LIABILITY (BP §3.3.2)

  // ============================================================

  // Re-declared for module self-containment; Listing 1 (§2.7) is
  normative.

  function getIndex() internal view virtual returns (uint256); // I_t,
  Ch. 9

  function getNAV() internal view virtual returns (uint256); // Ch. 14

  /// @dev Reference price: 1.12e18 means 1 MTQ = 1.12 USD (BP §3.3.2).

  function getMTQPrice() public view returns (uint256) {

  return (getIndex() * 1e18) / INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR;

  }

  /// @dev Total USD liability: circulating supply x reference price.

  function getLiability() public view returns (uint256) {

  return (getCirculatingSupply() * getMTQPrice()) / 1e18;

  }

  /// @dev Reserve ratio RR_t = V_net,t / L_t (§18.2; haircuts via Ch.
  14).

  function getReserveRatio() public view returns (uint256) {

  return (getNAV() * 1e18) / getLiability();

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 4. PRICE SANITY CHECKS (§18.4 / BP §3.5)

  // ============================================================

  bool public pausedMint;

  bool public pausedRebalance;

  event PriceSanityBreach(uint256 price, bool tooLow, uint256
  timestamp);

  event OperationsResumed(address indexed by, uint256 timestamp);

  /// @dev Guarded price: reverts outside the 0.50-2.00 band (BP §3.5).

  function getMTQPriceWithGuard() public view returns (uint256) {

  uint256 price = getMTQPrice();

  require(price >= PRICE_SAFETY_LOWER && price <= PRICE_SAFETY_UPPER,

  "MTQ: Price out of safety band");

  return price;

  }

  /// @notice Circuit breaker: pause minting and rebalancing on breach.

  /// Redemption stays live — users can exit at the prevailing price.

  function checkPriceSanity() external returns (bool paused) {

  uint256 price = getMTQPrice();

  if (price < PRICE_SAFETY_LOWER || price > PRICE_SAFETY_UPPER) {

  pausedMint = true;

  pausedRebalance = true;

  emit PriceSanityBreach(price, price < PRICE_SAFETY_LOWER,
  block.timestamp);

  return true;

  }

  return false;

  }

  /// @notice Manual resume by the 4/7 Risk Council after investigation.

  function resumeOperations() external {

  require(msg.sender == RISK_COUNCIL, "Only Risk Council");

  pausedMint = false;

  pausedRebalance = false;

  emit OperationsResumed(msg.sender, block.timestamp);

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 5. EVENT EMISSION (§18.4 / BP §3.6)

  // ============================================================

  event PriceUpdated(uint256 indexed oldPrice, uint256 indexed newPrice,
  uint256 timestamp);

  uint256 private _lastLoggedPrice;

  /// @dev Emit PriceUpdated on moves larger than 0.5% (log-spam guard).

  function _updatePrice() internal {

  uint256 newPrice = getMTQPrice();

  if (_lastLoggedPrice == 0) {

  _lastLoggedPrice = newPrice;

  return;

  }

  uint256 diff = newPrice > _lastLoggedPrice

  ? newPrice - _lastLoggedPrice

  : _lastLoggedPrice - newPrice;

  if (diff > _lastLoggedPrice / 200) { // 0.5% change

  emit PriceUpdated(_lastLoggedPrice, newPrice, block.timestamp);

  _lastLoggedPrice = newPrice;

  }

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 6. DAILY STATE-VECTOR DIGEST (§18.3 / §24.3)

  // ============================================================

  struct DailyState {

  uint256[7] weights; // W_t (smoothed, live)

  uint256 referencePrice; // P_MTQ,t

  uint256 liability; // L_t

  uint256 reserveRatio; // RR_t

  uint256 oracleConfidence; // Confidence_G digest (§17.6)

  uint256 crisisScore; // §5.5

  bytes32 methodologyVersion;

  bytes32 dataVersion;

  uint256 publishedAt;

  }

  mapping(uint256 => DailyState) public dailyStates; // key: epoch day

  DailyState public lastDailyState;

  event DailyStatePublished(uint256 indexed epochDay, bytes32 stateHash,
  uint256 timestamp);

  /// @notice Publish the daily state vector — the §24.3 publication
  unit.

  /// @dev X_t is assembled off-chain from published components; the
  module

  /// verifies the normative anchors before committing the digest.

  function publishDailyState(DailyState calldata s) external {

  require(msg.sender == STATE_PUBLISHER, "Only state publisher");

  require(s.referencePrice == getMTQPrice(), "Price mismatch");

  require(s.liability == getLiability(), "Liability mismatch");

  require(s.reserveRatio == getReserveRatio(), "RR mismatch");

  uint256 epochDay = block.timestamp / 1 days;

  dailyStates[epochDay] = s;

  lastDailyState = s;

  emit DailyStatePublished(epochDay, keccak256(abi.encode(s)),
  block.timestamp);

  _updatePrice();

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 7. INITIALIZATION

  // ============================================================

  constructor(

  uint256 indexBaseDenominator,

  address genesisReserveAddress,

  address riskCouncil,

  address statePublisher

  ) {

  INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR = indexBaseDenominator; // I_base from §3.4

  GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS = genesisReserveAddress;

  RISK_COUNCIL = riskCouncil;

  STATE_PUBLISHER = statePublisher;

  }

  }

  Fidelity notes: (i) BP §3.3.1's GFB_BASE_DENOMINATOR is renamed
  INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR to match the adaptive reference index of
  Chapter 9, set at genesis from the same snapshot (the BP §3.7
  developer-constants table carries over: INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR
  computed at genesis, PRICE_SAFETY_LOWER 0.50e18 and PRICE_SAFETY_UPPER
  2.00e18 immutable, GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS fixed at deployment). (ii)
  The v1.2 genesis quantities (Q_USD = 0.3890e18, Q_EUR = 0.2780e18,
  Q_GBP = 0.1669e18, Q_JPY = 0.1111e18, Q_CNY = 0.0550e18 — the
  five-currency GFB) are superseded by the v2.0 seven-component
  strategic-prior snapshot of Section 3.4, which adds CHF and GOLD and
  matches Listing 1; the constants block above documents the v2.0
  snapshot. (iii) BP's getters are re-declared for self-containment, but
  Listing 1 (Section 2.7) remains the single normative source for the
  deployment — the LCR getter is deliberately not duplicated here and
  lives in Listing 1. (iv) The mint/burn hooks are invoked by the
  Chapter 19 engine, whose fee formulas implement the pricing sketched
  in Section 18.1; _afterTokenTransfer is the only place the circulating
  base may change. (v) The state-vector digest implements Section 18.3's
  tamper-evident history: the contract verifies the normative anchors
  (price, liability, RR) and stores the full record keyed by epoch day;
  the remaining X_t fields (CVaR_t, MDD_t, PPError_t, Liquidity_t,
  Coverage_t, CrisisScore_t, Turnover_t, Cost_t) ride inside the hashed
  struct and are specified by the publication format of Section 24.3.

19. Minting and Redemption

19.1 Purpose and Pricing Doctrine

  The Minting and Redemption module is the user-facing entry and exit
  layer of the protocol. It allows users to mint MTQ Σ by depositing a
  single stablecoin (USDC, EURC, or another admitted instrument), which
  is atomically swapped into the full reserve basket, and to redeem MTQ
  Σ by burning tokens and receiving the proportional reserve basket — or
  a single asset through the optional router [BP §12.1].

Four core principles govern the module, and each is a direct expression
of the constitutional invariants stated in Section 2.6:

1.  Minting is priced against the adaptive index, never against the USD.
    The mint quote divides the net deposit by the current MTQ reference
    price P_{MTQ,t}. This is invariant I5 (Section 2.6), and it breaks
    any 1:1 USD peg by construction.

2.  Redemption is priced against NAV, never against a fixed proportion.
    The redeem quote is the burn quantity multiplied by the current net
    asset value per token NAV_t. This is invariant I6 (Section 2.6):
    users receive the current value of their claim, not a frozen 1:1
    face amount.

3.  All operations are atomic. The entire minting or redemption process
    is a single blockchain transaction. If any step fails, the entire
    transaction reverts and no partial state change survives.

4.  Slippage is protected. The user sets a minimum acceptable output for
    minting (minMTQOut) or a minimum asset value for redemption
    (minAssetOut), together with a slippage tolerance τ_user, and the
    transaction reverts if execution moves against the quoted price.

The two pricing rules, stated as formulas:

MTQ_(minted) = (X_(net))/(P_(MTQ,t)) (invariant I5)

RedeemValue = Y × NAV_(t) (invariant I6)

where X_{net} is the deposit net of the mint fee, Y is the burn
quantity, P_{MTQ,t} is the MTQ reference price derived from the
chain-linked index (Section 9.8) and NAV_t is the per-token net asset
value from the reserve accounting of Chapter 18. These two rules jointly
produce the purchasing-power-equivalence behavior specified in Section
3.5: a user minting when the index stands at 1.12 receives fewer tokens
per USD than a user minting at 0.92, because each token then represents
more basket value. The user receives the purchasing-power equivalent of
the deposit, not a fixed number of tokens, and redemption returns the
current basket value of the tokens rather than a frozen amount.

Minting and redemption are also the protocol's natural cash flows: MARP
nets deposit inflows and redemption outflows against required weight
corrections before any external trade is considered (Section 10.7 and
Chapter 11). Every price used in a quote is sourced from the canonical
multi-source aggregator of Chapter 17 rather than from any single market
feed; the solvency and liquidity gates of Chapter 13 guard entry, and
the risk-state machinery of Section 14.2 and Chapter 21 can re-price or
gate exits under stress. The full step-by-step flows are specified in
Sections 19.2 and 19.3, the guard mathematics in Section 19.4, and the
reference implementation in Listing 11.

19.2 The Minting Flow

The minting flow converts one admitted stablecoin into newly minted MTQ
Σ backed by the full reserve basket. The specification below carries the
complete user input set and the seven-step on-chain sequence [BP §12.2];
the reference implementation is Listing 11 in Section 19.5, and the
pricing doctrine that governs the quote is invariant I5 of Section 2.6.

19.2.1 User Inputs

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Parameter               Symbol                  Description
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  Deposit Asset           tokenIn                 The stablecoin the user
                                                  deposits (e.g., USDC,
                                                  EURC). Must be admitted
                                                  under the Asset
                                                  Admission Registry
                                                  (Chapter 15).

  Deposit Amount          X                       The amount of tokenIn
                                                  the user wants to
                                                  deposit, in the token's
                                                  native decimals.

  Slippage Tolerance      τ_user                  Maximum acceptable
                                                  deviation from the
                                                  quoted price (default
                                                  0.5%).
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

19.2.2 Step-by-Step Minting Process

Step 1 — Fee deduction. The gross deposit is reduced by the mint fee
before any pricing occurs:

X_(net) = X × (1 - F_(mint))

where F_{mint} = 0.001 (0.10%). The fee accrues to the Operational
Wallet, is not part of the reserve collateral, and is excluded from NAV
(Section 20.4). F_{mint} is the Normal-state default; the risk-state fee
schedule of Chapter 21 governs the effective rate, and the parameter
itself is tunable only through the governance process of Section 19.7.

Step 2 — Fetch the current MTQ reference price. The contract reads the
reference price from the chain-linked index:

P_(MTQ,t) = (I_(t))/(I_(base))

where I_t is the current index value (Section 9.8, Listing 3) and
I_{base} = INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR is the genesis value stored immutably
by the genesis event (Section 20.1.5). The component fixings feeding I_t
are sourced from the canonical aggregator (Chapter 17), so no single
market venue can move the mint quote.

Step 3 — Compute the minted amount.

MTQ_(minted) = (X_(net))/(P_(MTQ,t))

This step is where the not-a-dollar principle is enforced. If P_{MTQ,t}
= 1.12, the user receives X_{net}/1.12 tokens, not X_{net} tokens; the
token count flexes so that the value claim is preserved. A worked
comparison of the two cases appears in Section 19.6.1.

Step 4 — Atomic swap execution. The deposited stablecoin is swapped into
the full reserve basket in a single atomic transaction through the DEX
aggregator. The v1.2 target basket, carried for lineage, was [BP
§12.2.2]:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Currency                Asset                   Percentage of X_net
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  USD                     USDC                    38.90%

  EUR                     EURC                    27.80%

  GBP                     Registry-resolved       16.69%

  JPY                     Registry-resolved       11.11%

  CNY                     Registry-resolved       5.50%

  Gold                    PAXG                    (Remaining, ~0%)
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

with the actual allocation — core fiat plus buffer gold — computed as:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Asset                               Percentage of X_net
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  USDC                                31.12%

  EURC                                22.24%

  GBP₿                                13.35%

  JPY₿                                8.89%

  CNY₿                                4.40%

  PAXG                                20.00%

  Total                               100.00%
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

  v2.0 adaptation: the percentages above are the v1.2
  core-80%-fiat-plus-20%-gold split, computed dynamically from the
  then-fixed target. Under v2.0 the purchased split is the live weight
  vector W_t published by the MASE weight registry (Listing 2, Section
  2.7) — no percentage is hard-coded — while the liquidity buffer logic
  of Chapter 13 still overlays reserve holdings. The tables are retained
  as the canonical illustration of the mechanism: one deposit, one set
  of purchase legs, one transaction. Registry-resolved exposures (GBP₿,
  JPY₿, CNY₿ and, under v2.0, CHF) are implemented by whatever
  instruments the Asset Admission Registry currently admits (Chapter
  15).

Step 5 — Slippage guard. The user sets minMTQOut, the minimum number of
MTQ tokens they are willing to accept. If the actual minted amount would
be less than minMTQOut, the transaction reverts. The default value of
the guard is derived from the quote and the tolerance, as specified in
Section 19.4.1.

Step 6 — Mint tokens. The contract mints MTQ_{minted} to the user's
wallet. The mint increases both the reserve assets (purchased in Step 4)
and the token liability by construction, so the operation is
collateral-neutral at the moment of completion.

Step 7 — Update reserve state. The reserve state is updated to reflect
the new assets and liabilities (Chapter 18), keeping the reserve ratio
and liquidity coverage measurements current for the next operation.

On success the operation emits Minted(user, tokenIn, amountIn,
mtqMinted, mtqPrice), and SlippageWarning is emitted whenever a guard
binds; both events feed the transparency and audit layer (Section 24.4).

19.3 The Redemption Flow

The redemption flow burns MTQ Σ and releases the proportional reserve
basket. It is priced against NAV rather than the index — the exit side
uses what the reserve is actually worth, not what the reference unit
quotes [BP §12.3]. The full input set and the eight-step on-chain
sequence are carried below; the default exit is the full basket, and the
single-asset router is an optional convenience layered on top of it.

19.3.1 User Inputs

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Parameter               Symbol                  Description
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  Burn Amount             Y                       The amount of MTQ Σ the
                                                  user wants to redeem.

  Output Preference       —                       The user can choose to
                                                  receive the full basket
                                                  or a single asset (via
                                                  router).

  Slippage Tolerance      τ_user                  Maximum acceptable
                                                  deviation from the
                                                  oracle price (default
                                                  0.5%).

  Output Asset            outputAsset             The user can specify a
                                                  single asset (e.g.,
                                                  USDC) to receive.
                                                  Default is the full
                                                  basket (address(0)).
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

19.3.2 Step-by-Step Redemption Process

Step 1 — Fetch the current NAV. The per-token net asset value is read
from the reserve accounting:

NAV_(t) = (V_(net,t))/(S_(circ,t))

where V_{net,t} is the net reserve value and S_{circ,t} is the
circulating supply, which excludes the non-circulating Genesis Reserve
holdings (Section 20.1.4; Chapter 18).

Step 2 — Compute the redemption value.

RedeemValue_(USD) = Y × NAV_(t)

The redeem quote is therefore the current value of the burned tokens
expressed in USD-equivalent terms — invariant I6 of Section 2.6.

Step 3 — Fee deduction.

RedeemValue_(net) = RedeemValue_(USD) × (1 - F_(redeem))

where F_{redeem} = 0.0015 (0.15%). This is the Normal-state default: the
risk-state fee schedule of Chapter 21 raises the effective redemption
fee in elevated stress states, which both leans against exit demand when
the reserve most needs protection and compensates the protocol for the
liquidity it must surrender. Fees accrue to the Operational Wallet
(Section 20.4).

Step 4 — Calculate the proportional basket. The protocol releases a
proportional amount of each reserve asset [BP §12.3.2]:

Release_(i) = RedeemValue_(net) × (V_(i))/(V_(net))

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Asset                               Amount to Release
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  USDC                                RedeemValue_net × V_USDC / V_net

  EURC                                RedeemValue_net × V_EURC / V_net

  GBP₿                                RedeemValue_net × V_GBP / V_net

  JPY₿                                RedeemValue_net × V_JPY / V_net

  CNY₿                                RedeemValue_net × V_CNY / V_net

  PAXG                                RedeemValue_net × V_PAXG / V_net
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

  Under v2.0 the proportional release spans all components of the
  reserve, including the CHF instrument of the seven-component snapshot
  (Section 3.4) and any successor instruments admitted under Chapter 15;
  the table above reproduces the v1.2 six-asset release for lineage. The
  release is always proportional to actual reserve composition, never to
  target weights — redemption reflects what is held, not what is
  intended.

Step 5 — Release assets (full basket). The protocol transfers the
proportional assets to the user's wallet. Each transfer is part of the
same transaction as the burn, so the user cannot be left holding burned
tokens against unreleased assets.

Step 6 — Optional single-asset redemption (router). If the user prefers
to receive only USDC (or another single asset), the protocol uses a DEX
router to swap the proportional basket into that asset in a single
atomic transaction. The router guard of Section 19.4.2 applies, and the
user bears the market conversion cost implicit in the route; the
protocol itself never takes FX risk on the conversion.

Step 7 — Burn tokens. The contract burns Y MTQ Σ from the user's wallet,
reducing both the asset base and the liability in the same operation.

Step 8 — Update reserve state. The reserve state is updated to reflect
the reduced assets and liabilities (Chapter 18).

The full-basket branch emits Redeemed(user, mtqBurned, valueOut,
assetAmounts) and the router branch emits RedeemedSingle(user,
mtqBurned, valueOut, outputAsset); both feed the transparency layer
(Section 24.4). The full-basket release doctrine — why the default exit
