is the basket itself rather than a single currency — is stated in
Section 19.6.2.

19.4 Slippage Guard Mathematics

The guards are a critical implementation detail rather than an
afterthought. Quotes are computed before execution, and execution
happens inside the same transaction, but the swap legs inside that
transaction still face price movement between quote and fill — the
aggregator routes through venues whose prices can drift while the
transaction is in flight, and the oracle itself can refresh between the
quote and the fill [BP §12.4]. The guards bound the user's loss to a
pre-declared tolerance, at the cost of a revert.

19.4.1 Minting Slippage Guard

The user sets a minMTQOut. The transaction reverts if:

revert if MTQ_(minted) < minMTQOut

When the user does not supply an explicit value, the contract and
front-ends compute the default from the current quote and the declared
tolerance:

  minMTQOut_(default) = (X_(net))/(P_(MTQ,t)) × (1 - τ_(user))

where τ_user defaults to 0.005 (0.5%), the DEFAULT_SLIPPAGE constant of
Listing 11. A user may always tighten the guard below the default — for
example, to 0.1% around a large deposit — and the guard interacts with
the 5-minute trade deadline carried in Section 19.7: if the transaction
is not mined within the deadline, the aggregator call itself fails and
the whole operation reverts.

19.4.2 Redemption Slippage Guard (Single-Asset Router)

If the user chooses single-asset redemption, the DEX router provides a
quote for converting the released basket into the chosen asset. The
transaction reverts if:

  revert if ExecPrice < OraclePrice × (1 - τ_(user))

where ExecPrice is the effective price actually realized by the router
swap (value out divided by value in) and OraclePrice is the canonical
price for the same conversion implied by the canonical aggregator of
Chapter 17. τ_user again defaults to 0.005 (0.5%). For full-basket
redemption no conversion occurs, so the guard takes the simpler
minAssetOut form of Section 19.3.2: the transaction reverts if the net
redemption value — or the aggregate value of the released basket at
canonical prices — falls below the user's declared minimum. The
SlippageWarning event of Listing 11 is emitted whenever a guard binds,
so that the frequency of binding guards is itself observable in the
transparency layer (Section 24.4).

19.5 Smart Contract Implementation — mint() and redeem()

The reference implementation below carries BP §12.5 substantially
complete and cleaned: the constants of §12.5.1, the mint() function of
§12.5.2, the redeem() function of §12.5.3 (both the full-basket and
router branches), the atomic swap encoding of §12.5.4 as adapted, and
the events of §12.5.5. References are adapted to the v2.0 architecture:
the GFB base denominator is renamed INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR and resolved
through the chain-linked index of Chapter 9 (Listing 3); oracle feeds
are sourced from the canonical aggregator of Chapter 17; asset admission
is delegated to the registry of Chapter 15; and the purchased split
follows the live weight vector of the MASE registry (Listing 2) instead
of hard-coded percentages. The contract is the MTQ Σ token itself, so
_mint, _burn and balanceOf are the standard ERC-20 internals.

Listing 11 — Minting and redemption against the adaptive index (adapts
BP §12.5)

  // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT

  pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

  contract MTQMintRedeem {

  // ============================================================

  // CONSTANTS (BP §12.5.1)

  // ============================================================

  // Minting fee (0.10%)

  uint256 public constant MINT_FEE = 0.001e18; // 0.10%

  // Redemption fee (0.15%)

  uint256 public constant REDEEM_FEE = 0.0015e18; // 0.15%

  // Default slippage tolerance (0.5%)

  uint256 public constant DEFAULT_SLIPPAGE = 0.005e18; // 0.5%

  // Minimum mint amount (to prevent dust)

  uint256 public constant MIN_MINT = 1e18; // 1 MTQ

  // Minimum redeem amount (to prevent dust)

  uint256 public constant MIN_REDEEM = 1e18; // 1 MTQ

  // DEX Aggregator address (1inch or Paraswap; governed 4/7 + 48h
  timelock)

  address public constant AGGREGATOR = 0x...; // set at deployment

  // Trade deadline for aggregator calls (5 minutes, immutable)

  uint256 public constant SWAP_DEADLINE = 300; // seconds

  // ---- External references (v2.0 cross-references) ----

  // getMTQPrice() ........ chain-linked index, §9.8 / Listing 3

  // getNAV(), getCirculatingSupply(), updateReserveState() ... Ch 18

  // isApprovedAsset() .... Asset Admission Registry, Ch 15

  // getLiveWeights() ..... MASE weight registry, Listing 2

  // getProportionalAssets(), transferAssetsToUser(),

  // getInstrumentPath(), encodeSingleAssetSwapData() ... developer

  // helpers (Appendix B). All price inputs originate from the

  // canonical aggregator of Ch 17.

  address public weightRegistry; // MASE weight registry (Listing 2)

  // ============================================================

  // mint() (BP §12.5.2)

  // ============================================================

  /**

  * @dev Mints MTQ Σ by depositing a single stablecoin.

  * @param tokenIn The stablecoin address (e.g., USDC).

  * @param amount The amount of tokenIn to deposit.

  * @param minMTQOut The minimum MTQ the user is willing to accept

  * (slippage guard).

  * @return mtqMinted The amount of MTQ Σ minted.

  */

  function mint(address tokenIn, uint256 amount, uint256 minMTQOut)

  external onlyIfSolvent onlyIfLiquid nonReentrant returns (uint256)

  {

  require(amount >= MIN_MINT, "Amount too small");

  require(isApprovedAsset(tokenIn), "Asset not approved");

  // 1. Transfer tokens from user to contract

  IERC20(tokenIn).transferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), amount);

  // 2. Deduct minting fee (accrues to the Operational Wallet, §20.4)

  uint256 amountNet = amount * (1e18 - MINT_FEE) / 1e18;

  // 3. Get current MTQ price — chain-linked index (§9.8, Listing 3),

  // component feeds sourced from the canonical aggregator (Ch 17)

  uint256 mtqPrice = getMTQPrice();

  // 4. Compute minted amount (invariant I5: priced against the index)

  uint256 mtqMinted = (amountNet * 1e18) / mtqPrice;

  require(mtqMinted >= minMTQOut, "Slippage too high (mint)");

  // 5. Execute atomic swap to buy the full reserve basket

  bytes memory swapData = encodeMintSwapData(tokenIn, amountNet);

  (bool success, ) = AGGREGATOR.call(swapData);

  require(success, "Swap failed");

  // 6. Mint MTQ to user

  _mint(msg.sender, mtqMinted);

  // 7. Update reserve state (assets and liabilities, Ch 18)

  updateReserveState();

  // 8. Emit event

  emit Minted(msg.sender, tokenIn, amount, mtqMinted, mtqPrice);

  return mtqMinted;

  }

  // ============================================================

  // redeem() (BP §12.5.3)

  // ============================================================

  /**

  * @dev Redeems MTQ Σ for the proportional reserve basket or a single
  asset.

  * @param mtqAmount The amount of MTQ to redeem.

  * @param minAssetOut The minimum value of assets the user is willing

  * to accept.

  * @param outputAsset (Optional) The address of a single asset to
  receive.

  * Use address(0) for the full basket.

  * @return assetsReceived The amounts of each asset received.

  */

  function redeem(uint256 mtqAmount, uint256 minAssetOut, address
  outputAsset)

  external nonReentrant returns (uint256[] memory)

  {

  require(mtqAmount >= MIN_REDEEM, "Amount too small");

  require(balanceOf(msg.sender) >= mtqAmount, "Insufficient balance");

  // 1. Current NAV per token (Ch 18; supply excludes the Genesis

  // Reserve, §20.1)

  uint256 nav = getNAV();

  uint256 circSupply = getCirculatingSupply();

  uint256 navPerToken = (nav * 1e18) / circSupply;

  // 2. Redemption value priced against NAV (invariant I6)

  uint256 redeemValueUSD = (mtqAmount * navPerToken) / 1e18;

  // 3. Fee deduction (Normal-state default; risk-state schedule, Ch 21)

  uint256 redeemValueNet = redeemValueUSD * (1e18 - REDEEM_FEE) / 1e18;

  require(redeemValueNet >= minAssetOut, "Slippage too high (redeem)");

  if (outputAsset == address(0)) {

  // 4a. Full basket redemption

  uint256[] memory assetAmounts = getProportionalAssets(redeemValueNet);

  _burn(msg.sender, mtqAmount);

  transferAssetsToUser(assetAmounts);

  updateReserveState();

  emit Redeemed(msg.sender, mtqAmount, redeemValueNet, assetAmounts);

  return assetAmounts;

  } else {

  // 4b. Single-asset redemption (via router). The router quote

  // includes slippage; the user is responsible for setting

  // minAssetOut (guard math in §19.4.2).

  bytes memory swapData =

  encodeSingleAssetSwapData(redeemValueNet, outputAsset);

  (bool success, ) = AGGREGATOR.call(swapData);

  require(success, "Swap failed");

  _burn(msg.sender, mtqAmount);

  updateReserveState();

  emit RedeemedSingle(msg.sender, mtqAmount, redeemValueNet,
  outputAsset);

  // Return a single-element array for consistency

  uint256[] memory result = new uint256[](1);

  result[0] = IERC20(outputAsset).balanceOf(msg.sender); // approximate

  return result;

  }

  }

  // ============================================================

  // ATOMIC SWAP ENCODING (BP §12.5.4, adapted)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Encodes the swap data for minting the full reserve basket.

  /// v2.0: the split follows the live weight vector W_t published by

  /// the MASE weight registry (Listing 2). The v1.2 hard-coded split

  /// (USDC 31.12%, EURC 22.24%, GBP 13.35%, JPY 8.89%, CNY 4.40%,

  /// PAXG 20.00%) is superseded.

  function encodeMintSwapData(address tokenIn, uint256 amount)

  internal returns (bytes memory)

  {

  // Live weights (sum = 1e18) with methodology and data version hashes

  (uint256[7] memory weights, bytes32 methodologyVersion, bytes32
  dataVersion) =

  IWeightRegistry(weightRegistry).getLiveWeights();

  // One instrument leg per component, resolved through the Asset

  // Admission Registry (Ch 15); the deposit asset is the entry leg.

  address[] memory path = getInstrumentPath(tokenIn);

  // Split the amount according to the live basket weights

  uint256[] memory amounts = new uint256[](7);

  for (uint256 i = 0; i < 7; i++) {

  amounts[i] = amount * weights[i] / 1e18;

  }

  return abi.encodeWithSelector(

  IAggregator.swap.selector,

  path,

  amounts,

  address(this),

  block.timestamp + SWAP_DEADLINE // 5-minute deadline (immutable)

  );

  }

  // ============================================================

  // EVENTS (BP §12.5.5)

  // ============================================================

  event Minted(

  address indexed user,

  address indexed tokenIn,

  uint256 amountIn,

  uint256 mtqMinted,

  uint256 mtqPrice

  );

  event Redeemed(

  address indexed user,

  uint256 mtqBurned,

  uint256 valueOut,

  uint256[] assetAmounts

  );

  // Emitted by the single-asset branch of redeem() (BP §12.5.3);

  // declared here because the BP §12.5.5 event list omits it.

  event RedeemedSingle(

  address indexed user,

  uint256 mtqBurned,

  uint256 valueOut,

  address outputAsset

  );

  event SlippageWarning(

  string operation,

  uint256 expected,

  uint256 actual,

  uint256 tolerance

  );

  }

  interface IERC20 {

  function transferFrom(address from, address to, uint256 value)
  external returns (bool);

  function transfer(address to, uint256 value) external returns (bool);

  function balanceOf(address account) external view returns (uint256);

  }

  interface IWeightRegistry {

  function getLiveWeights()

  external view returns (uint256[7] memory weights, bytes32
  methodologyVersion, bytes32 dataVersion);

  }

  interface IAggregator {

  function swap(

  address[] calldata path,

  uint256[] calldata amounts,

  address recipient,

  uint256 deadline

  ) external;

  }

  Fidelity note (Listing 11 vs. BP §12.5): carried verbatim (cleaned)
  are the fee, slippage and dust constants, the mint() and redeem()
  pricing sequences, the guard revert conditions, the router-vs-basket
  branch structure, and the Minted / Redeemed / SlippageWarning events.
  Five adaptations are deliberate: (i) GFB base denominator references
  become the chain-linked INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR of Listing 3 (Chapter
  9); (ii) oracle references become the canonical aggregator of Chapter
  17; (iii) the hard-coded v1.2 basket split in the swap encoding is
  replaced by the live registry weights, with the 5-minute deadline
  retained; (iv) the re-entrancy guard required by the BP §12 security
  note is made explicit on both entry points; and (v) RedeemedSingle is
  declared with the §12.5.5 events because redeem() emits it. The helper
  functions getProportionalAssets(), transferAssetsToUser(),
  getInstrumentPath() and encodeSingleAssetSwapData() remain developer
  actions per the Appendix B checklist; the AGGREGATOR address must be
  immutable or governed by a 4/7 Multi-Sig with a 48-hour timelock.

19.6 Critical Notes

Four properties of the module are frequently misunderstood and are
therefore stated explicitly [BP §12.6]. Each restates a rule that is
already enforced by the code of Listing 11; they are collected here
because they define what a user can and cannot expect from the entry and
exit layers.

19.6.1 Minting Does Not Create a 1:1 USD Peg

The formula MTQ_{minted} = X_{net}/P_{MTQ,t} means that the token count
delivered for a given deposit moves inversely with the reference price:

-   If P_{MTQ,t} = 1.12, depositing 1,000 USDC yields 1{,}000/1.12
    \approx 892.86 MTQ.

-   If P_{MTQ,t} = 0.92, depositing 1,000 USDC yields 1{,}000/0.92
    \approx 1{,}086.96 MTQ.

  This is intentional. The user receives the purchasing power equivalent
  of their deposit, not a fixed number of tokens [BP §12.6.1].

The mirror image holds at redemption: because the exit is priced against
NAV_t, a holder never redeems a frozen 1:1 amount either. Both sides
flex around value, which is exactly what a purchasing-power unit
requires (Sections 3.5 and 2.6, invariants I5 and I6).

19.6.2 Redemption Releases the Full Basket

The default redemption gives the user all fiat exposures plus gold, in
proportion to the current reserve composition. This ensures the
redemption faithfully reflects the global purchasing-power claim: the
holder exits with the same diversified exposure the unit represented
while held, not with a single-currency settlement that would silently
re-impose USD (or any other currency) risk at the exit door. The
single-asset router is a user convenience that adds a market conversion
on top of this default; the conversion cost and the router slippage
guard are borne by the requesting user, never by the reserve.

19.6.3 Atomicity Is On-Chain Only

The term atomic refers to on-chain transaction atomicity: the entire
mint or redemption is a single blockchain transaction, and if any step
fails the whole transaction reverts with no partial state change.
External dependencies — the DEX aggregator, the oracle feeds of Chapter
17 — may nevertheless introduce latency or failure modes of their own (a
stale feed, a route that cannot fill within the deadline). These are
handled by the revert logic and the guards of Section 19.4: the failure
mode of every external dependency is a clean revert, never a partially
executed mint or redemption.

19.6.4 Minimum Amounts

The constants MIN_MINT and MIN_REDEEM prevent dust transactions that
would waste gas and clutter the reserve state. They are set to 1 MTQ
(1e18) by default. BP §12.6.4 describes the minimums as
governance-adjustable, while BP §12.7 classifies them as constitutional
(immutable); the listing resolves this tension by declaring them as
compile-time constants, so any change requires a governance-approved
redeployment — the stricter reading is normative, and the discrepancy is
recorded in the decision log (Section 24.4).

19.7 Summary of Developer-Facing Constants

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Parameter         Symbol            Value             Governance
  ----------------- ----------------- ----------------- -----------------
  Mint Fee          F_mint            0.10%             Risk Council
                                                        (4/7)

  Redeem Fee        F_redeem          0.15%             Risk Council
                                                        (4/7)

  Default Slippage  τ_user            0.5%              Risk Council
                                                        (4/7)

  Min Mint Amount   MIN_MINT          1 MTQ             Constitutional
                                                        (immutable)

  Min Redeem Amount MIN_REDEEM        1 MTQ             Constitutional
                                                        (immutable)

  Aggregator        —                 1inch/Paraswap    Multi-Sig (4/7)
  Address                                               

  Trade Deadline    —                 5 minutes         Constitutional
                                                        (immutable)
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

One reconciliation is required across the document: Section 2.9 lists
the fee parameters under DAO vote while BP §12.7 lists them under the
Risk Council (4/7). The definitive mapping is the parameter registry of
Section 22.3, and the risk-state fee schedule of Chapter 21 governs the
effective rates actually charged — the values above are the Normal-state
defaults. The full deployment checklist for the entry/exit layer (helper
functions, aggregator governance, re-entrancy guards, deadline handling)
is consolidated in Appendix B, and the testnet mock aggregator and mock
assets used to exercise mint() and redeem() before production are
specified in Appendix D.

20. Genesis, Accounting and Treasury

20.1 The Genesis Event

  The Genesis Event is the initialization of the protocol: it
  establishes the reserve vault, mints the first MTQ Σ tokens, and sets
  the chain-linked index to its base value of exactly 1.0000. Together
  with the accounting separation and the treasury sweep, it ensures that
  operational funds are kept strictly apart from reserve collateral,
  that excess surplus is moved automatically to a secure multi-signature
  cold treasury, and that all accounting is transparent and auditable
  on-chain [BP §13.1, §13.2].

20.1.1 Purpose of the Genesis Event

The Genesis Event serves three functions [BP §13.2.1]:

1.  Seed the Reserve Vault with the initial assets required to back the
    first MTQ Σ tokens.

2.  Establish the index base at exactly 1.0000, using the base-date FX
    fixings — the immutable genesis snapshot of Section 3.4.

3.  Create the Genesis Reserve — a non-circulating pool of MTQ Σ that
    acts as protocol-owned collateral, excluded from liability
    calculations (Chapter 18).

Because the base denominator is fixed at genesis, every subsequent index
value, mint quote and redemption value is measured against a checkable,
published starting point (Sections 9.6 and 9.8). The genesis
verification event makes the starting point independently auditable, and
the testnet genesis equivalents are listed in Appendix D.

20.1.2 The Genesis Deposit

At deployment, the protocol team deposits 1,100,000 USDC into the
contract. Why 1,100,000? To achieve a 110% reserve ratio against
1,000,000 MTQ Σ. Why 1,000,000 MTQ? That is the Genesis Supply — a
fixed, non-circulating amount that establishes the initial collateral
base [BP §13.2.2]. The mathematics:

Genesis Reserve Value = 1,100,000 USD

Genesis MTQ Minted = 1,000,000

RR_(genesis) = (1,100,000)/(1,000,000 × 1.0000) = 1.10 = 110%

Genesis therefore starts the protocol exactly at the RR target (1.10) of
Section 14.2, with the stress floor (1.05) and the hard floor (1.00)
below it, so that the first market movement is absorbed by a pre-funded
buffer rather than by an immediate governance response. The deposit
amount and the genesis supply are constitutional constants (Section
20.6) and cannot be changed after deployment.

20.1.3 The Atomic Genesis Swap

The deposited USDC is atomically swapped into the full reserve basket
using the same DEX aggregator logic as user minting (Section 19.2, Step
4; Listing 11). The v1.2 genesis basket allocation — core 100% plus
buffer 10% — was [BP §13.2.3]:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Asset             USD Value         Percentage        Quantity (at
                                                        genesis prices)
  ----------------- ----------------- ----------------- -----------------
  USDC              $342,100          31.10%            342,100 USDC

  EURC              $244,200          22.20%            232,571 EURC (at
                                                        1.05)

  GBP₿              $146,850          13.35%            117,480 GBP₿ (at
                                                        1.25)

  JPY₿              $97,790           8.89%             14,595,522 JPY₿
                                                        (at 0.0067)

  CNY₿              $48,400           4.40%             345,714 CNY₿ (at
                                                        0.14)

  PAXG              $220,660          20.06%            88.26 PAXG (at
                                                        $2,500)

  Total             $1,100,000        100.00%           —
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

  The quantities above are illustrative: the actual quantities depend on
  the base-date FX fixings and the DEX execution price at genesis [BP
  §13.2.3]. v2.0 adaptation: the genesis purchase follows the
  strategic-prior snapshot of Section 3.4 (seven components including
  CHF and gold at the prior weights, with the same base fixings),
  executed through the same aggregator path as Listing 11; the table
  reproduces the v1.2 allocation for lineage, and the mechanics — one
  atomic swap, full basket, one transaction — are unchanged. Testnet
  genesis specifics are in Appendix D.

20.1.4 The Genesis Mint

The genesis mint follows four rules [BP §13.2.4]:

1.  The contract mints 1,000,000 MTQ Σ.

2.  These tokens are sent to the Genesis Reserve Account — a dedicated
    4/7 Gnosis Safe with the same signers as the Emergency Council. This
    ensures that no single individual can access the Genesis Reserve.

3.  The Genesis Reserve Account is used only for emergency reserve
    replenishment (never for profit or operations).

4.  The address is mutable by the Constitutional Council (7/7) to allow
    signer rotation; the transferGenesisReserve() function enables this
    (Section 20.5).

The genesis supply is non-circulating: it is excluded from the
circulating supply (Chapter 18), so genesis tokens never count toward
the protocol liability and never dilute the reserve ratio from the
liability side. The replenishment trigger that can put these tokens to
work — and the Cold Treasury surplus path alongside it — is the RR
stress tier of Section 14.2, described operationally in Section 20.4.

20.1.5 The Index Base Denominator

At genesis, the contract computes and stores the base denominator of the
chain-linked index [BP §13.2.5]:

  I_(base) = q_(USD) · 1.00 + q_(EUR) · FX_(EUR/USD,base) + q_(JPY) ·
  FX_(JPY/USD,base) + q_(GBP) · FX_(GBP/USD,base) + q_(CNY) ·
  FX_(CNY/USD,base) + q_(CHF) · FX_(CHF/USD,base) + q_(XAU) ·
  P_(Gold,base)

where the q_i are the genesis quantities of the Section 3.4 snapshot
(USD-equivalent per unit at the base date) and the base fixings are the
hard-coded reference values of Section 3.4 (EUR/USD 1.0500, GBP/USD
1.2500, JPY/USD 0.0067, CNY/USD 0.1400, CHF/USD 1.1300, gold at USD
2,500 per reference unit). The v1.2 formula carried five currency terms;
the v2.0 extension adds CHF and gold so that the denominator matches the
seven-component genesis snapshot exactly. This value is stored as an
immutable constant — INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR (Listing 3, Chapter 9) — and
used in all future index calculations: P_{MTQ,t} = I_t/I_{base} (Section
9.8). The base fixings are used only to normalize the index and are
never updated; anyone can recompute the stored value against them via
verifyIndexBase() and the GenesisVerification event (Section 9.6;
Listing 12).

20.1.6 The Genesis Function

The full contract function is Listing 12 in Section 20.5; the normative
step sequence is [BP §13.2.6]:

1.  Transfer the genesis deposit from the deployer to the contract.

2.  Atomically swap the deposit into the full reserve basket (per the
    Section 3.4 snapshot).

3.  Compute and store the index base denominator.

4.  Emit GenesisVerification with the stored denominator and every base
    fixing.

5.  Mint the genesis supply to the Genesis Reserve Account.

6.  Update the reserve state.

7.  Mark genesis complete (preventing re-initialization) and emit
    GenesisCompleted.

The order matters: the base denominator is computed and verified before
the first token is minted, so the very first MTQ Σ is born with a
checkable price. The onlyDeployer guard and the single-call flag protect
against re-genesis, and the verification step ties the on-chain state to
the off-chain verification function of Section 9.6 — genesis is the one
moment in the protocol's life where the entire monetary definition is
fixed in a single auditable transaction.

20.2 Accounting Separation — The Three Pools

The protocol maintains three separate accounting pools to ensure clarity
and prevent commingling of user collateral and operating funds [BP
§13.3]:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Pool              Purpose           Assets            Who Controls
  ----------------- ----------------- ----------------- -----------------
  1. Reserve Vault  Backs MTQ Σ       USDC, EURC, GBP₿, Smart contract
                    liabilities       JPY₿, CNY₿, PAXG  (immutable)

  2. Operational    Pays for gas,     USDC (fee         Hot wallet
  Wallet            oracles, keepers, revenue)          (keeper bot)
                    audits                              

  3. Cold Treasury  Long-term surplus USDC, PAXG        4/7 Multi-Sig
                    storage                             (hardware
                                                        wallets)
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

  Critical rule: assets in the Operational Wallet and Cold Treasury are
  not counted toward the Reserve Ratio (RR) or collateralization. They
  are explicitly excluded from all liability calculations [BP §13.3].

The circulating liability is measured against the Reserve Vault alone,
which keeps the reported RR conservative: money that the protocol earns
can never silently prop up the reserve ratio. Surplus enters the reserve
through exactly one path — the governed, evented replenishment transfer
of Section 20.4 — and every movement between pools is an on-chain event
(TreasurySwept, ReserveReplenished) captured by the transparency layer
(Section 24.4). Under v2.0 the instrument set of the Reserve Vault
evolves with the Asset Admission Registry (Chapter 15); the pool
purposes and the separation rule are unchanged, because the separation
is what makes the closed-loop accounting of Chapter 18 reconcilable at
all times.

20.3 The Treasury Sweep

The treasury sweep is the automatic transfer of operational surplus out
of the hot path and into the cold treasury. Its purpose is to minimize
hot-wallet risk: the Operational Wallet holds only what day-to-day
operations require, and nothing more [BP §13.4].

20.3.1 The Sweep Threshold

The protocol maintains the Operational Wallet (hot wallet) for paying
day-to-day costs — gas, oracle subscriptions, keeper bot fees. To
minimize risk, any surplus above a defined threshold is automatically
swept to the Cold Treasury:

Sweep Threshold = 10,000 USD

The rule: if the Operational Wallet balance exceeds 10,000 USD in USDC
(or equivalent), the excess is swept to the Cold Treasury. The threshold
is a Risk-Council parameter (Section 20.6) sized to cover a comfortable
operating runway — large enough that sweeps are not triggered by normal
fee noise, small enough that the hot-wallet exposure stays bounded.

20.3.2 The Sweep Function

The sweep is executed by the keeper bot, called daily or after large fee
accruals. The logic is deliberately mechanical — no discretion, no
timing decisions: read the USDC balance of the contract; if the balance
exceeds SWEEP_THRESHOLD, transfer the excess to COLD_TREASURY and emit
TreasurySwept. The full implementation is in Listing 12. Because the
sweep is automatic and evented, the hot-wallet exposure at any moment is
an auditable function of the fee history rather than an operational
habit, and the sweep threshold changes are governance actions visible
on-chain (Section 22.3).

20.3.3 Cold Treasury Security Requirements

The cold treasury is designed so that compromising it requires
compromising seven independent, geographically distributed,
hardware-protected principals at once [BP §13.4.3]:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Requirement                         Description
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  Multi-Signature                     4-of-7 Gnosis Safe (or equivalent).

  Signer Separation                   Signers must be geographically
                                      distributed.

  Hardware Wallet                     All signers must use hardware
                                      wallets (Ledger, Trezor).

  Signer Rotation                     Signers are rotated annually, with
                                      a 48h timelock for changes.

  Withdrawal Delay                    Withdrawals from the Cold Treasury
                                      require a 48h timelock and 4/7
                                      signatures.

  Incident Response                   A pre-approved emergency signer set
                                      exists for critical infrastructure
                                      payments.
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

20.4 Fee Accrual, Surplus and Reserve Replenishment

All fees — minting, redemption and rebalancing — accrue to the
Operational Wallet in USDC (or the deposited asset, converted to USDC)
[BP §13.5]:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Fee Type                Rate                    Destination
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  Mint Fee                0.10%                   Operational Wallet

  Redeem Fee              0.15%                   Operational Wallet

  Rebalancing Fee         0.20%                   Operational Wallet
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Note. Fees are deducted before the mint, redemption or rebalance is
executed. They are not part of the reserve collateral and are excluded
from NAV calculations. The rates above are Normal-state defaults; the
effective schedule is risk-state dependent (Chapter 21), and the
rebalancing fee interacts with the MARP cost-benefit gates (Chapter 11)
as an explicit cost of execution. Each deduction emits FeeAccrued
(Listing 12), so the Operational Wallet's income statement is
reconstructible from events alone (Section 24.4).

20.4.1 Operational Surplus and Reserve Replenishment

The Cold Treasury may be used for emergency reserve replenishment if the
Reserve Ratio drops below 1.05 (Stress Mode, Section 14.2) [BP §13.6].
The flow:

1.  The Reserve Ratio drops below 1.05.

2.  The protocol enters Stress Mode (Section 14.2; risk states of
    Chapter 21).

3.  If rebalancing cannot restore the RR — after MARP's natural
    cash-flow netting (Section 10.7) and partial corrections
    (Chapter 11) are exhausted — the protocol may request a surplus
    transfer from the Cold Treasury to the Reserve Vault.

4.  The transfer requires 4/7 Multi-Sig approval and is fully documented
    on-chain.

The implementation guard is the replenishReserve() function of Listing
12: it requires the 4/7 Multi-Sig and a live RR below 1.05, and it is
not callable by keepers or the DAO. Replenishment is deliberately the
last line of defense in a fixed hierarchy — natural cash flows first,
then buffer adjustments (Chapter 13), then MARP trades (Chapter 11),
then treasury replenishment — so the protocol only spends its cold
surplus after the cheaper mechanisms have failed. The Genesis Reserve
(Section 20.1.4) is the second protocol-owned emergency source, governed
by the same discipline; both moves are multi-signature, timelocked where
applicable, and fully evented.

20.5 Smart Contract Implementation — Genesis and Treasury

Listing 12 carries BP §13.7 substantially complete and cleaned: the
storage variables of §13.7.1 (updated for governance and auditability),
the genesis function of §13.2.6 in its fuller §13.7 form — including the
GenesisVerification emission — the treasury sweep and emergency
replenishment functions, the governed transfer of the Genesis Reserve
address, and the index-base computation and verification helpers. Naming
is adapted: GFB_BASE_DENOMINATOR becomes INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR (Listing
3, Chapter 9), and the base computation includes the CHF and gold terms
of the seven-component genesis snapshot (Section 3.4). The event summary
of §13.7.3 and the storage summary of §13.7.2 follow the listing as
tables.

Listing 12 — Genesis, accounting separation and treasury sweep (adapts
BP §13.7)

  // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT

  pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

  contract MTQGenesisTreasury {

  // ============================================================

  // STORAGE VARIABLES (BP §13.7.1 — UPDATED for governance &
  auditability)

  // ============================================================

  // ---- Genesis Constants (set at deployment, immutable) ----

  // Genesis deposit amount: $1,100,000 (110% RR against 1,000,000 MTQ)

  uint256 public constant GENESIS_DEPOSIT = 1_100_000e18; // 18 decimals

  // Genesis supply: 1,000,000 MTQ (sent to Genesis Reserve)

  uint256 public constant GENESIS_SUPPLY = 1_000_000e18;

  // Genesis Reserve Address (MUTABLE — governed by Constitutional
  Council)

  // Testnet default (Arc/Monad):
  0xE71869C662733642bfBb262B8c6bad8B0fBfA7D0

  address public genesisReserveAddress;

  // Genesis complete flag (prevents re-initialisation)

  bool public genesisComplete;

  // ---- Index Denominator ----

  // Index base denominator (set once at genesis, immutable in effect).

  // v2.0 rename: GFB_BASE_DENOMINATOR -> INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR

  // (chain-linked index, Listing 3 / §9.8)

  uint256 public INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR;

  // ---- Treasury Sweep ----

  // Sweep threshold: $10,000

  uint256 public constant SWEEP_THRESHOLD = 10_000e18;

  // Cold Treasury Address (4/7 Multi-Sig, immutable)

  address public constant COLD_TREASURY = 0x...; // set at deployment

  // Keeper bot address (for automated sweeps)

  address public keeper;

  // ---- Operational Wallet (Hot Wallet) ----

  // The contract itself holds the operational surplus.

  // Use balanceOf(address(this)) for the current operational balance.

  // ---- External references (v2.0 cross-references) ----

  // USDC .................. reserve instrument (Ch 15; Appendix D
  testnet mock)

  // AGGREGATOR ............ DEX aggregator, shared with Listing 11

  // deployer .............. set at deployment

  // updateReserveState(), getReserveRatio() ... Ch 18 / Listing 1

  // encodeGenesisSwapData() ... same aggregator path as Listing 11

  // _mint .................. ERC-20 internals of the MTQ Σ token

  // isMultiSig(), isConstitutionalCouncil() ... governance contracts
  (Ch 22)

  address public USDC;

  address public constant AGGREGATOR = 0x...; // set at deployment

  address public deployer;

  // ---- Base-date fixings and genesis quantities (§3.4 snapshot) ----

  // Genesis quantities q_i per 1 MTQ unit, USD-equivalent at the base
  date

  // (strategic prior: USD 0.27 / EUR 0.20 / JPY 0.09 / GBP 0.08 / CNY
  0.05 /

  // CHF 0.05 / Gold 0.26). Hard-coded reference values, never updated.

  uint256 public constant Q_USD = 0.27e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_EUR = 0.20e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_JPY = 0.09e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_GBP = 0.08e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_CNY = 0.05e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_CHF = 0.05e18;

  uint256 public constant Q_GOLD = 0.26e18;

  // Base-date FX fixings (§3.4; hard-coded, never updated)

  uint256 public constant BASE_EUR_USD = 1.05e18;

  uint256 public constant BASE_GBP_USD = 1.25e18;

  uint256 public constant BASE_JPY_USD = 0.0067e18;

  uint256 public constant BASE_CNY_USD = 0.14e18;

  uint256 public constant BASE_CHF_USD = 1.13e18;

  uint256 public constant BASE_GOLD_USD = 2500e18; // per reference unit

  // ============================================================

  // EVENTS (BP §13.7.3)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Emitted when the genesis event is completed.

  event GenesisCompleted(

  uint256 indexed timestamp,

  uint256 depositAmount,

  uint256 genesisSupply

  );

  /// @dev Emitted to verify the index base denominator.

  /// Allows off-chain audit of the index base calculation (§9.6).

  event GenesisVerification(

  uint256 indexed indexBaseDenominator,

  uint256 eurUsdBase,

  uint256 gbpUsdBase,

  uint256 jpyUsdBase,

  uint256 cnyUsdBase,

  uint256 chfUsdBase, // v2.0: added (§3.4)

  uint256 goldUsdBase, // v2.0: added (§3.4)

  uint256 timestamp

  );

  /// @dev Emitted when the Genesis Reserve address is transferred.

  event GenesisReserveTransferred(

  address indexed oldAddress,

  address indexed newAddress,

  uint256 timestamp

  );

  /// @dev Emitted when operational surplus is swept to the Cold
  Treasury.

  event TreasurySwept(

  uint256 amount,

  uint256 timestamp

  );

  /// @dev Emitted when Cold Treasury funds are used to replenish the
  reserve.

  event ReserveReplenished(

  uint256 amount,

  uint256 timestamp

  );

  /// @dev Emitted when a fee accrues to the operational wallet.

  event FeeAccrued(

  string feeType,

  uint256 amount,

  uint256 timestamp

  );

  // ============================================================

  // GENESIS FUNCTIONS (BP §13.2.6 / §13.7)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Initialises the protocol (Genesis Event).

  /// Only callable once at deployment by the deployer.

  function genesis() external onlyDeployer {

  require(!genesisComplete, "Genesis already complete");

  // 1. Transfer genesis deposit from deployer to contract

  IERC20(USDC).transferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), GENESIS_DEPOSIT);

  // 2. Atomic swap to buy the full basket (per the §3.4 genesis

  // snapshot; same aggregator path as user minting — Listing 11)

  bytes memory swapData = encodeGenesisSwapData(GENESIS_DEPOSIT);

  (bool success, ) = AGGREGATOR.call(swapData);

  require(success, "Genesis swap failed");

  // 3. Compute and store the index base denominator

  INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR = computeIndexBase();

  // 4. Emit genesis verification event (see §9.6)

  emit GenesisVerification(

  INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR,

  BASE_EUR_USD,

  BASE_GBP_USD,

  BASE_JPY_USD,

  BASE_CNY_USD,

  BASE_CHF_USD,

  BASE_GOLD_USD,

  block.timestamp

  );

  // 5. Mint genesis MTQ to the Genesis Reserve Account

  _mint(genesisReserveAddress, GENESIS_SUPPLY);

  // 6. Update reserve state

  updateReserveState();

  // 7. Mark genesis complete

  genesisComplete = true;

  emit GenesisCompleted(block.timestamp, GENESIS_DEPOSIT,
  GENESIS_SUPPLY);

  }

  // ============================================================

  // GOVERNANCE FUNCTIONS (BP §13.7)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Transfers the Genesis Reserve address to a new 4/7 Multi-Sig.

  /// Only callable by the Constitutional Council (7/7) with a 90-day

  /// timelock. Why: the Genesis Reserve holds 1,000,000 MTQ — a

  /// critical protocol asset — so the address must be mutable to

  /// support signer rotation or migration to a new Safe.

  function transferGenesisReserve(address newReserve)

  external onlyConstitutionalCouncil

  {

  require(newReserve != address(0), "Invalid address");

  require(newReserve != genesisReserveAddress, "Already set");

  address old = genesisReserveAddress;

  genesisReserveAddress = newReserve;

  emit GenesisReserveTransferred(old, newReserve, block.timestamp);

  }

  // ============================================================

  // TREASURY SWEEP FUNCTIONS (BP §13.4.2 / §13.6)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Sweeps excess operational surplus to the Cold Treasury.

  /// Called by the keeper bot daily (or after large fee accruals).

  function sweepTreasury() external onlyKeeper {

  uint256 balance = IERC20(USDC).balanceOf(address(this));

  if (balance > SWEEP_THRESHOLD) {

  uint256 sweepAmount = balance - SWEEP_THRESHOLD;

  IERC20(USDC).transfer(COLD_TREASURY, sweepAmount);

  emit TreasurySwept(sweepAmount, block.timestamp);

  }

  }

  /// @dev Transfers funds from the Cold Treasury to the Reserve Vault

  /// (emergency only). Requires 4/7 Multi-Sig and RR < 1.05 (§14.2).

  /// Security: emergency function — not callable by keepers or the

  /// DAO, only by the Emergency Council (4/7 Multi-Sig).

  function replenishReserve(uint256 amount) external onlyMultiSig {

  require(getReserveRatio() < 1.05e18, "RR not in stress mode");

  IERC20(USDC).transferFrom(COLD_TREASURY, address(this), amount);

  // Update reserve state to reflect the new assets

  updateReserveState();

  emit ReserveReplenished(amount, block.timestamp);

  }

  // ============================================================

  // ACCOUNTING HELPERS (BP §13.7)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Computes the index base denominator. Called during genesis
  and

  /// stored as INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR. v2.0: seven components (CHF

  /// and gold included, §3.4); the v1.2 sum had five currency terms.

  /// All quantities and fixings are 1e18-scaled; the sum is

  /// normalized back to the 1e18 scale.

  function computeIndexBase() internal view returns (uint256) {

  return (Q_USD * 1e18 // USD leg: fixing 1.00

  + Q_EUR * BASE_EUR_USD

  + Q_GBP * BASE_GBP_USD

  + Q_JPY * BASE_JPY_USD

  + Q_CNY * BASE_CNY_USD

  + Q_CHF * BASE_CHF_USD

  + Q_GOLD * BASE_GOLD_USD

  ) / 1e18;

  }

  /// @dev Verifies that the stored INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR matches the

  /// base-date FX fixings (§3.4). Used for off-chain audit (§9.6).

  function verifyIndexBase() external view returns (bool) {

  uint256 recomputed = (Q_USD * 1e18

  + Q_EUR * BASE_EUR_USD

  + Q_GBP * BASE_GBP_USD

  + Q_JPY * BASE_JPY_USD

  + Q_CNY * BASE_CNY_USD

  + Q_CHF * BASE_CHF_USD

  + Q_GOLD * BASE_GOLD_USD

  ) / 1e18;

  return recomputed == INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR;

  }

  // ============================================================

  // MODIFIERS (BP §13.7)

  // ============================================================

  modifier onlyDeployer() {

  require(msg.sender == deployer, "Only deployer");

  _;

  }

  modifier onlyKeeper() {

  require(msg.sender == keeper, "Only keeper");

  _;

  }

  modifier onlyMultiSig() {

  // Implement via Gnosis Safe or equivalent 4/7 Multi-Sig

  require(isMultiSig(msg.sender), "Only Multi-Sig");

  _;

  }

  modifier onlyConstitutionalCouncil() {

  // Implement via 7/7 Multi-Sig with 90-day timelock

  require(isConstitutionalCouncil(msg.sender), "Only Constitutional
  Council");

  _;

  }

  }

  interface IERC20 {

  function transferFrom(address from, address to, uint256 value)
  external returns (bool);

  function transfer(address to, uint256 value) external returns (bool);

  function balanceOf(address account) external view returns (uint256);

  }

20.5.3 Deployment Notes (Summary)

At deployment: set genesisReserveAddress to a dedicated 4/7 Gnosis Safe
— on Arc/Monad testnet the default is
0xE71869C662733642bfBb262B8c6bad8B0fBfA7D0, with the Cold Treasury using
the same address on testnet — and set keeper to a trusted automated bot
address (the deployer or a dedicated bot). The Cold Treasury itself must
be deployed as a 4/7 Gnosis Safe with hardware-wallet signers satisfying
the requirements of Section 20.3.3. The genesis() function is protected
by onlyDeployer and is callable only once; replenishReserve() is
protected by the 4/7 Multi-Sig with the RR < 1.05 guard;
transferGenesisReserve() requires the Constitutional Council (7/7) with
the 90-day timelock of the governance process (Chapter 22). Post-genesis
verification uses Foundry — verifyIndexBase() to check the stored
denominator, balanceOf(genesisReserveAddress) to check the genesis
reserve balance, and the contract and Cold Treasury balances to check
the operational pools — with the complete command set and deployment
checklist consolidated in Appendix B. On Solana Devnet the address-based
mechanism is replaced by the testnet-specific approach of Appendix D.

  Fidelity note (Listing 12 vs. BP §13.7): carried verbatim (cleaned)
  are the genesis constants and deposit/supply values, the genesis()
  sequence including the verification emission, the sweep and
  replenishment functions with their guards, the governed
  genesis-reserve transfer, all six events and the modifier set.
  Deliberate adaptations: (i) GFB_BASE_DENOMINATOR is renamed
  INDEX_BASE_DENOMINATOR and computed over seven components — the v1.2
  five-currency sum is extended with the CHF and gold base terms per the
  Section 3.4 snapshot, and GenesisVerification correspondingly carries
  the CHF and gold fixings; (ii) the genesis quantities and base fixings
  are declared explicitly from the Section 3.4 snapshot, with decimal
  normalization of the sum made explicit; (iii) the genesis basket
  purchase follows the Section 3.4 snapshot weights rather than the v1.2
  fixed split, via the same aggregator path as Listing 11; and (iv) the
  Foundry verification commands are summarized in prose with the full
  checklist in Appendix B.
