Chapters 19 and 20 close the user-facing and initialization layers of
the protocol: entry and exit are priced against the adaptive index and
NAV respectively (invariants I5 and I6 of Section 2.6), genesis fixes
the normalization base immutably and verifiably (Sections 3.4 and 9.6),
and the three-pool accounting keeps collateral, operations and surplus
strictly separated so that the closed-loop reconciliation of Chapter 18
always balances. The remaining operational layer defines who may change
what: the risk-state machinery and fee schedule of Chapter 21 (with the
RR tiers of Section 14.2), and the governance processes — parameter
registry, data-provider approval, timelocks and councils — of Chapter
22. Every constant carried above is registered there with its governance
class, and the deployment checklists of Appendix B and the testnet
genesis procedures of Appendix D operationalize this chapter end to end.

21. The Risk State Machine, Crisis Execution and Emergency Actions

21.1 Purpose

  The risk state machine is the protocol's immune system: a
  deterministic control plane that continuously diagnoses the solvency
  and liquidity condition of MTQ Σ and reconfigures the protocol's
  operating posture in response [BP §14.1]. It is the single layer at
  which fees, execution behavior, rebalancing urgency, oracle
  confirmation depth and governance communication all change together —
  coherently, predictably and without human discretion — as conditions
  deteriorate and recover.

The module performs four functions [BP §14.1]. First, it defines the
risk state of the protocol — one of NORMAL, CAUTION, STRESS, DEFENSIVE,
EMERGENCY or RECOVERY — derived from the Reserve Ratio (RR) and the
Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR), both computed by the reserve valuation
layer with asset-specific haircuts (Chapter 14; Invariant I4). Second,
it enforces state-dependent actions: minting throttles, redemption fees,
rebalancing urgency and communication cadence. Third, it provides the
governance hierarchy with a clear separation of powers — Constitutional,
Monetary, Risk and Emergency (Section 22.3). Fourth, it enables
time-locked parameter updates, so that no parameter can be changed
maliciously or in a rush (Sections 22.4 and 22.6).

1.  Deterministic state. The state is derived entirely from on-chain
    metrics (RR, LCR, oracle health); no human subjectivity is involved
    in the classification.

2.  Monotonic transitions under stress. The protocol moves immediately
    toward more restrictive states during a crisis, while recovery
    requires sustained, time-based improvement.

3.  Layered governance. Constitutional parameters are immutable,
    monetary parameters require a DAO vote and timelock, risk parameters
    require Risk Council approval, and emergency actions require a
    Multi-Sig with hardware wallet requirements.

The risk state must not be confused with the crisis score of Section
5.5. The crisis score aggregates market-side stress evidence (FX
volatility, cross-asset correlation, liquidity stress, drawdowns) and
modulates the MASE objective and the execution posture (§13.7); the risk
state is the protocol-side solvency and liquidity condition of the
reserve itself. The two are complementary and converge on execution: a
market crisis without solvency stress changes how carefully the system
trades, while a deterioration of RR or LCR changes what the system is
permitted to do at all, including the state-contingent reserve posture
of Sections 14.4–14.5. Both publish their outputs through the
transparency layer (Chapter 24), and both feed the honest-status
declaration of Section 25.

21.2 The Six Risk States

The protocol classifies itself into exactly six risk states, labeled S1
through S6 [BP §14.2.1]. The classification is a pure function of the
two solvency metrics — the reserve ratio RR_t and the liquidity coverage
ratio LCR_t (Section 2.5) — evaluated after every mint, redemption,
rebalance and price update:

State_(t) = F(RR_(t), LCR_(t)) ∈ {S₁,…,S₆}

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  State          RR Range       LCR Range      Entry Trigger  Description
  -------------- -------------- -------------- -------------- ----------------
  NORMAL (S1)    RR ≥ 1.10      LCR ≥ 1.00     Immediate      Standard
                                               while both     operations. Full
                                               targets hold   mint, redeem,
                                                              and rebalancing.

  CAUTION (S2)   1.05 ≤ RR <    LCR ≥ 0.90     Immediate on   Defensive
                 1.10                          RR < 1.10 or   posture. Minting
                                               LCR < 1.00     throttled to
                                                              50%. Rebalancing
                                                              urgency
                                                              increased.

  STRESS (S3)    1.02 ≤ RR <    LCR ≥ 0.80     Immediate on   Significant
                 1.05                          RR < 1.05 or   stress. Minting
                                               LCR < 0.90     paused.
                                                              Redemption fee
                                                              raised to 0.50%.
                                                              Emergency
                                                              rebalancing
                                                              triggered.

  DEFENSIVE (S4) 1.00 ≤ RR <    LCR ≥ 0.70     Immediate on   Severe stress.
                 1.02                          RR < 1.02 or   Redemptions
                                               LCR < 0.80     throttled (fee
                                                              1.00%).
                                                              Rebalancing
                                                              forced.
                                                              Governance
                                                              notified.

  EMERGENCY (S5) RR < 1.00      Any            Immediate on   Existential.
                                               hard-floor     Redemptions
                                               breach         paused. Circuit
                                               (Invariant I2, breakers
                                               §2.6)          engaged. Council
                                                              emergency
                                                              session called.

  RECOVERY (S6)  RR ≥ 1.05      LCR ≥ 0.90     From a more    Gradual
                 (rising)                      restrictive    restoration.
                                               state, after   Minting resumes
                                               48 h sustained at 25% capacity.
                                               confirmation   Fees reduced
                                                              incrementally.
  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Two reading rules apply to the table. First, both metrics must qualify
for a state: NORMAL requires RR ≥ 1.10 and LCR ≥ 1.00 simultaneously, so
a liquidity shortfall alone can hold the protocol in CAUTION even while
the reserve ratio is comfortable — the worse of the two metrics is
always the binding constraint. Second, the EMERGENCY boundary is
constitutional: RR < 1.00 violates the hard floor of Invariant I2
(Section 2.6), which is why the S5 entry trigger is immediate and why
the hard floor itself is immutable in the parameter registry (Section
22.4).

RECOVERY (S6) is the residual posture of the classifier: solvency at or
above the hard floor with ratios improving but not yet back to the
NORMAL thresholds. The formal classifier reaches it when the ratios fail
the DEFENSIVE liquidity test while solvency holds — for example, RR ≥
1.05 with LCR below 0.70 — or during a confirmed climb back from a
deeper state. Its action profile (25% minting throttle, 0.30% redemption
fee, increased rebalancing urgency) sits deliberately between CAUTION
and STRESS, which is also how the transition logic of Section 21.3 ranks
it.

21.3 State Transition Logic

State transitions obey one asymmetric rule [BP §14.2.2]. Movement toward
a more restrictive state (for example NORMAL → CAUTION) is immediate
upon breaching the threshold. Movement toward a less restrictive state
(for example STRESS → CAUTION) requires the protocol to remain above the
destination state's threshold continuously for 48 consecutive hours —
the recovery confirmation period. Degradation is fast; recovery is slow
and must be earned:

NORMAL → CAUTION → STRESS → DEFENSIVE → EMERGENCY (immediate on breach)

EMERGENCY → DEFENSIVE → STRESS → CAUTION → NORMAL (48 h sustained
confirmation per step)

t_(confirm) ≥ 48 h (sustained, no breach)

Each upward step requires the metrics to satisfy the destination state's
thresholds continuously for the confirmation period: 48 hours at RR ≥
1.10 to leave CAUTION into NORMAL, 48 hours at RR ≥ 1.05 to leave
STRESS, 48 hours at RR ≥ 1.02 to leave DEFENSIVE, and 48 hours at RR ≥
1.00 to leave EMERGENCY. The confirmation period is the control-plane
member of the protocol's anti-oscillation family — it composes with
constituency hysteresis (Section 4.7), stress-adaptive smoothing
(Section 8.4) and the rebalancing direction lock (Section 11.5), each of
which prevents oscillation at a different timescale. A noisy metric
sequence can therefore never flip the protocol between postures; the
worst it can do is hold the protocol in the more conservative state.

The 48-hour constant is carried in the reference implementation as
RECOVERY_CONFIRMATION_PERIOD (Listing 13), alongside the immutable
RR_HARD_FLOOR of Invariant I2. The source parameter table classes the
confirmation period as a Monetary (DAO 51%) parameter while the
reference contract declares it immutable; this edition retains the
immutable constant as the conservative reading — a stricter governance
class than the table assigns — and flags the discrepancy for resolution
during deployment registration (Section 22.4).

Operationally, updateState() runs after every mint, redeem, rebalance
and price update (the _updateState hook of Listing 13), so the state is
never stale by more than one state-relevant event. Because deterioration
is immediate and recovery is delayed, the machine is biased toward
conservatism by construction — exactly the bias an instrument whose
promise is purchasing-power protection should have.

21.4 The State-Dependent Action Matrix

Each state activates a coherent bundle of actions, so that the
protocol's fee schedule, execution posture, reserve objective and
communication cadence move together rather than independently [BP
§14.3]. The full matrix:

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Action         S1 NORMAL    S2 CAUTION     S3 STRESS      S4 DEFENSIVE   S5          S6 RECOVERY
                                                                           EMERGENCY   
  -------------- ------------ -------------- -------------- -------------- ----------- --------------
  Minting        Allowed      50% throttle   Paused         Paused         Paused      25% throttle

  Redemption     Allowed      Allowed        Allowed        Allowed        Paused      Allowed
                 (0.15%)      (0.15%)        (0.50%)        (1.00%)                    (0.30%)

  Rebalancing    Active       Active         Active —       Active —       Paused      Active
                 (urgency 1)  (urgency 2)    emergency      forced                     (urgency 2)
                                             (urgency 3)    (urgency 4)                

  RR Target      1.10         1.08           1.05           1.03           1.00        1.08

  Sweep          $10,000      $10,000        $10,000        $10,000        Paused      $10,000
  Threshold                                                                            

  Oracle         Standard     Dual-source    Dual-source,   Full           Quorum +    Dual-source
  Confirmation   validation   confirmation   tightened      multi-source   circuit     confirmation
                                             bounds         quorum         breakers    

  Governance     Quarterly    Monthly        Weekly         Daily          Immediate   Weekly
  Notification                                                                         
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The oracle-confirmation row is a v2.0 integration (Chapter 17 and
  Section 5.5): the v1.2 matrix rows — minting, redemption, rebalancing,
  RR target, sweep threshold and governance notification — are carried
  unchanged from BP §14.3.1, and the confirmation ladder is added so
  that price trust tightens exactly when solvency deteriorates.

How to read the matrix. The redemption row is the state-dependent fee
schedule applied by the minting and redemption contracts (Chapter 19):
fees rise from 0.15% (S1–S2) to 0.50% (S3) and 1.00% (S4), redemptions
pause in S5, and S6 re-admits them at 0.30% — an anti-run circuit that
prices liquidity demand by the state that demands it. The rebalancing
row maps onto the MARP execution hierarchy (Section 10.3): urgency
levels 1–4 correspond to the normal, increased, emergency and forced
execution regimes, while S5 suspends routine rebalancing entirely;
council-directed execution (forceRebalance, Section 22.5; Level 5 of the
§10.3 hierarchy) remains the only discretionary path in EMERGENCY. The
RR-target row resets the reserve manager's objective ratio by state
(Chapters 13–14, Sections 14.4–14.5), and the sweep-threshold row
suspends treasury sweeps in S5 (Chapter 20).

The communication row is the protocol's crisis voice. Governance
notification frequency rises from quarterly (S1) to immediate (S5), and
every state transition emits an on-chain event consumed by the
transparency layer (Chapter 24). Nothing about a crisis can be silent:
the state, the entry trigger, the applied actions and the recovery clock
are all public, machine-readable and archived — which is precisely what
the honest-status declaration (Section 25) requires.

21.5 Geopolitical Eject — Staged Liquidation and Reintegration

  The Geopolitical Eject module is the reserve-level emergency defence
  of the protocol: it protects the assets backing MTQ Σ from instruments
  that become unstable, de-pegged, frozen or sanctioned [BP §11.1].
  Where the risk state machine guards the protocol's solvency ratios,
  the eject module guards the quality of the individual reserve assets
  themselves.

  When an asset becomes unreliable, exit slowly and intelligently. Never
  be a forced seller into a thin market.

Unlike a simple sell-everything response, the module uses a staged
liquidation ladder that minimizes market impact and avoids crystallizing
losses during temporary dislocations, and a reintegration score that
prevents gaming on the way back in [BP §11.1]. The module depends on
three neighbors: the Asset Admission Registry for asset metadata
(Chapter 15), the oracle architecture for price feeds (Chapter 17), and
the execution layer for trade execution (Section 11.11). One boundary
must be stated crisply: liquidation proceeds are redeployed into
eligible reserve assets under the reserve rules (Chapters 13–15),
because the eject module protects solvency — it never participates in
secondary-market price support of the MTQ Σ token itself, which is
constitutionally forbidden (Invariant I12, Section 13.6).

21.5.1 Purpose

The module exists because reserve instruments — stablecoins above all —
carry issuer risk that the index layer cannot see. A reference weight of
5% CNY exposure is a monetary statement; the reserve instrument
implementing that exposure can still de-peg, freeze redemptions, or fall
under sanctions. The eject module is the emergency defence mechanism
that answers instrument-level unreliability: it detects the failure
(Section 21.5.2), exits it gradually (Section 21.5.3), re-admits it only
under accumulated evidence (Section 21.5.4), and bypasses all gradualism
when the issuer itself fails (Section 21.5.5) [BP §11.1].

The design tension the module resolves is time. Selling 100% of a
distressed position at once converts a temporary dislocation into a
permanent loss and makes the protocol the forced seller that thin
markets punish. Selling too slowly converts a deteriorating position
into a solvency problem. The staged ladder prices that tension
explicitly: exit velocity increases with the duration of the distress
and with the deterioration of the reserve ratio, so the protocol sells
faster precisely when the evidence gets worse — and pauses when its own
selling starts moving the market against it (the 5% rule, Section
21.5.3).

21.5.2 The De-peg Detector

The protocol continuously monitors each stablecoin held in the reserve
against its fiat peg, using the oracle architecture (Chapter 17) [BP
§11.2]. Monitoring attaches to the currency code of the exposure: the
specific token implementations are resolved through the Asset Admission
Registry (Chapter 15), and every admitted instrument for a monitored
currency is checked independently. Gold is not part of this detector —
it has no peg; its source risk is handled instead by the canonical
multi-source price and its independent-source quorum (Chapter 17).

Each stablecoin has an asset-specific peg band [BP §11.2.1]:

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Asset                 Peg Band (Lower)  Peg Band (Upper)  Rationale
  --------------------- ----------------- ----------------- -----------------
  USDC, USDP            0.995             1.005             Extremely liquid;
                                                            tight band.

  EURC                  0.995             1.005             Highly liquid;
                                                            tight band.

  GBP                   0.990             1.010             Lower liquidity;
  (registry-resolved)                                       wider band.

  JPY                   0.990             1.010             Lower liquidity;
  (registry-resolved)                                       wider band.

  CNY                   0.985             1.015             Limited
  (registry-resolved,                                       liquidity; widest
  CNH)                                                      band.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

The band width tracks liquidity: the deepest markets (USDC, USDP, EURC)
admit a tight ±0.5% band because routine trading noise stays inside it,
while the less liquid currency implementations admit wider bands so that
ordinary illiquidity is not mistaken for a broken peg. The detector rule
is then [BP §11.2.1]:

Unstable ⇔ ( P_(t) < band_(low) or P_(t) > band_(high) ) for more than
12 consecutive hours

The 12-hour window is deliberately conservative: it prevents false
positives from temporary wicks and flash dislocations, which routinely
resolve within hours. The consecutive-hours requirement is equally
deliberate: the timer resets to zero the moment the price returns inside
the band, so an asset oscillating in and out of the band never
accumulates detector time. The detector function (isDepegged, Listing
13) implements exactly this logic — timer start on the first
outside-band observation, timer reset on return, flag after the window —
and the detection window and the bands themselves are Risk-Council
parameters in the parameter registry (Section 22.4).

21.5.3 The Staged Liquidation Ladder

Once a de-peg is confirmed, the protocol executes a staged liquidation
rather than selling 100% at once [BP §11.3]. Each stage sells an
additional fraction of the original position, and each stage's trigger
conditions escalate with elapsed distress time and with the protocol's
own reserve ratio:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Stage             Cumulative Sell % Trigger Condition Action
  ----------------- ----------------- ----------------- -----------------
  Stage 1           10%               De-peg confirmed  Sell 10% of the
                                      for more than 12  asset
                                      hours             immediately.

  Stage 2           25%               De-peg for more   Sell an
                                      than 24 hours OR  additional 15%
                                      RR < 1.08         (total 25%).

  Stage 3           50%               De-peg for more   Sell an
                                      than 48 hours OR  additional 25%
                                      RR < 1.05         (total 50%).

  Stage 4           100%              Issuer freeze     Sell the
                                      confirmed OR RR < remaining 50%
                                      1.02              (total 100%).
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

The ladder couples asset-level distress to protocol-level solvency: the
RR thresholds 1.08, 1.05 and 1.02 sit exactly at the state boundaries of
Section 21.2, so a de-pegging asset is liquidated faster precisely as
the protocol itself slides toward STRESS and DEFENSIVE. The cumulative
percentages (10% → 25% → 50% → 100%) are constitutional and immutable
(Section 22.4): the shape of the ladder is part of the promise that the
protocol will never dump a distressed position in one trade, while the
trigger conditions allow governance to tune sensitivity within that
fixed shape. One rule guards every stage transition [BP §11.3.1]: each
stage executes only if the previous stage's sell did not worsen the
deviation by more than 5%.

The 5% rule formalizes the pause [BP §11.3.3]:

next stage paused ⇔ (P_(stage start) - P_(t))/(P_(stage start)) > 5%

If, during a staged liquidation, the asset price drops more than 5% from
the price at the start of the current stage, the protocol pauses the
next stage until the price stabilizes — no further drop for 24 hours.
The rule exists because the protocol's own selling can become the
market's problem: if each tranche moves the price against the next, the
ladder halts rather than chasing the price down. Exiting is thereby
bounded in both directions — never all at once, and never into a
collapsing tape. Sell execution itself runs through the execution layer
(Section 11.11), whose crisis-mode rules widen no-trade thresholds and
shrink execution sizes precisely when conditions are distressed (Section
13.7).

21.5.4 The Reintegration Score (Anti-Gaming)

An ejected asset is not automatically re-purchased when it returns to
its peg band [BP §11.4]. A naive band-triggered re-entry would be
gameable: an attacker with sufficient market power could push the price
back inside the band temporarily, trigger re-purchase at distressed
prices, and profit as the peg breaks again. The reintegration score
closes that attack surface by demanding sustained, multi-dimensional
evidence of genuine stability:

  R_(score) = w₁ · TimeInBand + w₂ · LiquidityDepth + w₃ ·
  OracleAgreement + w₄ · (1 - Volatility)

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Component               Weight (w_i)            Description
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  TimeInBand              0.40                    Duration the asset has
                                                  traded within its peg
                                                  band (scaled 0–1;
                                                  maximum 7 days).

  LiquidityDepth          0.25                    24-hour DEX volume
                                                  relative to the
                                                  protocol's holdings
                                                  (scaled 0–1).

  OracleAgreement         0.20                    Agreement between the
                                                  three oracle sources
                                                  (scaled 0–1).

  Volatility              0.15                    24-hour price
                                                  volatility (scaled 0–1,
                                                  higher = more volatile;
                                                  enters the score as 1 −
                                                  Volatility, so calm
                                                  markets score higher).
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Time-in-band dominates (w_1 = 0.40) because persistence is the strongest
single evidence that a peg has genuinely healed; liquidity depth guards
against re-entering a market too thin to absorb the protocol's size;
oracle agreement (Chapter 17) requires the independent sources to
concur, defeating single-feed manipulation; and the volatility term
penalizes choppy in-band trading, which is stability in appearance only.
The component weights and the threshold are Risk-Council parameters
(Section 22.4); the score is evaluated by the re-entry function of
Listing 13, whose helper functions query oracle history and DEX volume
data [BP §11]. The re-entry threshold is:

R_(score) ≥ 0.80

A score at or above 0.80 means the asset is considered stable enough to
begin re-entry — but re-entry itself is staged, mirroring the
liquidation ladder in reverse [BP §11.4.3]:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Stage                   Cumulative Buy %        Condition
  ----------------------- ----------------------- -----------------------
  1                       25%                     R_score ≥ 0.80
                                                  sustained for 24 hours

  2                       50%                     R_score ≥ 0.85
                                                  sustained for 48 hours

  3                       100%                    R_score ≥ 0.90
                                                  sustained for 72 hours
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

The reverse ladder is deliberately stricter than the exit ladder at
every step: it requires higher scores (0.80, 0.85, 0.90) sustained for
longer (24, 48, 72 hours) as the position size grows. The asymmetry is
the anti-gaming posture of the whole module — getting out is defensive
and starts small; getting back in is offensive and starts only under
accumulated evidence. Re-purchased amounts are executed through the same
execution layer (Section 11.11) and remain subject to the admission
rules of Chapter 15, so a fully reintegrated asset ends where it
started: an admitted, monitored reserve instrument.

21.5.5 Special Cases — Issuer Freeze and Sanctions

If an asset issuer freezes redemptions or is sanctioned, the protocol
bypasses the staged ladder entirely and executes immediate 100%
liquidation [BP §11.5]. The logic is that a freeze or sanctions event is
not a price dislocation that may heal — it is a structural impairment of
the instrument's redeemability and legality, and every hour of continued
holding concentrates legal and solvency risk. The staged ladder's
virtue, patience, would here be its vice.

Freeze detection runs on two channels [BP §11.5.1]. On-chain, the
protocol watches for the asset's pause() or freeze() functions being
called, detected via event logs. Off-chain, an issuer announcement or
regulator action is reported through the governance oracle (Chapter 17)
and confirmed by the Risk Council. When either channel confirms, the
emergencyLiquidate function (Listing 13) sells the entire balance and
removes the asset from the Asset Admission Registry.

Two safeguards discipline this strongest of powers. The function is
executable only by the Emergency Council — a 4/7 Multi-Sig with hardware
wallet requirements [BP §11] — and removal from the registry means that
re-admission can never occur through the reintegration score alone: a
frozen-then-unfrozen or delisted-then-relisted instrument must pass the
full admission process of Chapter 15 again, including eligibility,
custody and issuer-quality review. The special case is thus the one
place the protocol moves fast, and it pays for that speed with the
slowest possible path back in.

21.6 Smart Contract Implementation — Risk State Machine and Eject

The state machine and the eject module deploy as a single operations
contract: the states share the reserve-ratio feeds, the eject ladder's
triggers read the same RR used by the state classifier, and the
state-dependent actions (minting throttles, redemption fees, rebalancing
urgency) are applied through abstract hooks implemented by the minting
and redemption contracts (Chapter 19) and the MARP executor (Section
11.11) [BP §14.6]. updateState() is called after every mint, redeem,
rebalance and price update, and updateDepegStatus() runs on each price
update alongside it. Data hooks read the reserve valuation (Chapter 14),
the oracle layer (Chapter 17) and the asset registry (Chapter 15). The
governance addresses — dao, riskCouncil, emergencyCouncil and
constitutionalCouncil — are set at deployment [BP §14]. Parameter
updates arrive exclusively through the governance registry of Section
22.6, which enforces role checks and timelocks before dispatching here;
this contract additionally verifies that its caller is the governance
registry, so the two contracts form an authorization plane and an
execution plane that each check the other.

Listing 13 — Risk state machine, state-dependent actions and staged
liquidation (adapts BP §14.6 and §11)

  // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT

  pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

  /// @notice Risk state machine, state-dependent actions and staged
  liquidation.

  /// @dev Authorization plane for parameter updates lives in the
  governance

  /// registry (Listing 14); this contract is the execution plane.

  abstract contract RiskStateMachine {

  // ============================================================

  // 1. STATE DEFINITIONS (BP §14.6.1)

  // ============================================================

  enum ProtocolState { NORMAL, CAUTION, STRESS, DEFENSIVE, EMERGENCY,
  RECOVERY }

  ProtocolState public currentState;

  // State transition timestamps (for recovery confirmation)

  mapping(ProtocolState => uint256) public stateEntryTime;

  // ---- Constitutional parameters (immutable) ----

  uint256 public constant RR_HARD_FLOOR = 1.00e18; // Invariant I2
  (§2.6)

  uint256 public constant RECOVERY_CONFIRMATION_PERIOD = 48 hours; //
  §21.3

  // ---- Monetary parameters (DAO-controlled via the registry, §22.6)
  ----

  uint256 public rrTarget = 1.10e18;

  uint256 public rrStressFloor = 1.05e18;

  uint256 public mintFee = 0.001e18; // 0.10%; applied by Chapter 19

  uint256 public redeemFeeNormal = 0.0015e18; // 0.15%

  // ---- Risk parameters (Risk Council-controlled via the registry,
  §22.6) ----

  uint256 public redeemFeeStress = 0.005e18; // 0.50%

  uint256 public redeemFeeDefensive = 0.010e18; // 1.00%

  uint256 public lcrTarget = 1.00e18;

  uint256 public depegWindow = 12 hours; // §21.5.2; Risk Council (4/7)

  // ---- Governance addresses (set at deployment) ----

  address public dao; // DAO with 51% quorum

  address public riskCouncil; // 4/7 Multi-Sig

  address public emergencyCouncil; // 4/7 Multi-Sig, hardware wallets

  address public constitutionalCouncil; // 7/7 Multi-Sig

  address public governance; // GovernanceParameterRegistry (Listing 14)

  // ============================================================

  // 2. GEOPOLITICAL EJECT STATE (BP §11.6.1)

  // ============================================================

  // De-peg tracking

  mapping(bytes3 => uint256) public depegStartTimes;

  mapping(bytes3 => uint256) public liquidationStage; // 0 = not
  ejected, 1-4 = stage

  mapping(bytes3 => uint256) public stageStartPrices;

  mapping(bytes3 => uint256) public reintegrationStage; // 0 = none, 1-3
  = re-entry stage

  // Peg bands (asset-specific; Risk-Council parameters, §21.5.2)

  mapping(bytes3 => uint256) public pegLowerBand;

  mapping(bytes3 => uint256) public pegUpperBand;

  // Reintegration score thresholds and component weights (BP §11.6.1)

  uint256 public constant REINTEGRATION_THRESHOLD = 0.80e18; // 80%

  uint256 public constant WEIGHT_TIME_IN_BAND = 0.40e18;

  uint256 public constant WEIGHT_LIQUIDITY = 0.25e18;

  uint256 public constant WEIGHT_ORACLE_AGREEMENT = 0.20e18;

  uint256 public constant WEIGHT_VOLATILITY = 0.15e18;

  // ============================================================

  // 3. EVENTS (BP §14.7 and §11.6.2)

  // ============================================================

  event StateTransition(ProtocolState indexed oldState, ProtocolState
  indexed newState, bool immediate, uint256 timestamp);

  event MintingPaused(uint256 timestamp);

  event MintingResumed(uint256 timestamp, uint256 throttlePercent);

  event RedeemingPaused(uint256 timestamp);

  event RedeemingResumed(uint256 timestamp);

  event RebalancingPaused(uint256 timestamp);

  event RebalancingResumed(uint256 timestamp);

  event OperationsResumed(uint256 timestamp);

  event RedemptionFeeUpdated(uint256 newFee, uint256 timestamp);

  event DepegDetected(bytes3 indexed currencyCode, uint256 price,
  uint256 timestamp);

  event LiquidationExecuted(bytes3 indexed currencyCode, uint256 amount,
  uint256 stage);

  event ReintegrationScore(bytes3 indexed currencyCode, uint256 score,
  bool thresholdMet);

  event ReintegrationExecuted(bytes3 indexed currencyCode, uint256
  amount, uint256 stage);

  event EmergencyLiquidation(bytes3 indexed currencyCode, uint256
  amount);

  modifier onlyEmergencyCouncil() { require(msg.sender ==
  emergencyCouncil, "Only Emergency Council"); _; }

  modifier onlyGovernance() { require(msg.sender == governance, "Only
  governance registry"); _; }

  constructor(address dao_, address riskCouncil_, address
  emergencyCouncil_, address constitutionalCouncil_, address
  governance_) {

  dao = dao_;

  riskCouncil = riskCouncil_;

  emergencyCouncil = emergencyCouncil_;

  constitutionalCouncil = constitutionalCouncil_;

  governance = governance_;

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 4. STATE UPDATE (BP §14.6.2)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Updates the protocol state based on current RR and LCR.

  /// Called after every mint, redeem, rebalance, and price update.

  function updateState() internal {

  uint256 rr = getReserveRatio();

  uint256 lcr = getLCR();

  ProtocolState newState;

  // Determine the target state based on RR and LCR

  if (rr >= rrTarget && lcr >= lcrTarget) {

  newState = ProtocolState.NORMAL;

  } else if (rr >= rrStressFloor && lcr >= 0.90e18) {

  newState = ProtocolState.CAUTION;

  } else if (rr >= 1.02e18 && lcr >= 0.80e18) {

  newState = ProtocolState.STRESS;

  } else if (rr >= RR_HARD_FLOOR && lcr >= 0.70e18) {

  newState = ProtocolState.DEFENSIVE;

  } else if (rr < RR_HARD_FLOOR) {

  newState = ProtocolState.EMERGENCY;

  } else {

  // RECOVERY: solvency at or above the hard floor but ratios not

  // yet back to NORMAL thresholds (rising-RR residual case)

  newState = ProtocolState.RECOVERY;

  }

  // State transition logic

  if (newState != currentState) {

  // Moving to a more restrictive state? Immediate.

  if (isMoreRestrictive(newState, currentState)) {

  transitionTo(newState, true);

  } else {

  // Moving to a less restrictive state? 48h confirmation.

  if (block.timestamp - stateEntryTime[currentState] >=
  RECOVERY_CONFIRMATION_PERIOD) {

  transitionTo(newState, false); // gradual recovery

  } else {

  newState = currentState; // not yet confirmed; stay

  }

  }

  }

  }

  /// @dev Transitions to a new state and applies state-dependent
  actions.

  function transitionTo(ProtocolState newState, bool immediate) internal
  {

  ProtocolState oldState = currentState;

  currentState = newState;

  stateEntryTime[newState] = block.timestamp;

  // Apply state-dependent actions

  applyStateActions(newState);

  // Log the transition

  emit StateTransition(oldState, newState, immediate, block.timestamp);

  }

  /// @dev Restrictiveness rank used by the transition logic: moving up
  the

  /// rank (more restrictive) is immediate; moving down requires the

  /// 48-hour confirmation. RECOVERY sits between CAUTION and STRESS,

  /// matching its action profile (§21.2).

  function restrictiveness(ProtocolState s) internal pure returns
  (uint256) {

  if (s == ProtocolState.NORMAL) return 0; // S1

  if (s == ProtocolState.CAUTION) return 1; // S2

  if (s == ProtocolState.RECOVERY) return 2; // S6

  if (s == ProtocolState.STRESS) return 3; // S3

  if (s == ProtocolState.DEFENSIVE) return 4; // S4

  return 5; // S5 EMERGENCY

  }

  function isMoreRestrictive(ProtocolState a, ProtocolState b) internal
  pure returns (bool) {

  return restrictiveness(a) > restrictiveness(b);

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 5. STATE-DEPENDENT ACTIONS (BP §14.6.3)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Applies actions based on the current state (§21.4 matrix).

  function applyStateActions(ProtocolState state) internal {

  // Minting controls

  if (state == ProtocolState.NORMAL || state == ProtocolState.RECOVERY)
  {

  _resumeMinting();

  if (state == ProtocolState.RECOVERY) {

  _setMintThrottle(25); // 25% capacity

  } else {

  _setMintThrottle(100); // full capacity

  }

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.CAUTION) {

  _setMintThrottle(50); // 50% capacity

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.STRESS || state ==
  ProtocolState.DEFENSIVE) {

  _pauseMinting();

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.EMERGENCY) {

  _pauseMinting();

  _pauseRebalancing();

  }

  // Redemption fees

  if (state == ProtocolState.NORMAL) {

  _setRedemptionFee(redeemFeeNormal);

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.CAUTION) {

  _setRedemptionFee(redeemFeeNormal);

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.STRESS) {

  _setRedemptionFee(redeemFeeStress);

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.DEFENSIVE) {

  _setRedemptionFee(redeemFeeDefensive);

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.EMERGENCY) {

  _pauseRedeeming();

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.RECOVERY) {

  _setRedemptionFee(redeemFeeNormal * 2); // 0.30%

  }

  // Rebalancing urgency (maps to the MARP hierarchy levels, §10.3)

  if (state == ProtocolState.NORMAL) {

  _setRebalanceUrgency(1); // normal

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.CAUTION) {

  _setRebalanceUrgency(2); // increased

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.STRESS) {

  _setRebalanceUrgency(3); // emergency

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.DEFENSIVE) {

  _setRebalanceUrgency(4); // forced

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.EMERGENCY) {

  _setRebalanceUrgency(0); // paused

  } else if (state == ProtocolState.RECOVERY) {

  _setRebalanceUrgency(2); // increased

  }

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 6. DE-PEG DETECTOR (BP §11.2.2)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Maintains the consecutive-hours de-peg timer for one asset.

  /// The timer starts on the first observation outside the band and

  /// resets when the price returns inside the band. Emits the first

  /// confirmation event after the detection window is exceeded.

  function updateDepegStatus(bytes3 currencyCode) internal {

  address token = getAssetForCurrency(currencyCode);

  uint256 price = getPrice(token);

  (uint256 lower, uint256 upper) = getPegBand(currencyCode);

  if (price < lower || price > upper) {

  if (depegStartTimes[currencyCode] == 0) {

  depegStartTimes[currencyCode] = block.timestamp;

  } else if (

  block.timestamp - depegStartTimes[currencyCode] > depegWindow &&

  liquidationStage[currencyCode] == 0

  ) {

  emit DepegDetected(currencyCode, price, block.timestamp);

  }

  } else {

  // Reset the timer if back in band

  depegStartTimes[currencyCode] = 0;

  }

  }

  /// @dev Checks if an asset is de-pegged.

  /// @param currencyCode The currency code (e.g., "EUR").

  /// @return bool True if the asset is outside its peg band for more
  than

  /// the de-peg detection window (12 hours by default).

  function isDepegged(bytes3 currencyCode) public view returns (bool) {

  address token = getAssetForCurrency(currencyCode);

  uint256 price = getPrice(token);

  (uint256 lower, uint256 upper) = getPegBand(currencyCode);

  // Check if price is outside the band

  if (price < lower || price > upper) {

  // Check duration

  uint256 depegStart = depegStartTimes[currencyCode];

  return depegStart != 0 && (block.timestamp - depegStart) >
  depegWindow;

  } else {

  // Back in band: not de-pegged (timer resets on the write path)

  return false;

  }

  }

  function getPegBand(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view returns
  (uint256, uint256) {

  return (pegLowerBand[currencyCode], pegUpperBand[currencyCode]);

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 7. STAGED LIQUIDATION LADDER (BP §11.3.2 and §11.3.3)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Executes staged liquidation of a de-pegged asset.

  /// @param currencyCode The currency code of the asset to liquidate.

  function executeStagedLiquidation(bytes3 currencyCode) external {

  require(isDepegged(currencyCode), "Asset not depegged");

  address token = getAssetForCurrency(currencyCode);

  uint256 totalValue = getAssetValue(currencyCode);

  uint256 sellAmount = 0;

  // Determine stage based on duration and RR

  uint256 depegDuration = block.timestamp -
  depegStartTimes[currencyCode];

  uint256 rr = getReserveRatio();

  // Stage 1: 10% (if depeg > 12h)

  if (depegDuration > depegWindow && liquidationStage[currencyCode] < 1)
  {

  sellAmount = totalValue * 10 / 100;

  liquidationStage[currencyCode] = 1;

  }

  // Stage 2: 25% total (if depeg > 24h OR RR < 1.08)

  else if ((depegDuration > 24 hours || rr < 1.08e18) &&
  liquidationStage[currencyCode] < 2) {

  sellAmount = totalValue * 15 / 100; // additional 15%

  liquidationStage[currencyCode] = 2;

  }

  // Stage 3: 50% total (if depeg > 48h OR RR < 1.05)

  else if ((depegDuration > 48 hours || rr < 1.05e18) &&
  liquidationStage[currencyCode] < 3) {

  sellAmount = totalValue * 25 / 100; // additional 25%

  liquidationStage[currencyCode] = 3;

  }

  // Stage 4: 100% total (if issuer freeze OR RR < 1.02)

  else if ((isIssuerFrozen(currencyCode) || rr < 1.02e18) &&
  liquidationStage[currencyCode] < 4) {

  sellAmount = totalValue - getRemainingValue(currencyCode);

  liquidationStage[currencyCode] = 4;

  }

  // Execute the sell if an amount is due, gated by the 5% rule
  (§11.3.3)

  if (sellAmount > 0 && isPriceStable(currencyCode)) {

  uint256 amountInTokens = (sellAmount * 1e18) / getPrice(token);

  sellAsset(token, amountInTokens);

  stageStartPrices[currencyCode] = getPrice(token);

  emit LiquidationExecuted(currencyCode, sellAmount,
  liquidationStage[currencyCode]);

  }

  }

  /// @dev The 5% rule (BP §11.3.3): the next stage is paused when the
  price

  /// has dropped more than 5% from the start of the current stage, and

  /// resumes only after 24 hours without further deterioration.

  function isPriceStable(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view returns
  (bool) {

  uint256 currentPrice = getPrice(getAssetForCurrency(currencyCode));

  uint256 stageStartPrice = stageStartPrices[currencyCode];

  if (stageStartPrice == 0) return true; // no stage executed yet

  if (currentPrice >= stageStartPrice) return true;

  uint256 drop = (stageStartPrice - currentPrice) * 1e18 /
  stageStartPrice;

  return drop < 0.05e18; // 5% drop tolerance

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 8. REINTEGRATION (BP §11.4.4)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Evaluates the reintegration score and re-enters the asset if

  /// conditions are met.

  function evaluateReintegration(bytes3 currencyCode) external {

  require(liquidationStage[currencyCode] > 0, "Asset not ejected");

  // 1. Compute score components (each scaled 0..1e18; §21.5.4)

  uint256 timeInBand = getTimeInBandScore(currencyCode);

  uint256 liquidityDepth = getLiquidityDepthScore(currencyCode);

  uint256 oracleAgreement = getOracleAgreementScore(currencyCode);

  uint256 volatility = getVolatilityScore(currencyCode);

  // 2. Compute the weighted score

  uint256 score = (

  WEIGHT_TIME_IN_BAND * timeInBand / 1e18

  + WEIGHT_LIQUIDITY * liquidityDepth / 1e18

  + WEIGHT_ORACLE_AGREEMENT * oracleAgreement / 1e18

  + WEIGHT_VOLATILITY * volatility / 1e18

  );

  // 3. Check whether the score meets the threshold

  if (score < REINTEGRATION_THRESHOLD) {

  emit ReintegrationScore(currencyCode, score, false);

  return;

  }

  // 4. Determine the buy amount based on staged re-entry (§21.5.4)

  uint256 totalValue = getOriginalAssetValue(currencyCode);

  uint256 buyAmount = 0;

  if (score >= 0.90e18 && reintegrationStage[currencyCode] < 3) {

  buyAmount = totalValue - getCurrentAssetValue(currencyCode);

  reintegrationStage[currencyCode] = 3;

  } else if (score >= 0.85e18 && reintegrationStage[currencyCode] < 2) {

  buyAmount = totalValue * 50 / 100;

  reintegrationStage[currencyCode] = 2;

  } else if (reintegrationStage[currencyCode] < 1) {

  buyAmount = totalValue * 25 / 100;

  reintegrationStage[currencyCode] = 1;

  }

  // 5. Execute the buy if an amount is due

  if (buyAmount > 0) {

  address token = getAssetForCurrency(currencyCode);

  uint256 amountInTokens = (buyAmount * 1e18) / getPrice(token);

  buyAsset(token, amountInTokens);

  emit ReintegrationExecuted(currencyCode, buyAmount,
  reintegrationStage[currencyCode]);

  }

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 9. ISSUER FREEZE AND SANCTIONS (BP §11.5.2)

  // ============================================================

  /// @dev Immediately liquidates an asset (100%) when freeze or
  sanctions

  /// are confirmed. Bypasses the staged ladder entirely.

  function emergencyLiquidate(bytes3 currencyCode) external
  onlyEmergencyCouncil {

  address token = getAssetForCurrency(currencyCode);

  uint256 balance = IERC20(token).balanceOf(address(this));

  if (balance > 0) {

  sellAsset(token, balance);

  emit EmergencyLiquidation(currencyCode, balance);

  }

  // Remove the asset from the registry; re-admission runs through the

  // full admission process (Chapter 15), not the reintegration score

  removeAsset(currencyCode);

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 10. GOVERNANCE ENTRY POINTS (dispatched from Listing 14)

  // ============================================================

  // Parameter identifiers (registered in the parameter registry, §22.4)

  bytes32 public constant PARAM_RR_TARGET = keccak256("RR_TARGET");

  bytes32 public constant PARAM_RR_STRESS_FLOOR =
  keccak256("RR_STRESS_FLOOR");

  bytes32 public constant PARAM_MINT_FEE = keccak256("MINT_FEE");

  bytes32 public constant PARAM_REDEEM_FEE_NORMAL =
  keccak256("REDEEM_FEE_NORMAL");

  bytes32 public constant PARAM_REDEEM_FEE_STRESS =
  keccak256("REDEEM_FEE_STRESS");

  bytes32 public constant PARAM_REDEEM_FEE_DEFENSIVE =
  keccak256("REDEEM_FEE_DEFENSIVE");

  bytes32 public constant PARAM_LCR_TARGET = keccak256("LCR_TARGET");

  bytes32 public constant PARAM_DEPEG_WINDOW =
  keccak256("DEPEG_WINDOW");

  /// @dev Applies a registry-executed parameter update. Role checks,

  /// timelocks and envelope bounds are enforced by the governance

  /// contract (Listing 14, §22.6); this dispatcher only writes values.

  function applyParameter(bytes32 parameterId, uint256 newValue)
  external onlyGovernance {

  if (parameterId == PARAM_RR_TARGET) {

  rrTarget = newValue;

  } else if (parameterId == PARAM_RR_STRESS_FLOOR) {

  rrStressFloor = newValue;

  } else if (parameterId == PARAM_MINT_FEE) {

  mintFee = newValue;

  } else if (parameterId == PARAM_REDEEM_FEE_NORMAL) {

  redeemFeeNormal = newValue;

  } else if (parameterId == PARAM_REDEEM_FEE_STRESS) {

  redeemFeeStress = newValue;

  } else if (parameterId == PARAM_REDEEM_FEE_DEFENSIVE) {

  redeemFeeDefensive = newValue;

  } else if (parameterId == PARAM_LCR_TARGET) {

  lcrTarget = newValue;

  } else if (parameterId == PARAM_DEPEG_WINDOW) {

  depegWindow = newValue;

  } else {

  revert("Unknown parameter");

  }

  }

  /// @dev Peg bands are per-currency Risk-Council parameters (§21.5.2).

  function setPegBand(bytes3 currencyCode, uint256 lower, uint256 upper)
  external onlyGovernance {

  require(lower < upper, "Invalid band");

  pegLowerBand[currencyCode] = lower;

  pegUpperBand[currencyCode] = upper;

  }

  // ---- Discretionary pause/resume entry points (BP §14.6.5 bodies).

  // Authorization is enforced by the governance contract (§22.6);

  // this contract restricts callers to the governance registry. ----

  function pauseMinting() external onlyGovernance { _pauseMinting(); }

  function pauseRedeeming() external onlyGovernance { _pauseRedeeming();
  }

  function pauseRebalancing() external onlyGovernance {
  _pauseRebalancing(); }

  function forceRebalance() external onlyGovernance { _forceRebalance();
  }

  /// @dev Resumes operations (Emergency Council action, executed
  through

  /// the governance contract). Blocked while in EMERGENCY.

  function resumeOperations() external onlyGovernance {

  require(currentState != ProtocolState.EMERGENCY, "Cannot resume in
  EMERGENCY");

  _resumeMinting();

  _resumeRedeeming();

  _resumeRebalancing();

  }

  // ============================================================

  // 11. ABSTRACT HOOKS AND EXTERNAL INTEGRATIONS

  // ============================================================

  // ---- Operation hooks (mint/redeem contracts, Chapter 19; MARP,
  §11.11) ----

  function _pauseMinting() internal virtual;

  function _resumeMinting() internal virtual;

  function _setMintThrottle(uint256 percent) internal virtual;

  function _pauseRedeeming() internal virtual;

  function _resumeRedeeming() internal virtual;

  function _pauseRebalancing() internal virtual;

  function _resumeRebalancing() internal virtual;

  function _setRedemptionFee(uint256 fee) internal virtual;

  function _setRebalanceUrgency(uint256 level) internal virtual;

  function _forceRebalance() internal virtual;

  // ---- Data hooks (reserve valuation, Chapter 14; oracle layer,

  // Chapter 17; asset registry, Chapter 15; execution, §11.11) ----

  function getReserveRatio() internal view virtual returns (uint256);

  function getLCR() internal view virtual returns (uint256);

  function getAssetForCurrency(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view
  virtual returns (address);

  function getPrice(address token) internal view virtual returns
  (uint256);

  function getAssetValue(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view virtual
  returns (uint256);

  function getRemainingValue(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view virtual
  returns (uint256);

  function getOriginalAssetValue(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view
  virtual returns (uint256);

  function getCurrentAssetValue(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view
  virtual returns (uint256);

  function isIssuerFrozen(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view virtual
  returns (bool);

  function sellAsset(address token, uint256 amountInTokens) internal
  virtual;

  function buyAsset(address token, uint256 amountInTokens) internal
  virtual;

  function removeAsset(bytes3 currencyCode) internal virtual;

  // ---- Reintegration score components (query oracle history and DEX

  // volume data; BP §11 developer action) ----

  function getTimeInBandScore(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view virtual
  returns (uint256);

  function getLiquidityDepthScore(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view
  virtual returns (uint256);

  function getOracleAgreementScore(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view
  virtual returns (uint256);

  function getVolatilityScore(bytes3 currencyCode) internal view virtual
  returns (uint256);

  }

  interface IERC20 {

  function balanceOf(address account) external view returns (uint256);

  }

21.6.1 Event Emissions

Every state transition, applied action and eject event is emitted
on-chain (BP §14.7 and §11.6.2). Together the events form the
machine-readable crisis narrative consumed by the transparency layer
(Chapter 24) and the honest-status report (Section 25):

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Event                                 Emitted When
  ------------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  StateTransition(oldState, newState,   Every state change; the immediate
  immediate, timestamp)                 flag distinguishes stress
                                        transitions from 48-hour-confirmed
                                        recoveries.

  MintingPaused(timestamp) /            Minting suspension and resumption;
  MintingResumed(timestamp,             the throttle records the state's
  throttlePercent)                      capacity (100 / 50 / 25).

  RedeemingPaused(timestamp) /          Redemption suspension and
  RedeemingResumed(timestamp)           resumption.

  RebalancingPaused(timestamp) /        Rebalancing suspension and
  RebalancingResumed(timestamp)         resumption.

  RedemptionFeeUpdated(newFee,          Fee-schedule change applied on a
  timestamp)                            state transition.

  OperationsResumed(timestamp)          Full operational resume after
                                        confirmed recovery.

  DepegDetected(currencyCode, price,    First confirmation that an asset
  timestamp)                            exceeded its de-peg detection
                                        window outside the band.

  LiquidationExecuted(currencyCode,     Each executed liquidation tranche,
  amount, stage)                        with its stage 1–4.

  ReintegrationScore(currencyCode,      Every reintegration evaluation,
  score, thresholdMet)                  whether or not the threshold is
                                        met.

  ReintegrationExecuted(currencyCode,   Each re-entry tranche, with its
  amount, stage)                        stage 1–3.

  EmergencyLiquidation(currencyCode,    Immediate 100% liquidation on
  amount)                               issuer freeze or sanctions.

  ParameterProposed / ParameterExecuted Governance registry updates
  / ParameterVetoed (parameterId,       (emitted by Listing 14, §22.6).
  value, timestamp)                     
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The parameter events are emitted by the governance registry of Section
  22.6; all other events are emitted by the operations contract of
  Listing 13. Rejected or gated liquidation attempts leave the
  liquidation stage unchanged and are observable from the absence of a
  LiquidationExecuted event after a DepegDetected event — the
  transparency layer treats that gap as an open incident.

the state machine and the eject module, with their governance classes
(BP §14.8 and §11.7). The full cross-chapter authority matrix —
including the weighting, reserve and oracle parameters — is carried in

One discrepancy is carried honestly rather than silently resolved: the
source tables class the 48-hour recovery confirmation period as a
Monetary (DAO) parameter, while the reference implementation declares it
as an immutable constant. This edition retains the immutable constant as
the conservative reading — a stricter governance class than the table
assigns — and flags the mismatch for resolution during deployment
registration (Section 22.4).

  Fidelity note: Listing 13 adapts BP §14.6.1–§14.6.3, the §14.7 events
  and the §11.6 eject module. The v1.2 detector combined timer
  bookkeeping inside a view function (which cannot write state); it is
  cleaned here into a stateful updateDepegStatus plus a pure isDepegged
  view. The restrictiveness ordering (isMoreRestrictive) was left to the
  developer in v1.2 and is supplied here, with RECOVERY ranked between
  CAUTION and STRESS to match its action profile. The 5% pause rule is
  wired into the stage gate — v1.2 defined isPriceStable but never
  called it — and stage-start prices are recorded after each executed
  stage; the 24-hour stabilization hold is enforced by the keeper
  re-evaluation cadence. The de-peg detection window is promoted from a
  hard-coded 12 hours to a registry-governed parameter (default 12
  hours, Risk Council), while the 24- and 48-hour stage durations remain
  fixed as in the source. Fixed-point arithmetic in the reintegration
  score is cleaned to use the declared constant weights, and peg bands
  are set through the governance entry point rather than hard-coded. All
  numeric values — states, thresholds, fees, ladder percentages, score
  weights and timelocks — are carried unchanged from BP §14 and BP §11.

22. Governance

22.1 Constitutional Separation of the Algorithmic Layers

  The architecture has three independent mathematical layers, and their
  independence is constitutional: each layer is governed, validated and
  audited separately, so that no single algorithm can silently control
  the system [MS §90].

  This prevents one algorithm from silently controlling everything.

Layer 1 is the Constituency Engine, and it answers one question: who is
eligible? (Chapter 4). Its domain is the candidate universe — which
currencies and gold qualify for the active basket — decided by the
eligibility score Q_i with entry and exit hysteresis. Its boundary is
equally narrow: it may never assign a weight, execute a trade or hold an
asset; it only admits and retires members. Layer 2 is MASE, the
Multi-model Adaptive Stability Engine, and it answers the question: how
much weight? (Chapters 5–7). Its domain is the target weight vector for
the eligible members, produced by the ensemble under the composite
objective. Its boundary: it weighs only what Layer 1 has admitted, and
it never executes anything — its output is a target, not a trade. Layer
3 is the MARP / Reserve Engine, and it answers the question: when and
how do we execute? (Chapters 10–16). Its domain is execution decisions
and reserve management. Its boundary: it moves toward Layer 2's targets,
within Layer 1's membership, under the reserve rules — it never
redefines the target or the membership. The summary table of Section 2.2
carries the same three layers with their primary chapters.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Layer             Question          Primary Output    Governed and
                                                        Audited Via
  ----------------- ----------------- ----------------- -----------------
  Layer 1 —         Who is eligible?  The active basket Eligibility
  Constituency                        membership        methodology and
  Engine                                                thresholds (Risk
                                                        class, §22.4);
                                                        audited by
                                                        re-running the
                                                        published scoring
                                                        (Chapter 4).

  Layer 2 — MASE    How much weight?  The target weight Fixed published
                                      vector W^{Target} methodology
                                                        (Invariant I3)
                                                        and
                                                        constitutional
                                                        envelopes;
                                                        audited by
                                                        deterministic
                                                        reproduction
                                                        (§7.7, Chapter
                                                        24).

  Layer 3 — MARP /  When and how do   Execution         Execution and
  Reserve Engine    we execute?       decisions and the reserve
                                      reserve portfolio parameters (Risk
                                                        class); audited
                                                        via decision logs
                                                        and reserve
                                                        reporting
                                                        (Chapters 10–16,
                                                        Chapter 24).
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Independent governance means each layer has its own parameter classes in
the parameter registry (Section 22.4), its own version stream (Section
24.2) and its own validation tests in the research program (Chapter 23).
Independent audit means each layer's outputs are reproducible from its
own published inputs — an auditor can attribute any change in the live
weight vector to exactly one layer (Section 2.2). The separation is also
the containment strategy: a faulty optimizer cannot admit a broken
currency, because Layer 1's eligibility gate stands in front of it; a
faulty executor cannot chase a bad target, because MARP's justification
gates (Chapter 10) stand between target and trade; and a compromised
data feed cannot silently reweight the basket, because the oracle layer
(Chapter 17) and the registry's envelope checks (Section 7.7) bound what
any single input can do.

22.2 Governance Separation

Governance in MTQ Σ approves rules, never outcomes [MS §91]. The
governance bodies may approve six categories of change:

-   Methodology — the published, versioned methodology that defines how
    weights are computed (Invariant I3); methodology changes are
    constitutional and follow the 90-day process.

-   Parameter envelopes — the admissible bounds within which tunable
    parameters may move (Section 22.4); the envelope itself is
    constitutional, while the value inside it follows the layer's own
    path.

-   Eligible data providers — which institutional and oracle sources may
    feed the engines (Chapters 4 and 17).

-   Eligible reserve instruments — which tokens and instruments may back
    the unit (Chapter 15).

-   Risk thresholds — the solvency, liquidity and eject thresholds of
    Chapter 21.

-   Emergency rules — the emergency action set and its authorization
    (Sections 22.5–22.6).

What no governance body may do — on any layer, under any quorum, in any
state — is manually set an individual live weight on a discretionary
basis [MS §91]:

Governance → {rules} Governance not→ W_(USD,t), W_(Gold,t)

This is Invariant I8 (Section 2.6): no governance body may set
individual live weights by discretion. The prohibition is structural,
not merely legalistic. Live weights enter the system through exactly one
door — the MASE submission path of Section 7.7 — which verifies the data
version, the methodology hash, the constitutional envelopes, the
velocity limits and the smoothing rule before a vector can become live.
To set a weight by hand, a governance body would have to counterfeit an
entire data-and-methodology pipeline, in public, against a contract that
checks every property it can verify on-chain. Discretionary weights
would also destroy the two properties the unit is built on:
reproducibility (Section 24.1 — an auditor could no longer re-derive the
live vector from published inputs) and the fixed-methodology promise
itself (Invariant I3).

The boundary has a precise shape: governance can change the rules of the
game — the methodology, the envelopes, the thresholds — but never the
score of a particular game. A Risk Council that dislikes the live CHF
weight may tighten the CHF envelope (a constitutional action),
commission a methodology revision (also constitutional), or wait for the
data to move; it may not type a number into the registry and make it
live. This is what distinguishes an algorithmic monetary architecture
from a managed one, and it is the reason Invariants I7 and I8 point at
Sections 22.3 and 22.4 as their enforcement.

22.3 The Four Governance Layers

All protocol authority is partitioned into four layers, ordered by how
fundamental the change is and how much consensus it requires [BP
§14.4.1]. The deeper the layer, the more signatures and the more time a
change demands:

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Layer            Scope               Authority      Timelock       Quorum
  ---------------- ------------------- -------------- -------------- --------------
  Constitutional   Immutable           7/7 Multi-Sig  90 days        7/7
                   parameters (core                                  
                   architecture,                                     
                   constitutional                                    
                   envelopes, hard                                   
                   floors, liquidation                               
                   staging; the v1.2                                 
                   GFB-quantity                                      
                   immutability is                                   
                   superseded by the                                 
                   fixed-methodology                                 
                   invariant I3)                                     

  Monetary         RR target, fee      DAO Vote       48 hours       51%
                   structure,                                        
                   smoothing                                         
                   parameters                                        

  Risk             Haircuts,           Risk Council   24 hours       4/7
                   thresholds, eject   (4/7)                         
                   parameters, LCR                                   
                   targets                                           

  Emergency        Pause operations,   Emergency      Instant        4/7
                   force rebalance,    Council (4/7)                 
                   emergency eject                                   
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Reading the table: Constitutional authority (a 7/7 Multi-Sig with a
90-day timelock) covers the immutable core — the architecture, the
constitutional envelopes, the hard floors, the fixed methodology and the
liquidation ladder's shape. Monetary authority (DAO vote, 51% quorum,
48-hour timelock) covers the monetary-policy parameters: the RR targets,
the fee structure and the smoothing parameters. Risk authority (Risk
Council, 4/7 with a 24-hour timelock) covers the technical risk
parameters: haircuts, thresholds, eject parameters and LCR targets.
Emergency authority (Emergency Council, 4/7, instant) covers only the
pause/eject/rebalance action set of Section 22.5 — the one layer that
trades deliberation for speed, and pays for it with the narrowest scope
in the system.

The layering implements Invariant I7 (Section 2.6): the protocol is
governed by a layered hierarchy, and no layer can reach into another's
domain. The timelocks themselves are constitutional (Section 22.4): the
Emergency Council cannot shorten the monetary timelock, and the DAO
cannot raise a constitutional change through a 51% vote. The councils
are distinct bodies with distinct keys — the Risk Council and the
Emergency Council operate separate 4/7 Multi-Sigs, both with hardware
wallet requirements, so that compromising one signers set does not yield
both authorities. Every action on every layer emits events and is
published (Chapter 24); the layer boundaries are exactly what Invariants
I7 and I8 reference from Section 2.6.

22.4 The Parameter Registry and Authority Matrix

Every tunable parameter in the protocol is registered — with its
identifier, its governance layer, its current value and its admissible
envelope — in a single on-chain parameter registry [BP §14.4.2]. The
registry is the normative answer to the question of who may change what:
an update path exists if and only if the parameter is registered, and
the layer of the record determines which body, which quorum and which
timelock govern the change. Unregistered parameters fail closed — the
governance contract of Listing 14 reverts on any update to an unknown
parameter identifier — so new parameters must be deliberately registered
(a constitutional action) before they can ever be touched.

-   Registration declares the parameter identifier, its layer
    (Constitutional, Monetary, Risk or Emergency), its initial value and
    its envelope [min, max]; registration and envelope changes follow
    the constitutional process with the 90-day timelock.

-   Envelope discipline: proposed values must lie inside the registered
    envelope at proposal time, and are re-checked at execution time and
    again on arrival at the operations contract — three independent
    checks, so a stale or manipulated proposal cannot bypass the bounds.

-   Timelock discipline: monetary changes execute only after 48 hours,
    risk changes only after 24 hours, and the Risk Council may veto a
    monetary proposal inside its timelock window (Section 22.6).

-   Audit trail: every registration, proposal, execution and veto emits
    an event and is versioned with the data and methodology versions
    (Section 24.2), so the complete parameter history is reconstructable
    after the fact.

The consolidated authority matrix below merges the registry table of BP
§14.4.2 with the constants tables of the preceding chapters; it is the
single normative reference for parameter governance across the protocol:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
