Gate 2 deserves emphasis because the adaptive architecture makes it
heavier than in v1.2: the independent firm must validate not only the
reserve model and the index but the weighting and execution engines as
well — the rebuilt backtests, walk-forward results, perturbation studies
and robustness scores of Chapter 23 are the internal preparation for
that engagement, not a substitute for it. The on-chain counterpart of
this table is implemented in Section 25.7: the gate list, the per-gate
pass state and the production-authorization flag are published as
contract state, so the path to production is queryable by anyone at any
time. The source blueprint repeats this gate table verbatim in its final
section (BP §16.3); that duplicate is not reproduced again in Chapter 26
— Section 25.5 is the authoritative statement.

25.6 The Honest Messaging Guide

All external communications — documentation, website, exchange listings,
social channels and investor materials — must be reviewed against this
guide before publication. The guide operationalizes the core principle
of Section 25.1: each approved sentence is either supported by the
specification (Section 25.2) or is a status statement consistent with
Section 25.4; each forbidden sentence is an unsupported claim (Section
25.3). A v2.0 row is added to both lists for composition claims, which
are the most likely misstatement of an adaptive basket [BP §15.6].

25.6.1 What to Say (Supported Claims)

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Context                             Correct Statement
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  Elevator Pitch                      "MTQ Σ is a non-USD,
                                      multi-currency-and-gold reference
                                      unit backed by an
                                      over-collateralized reserve of
                                      stablecoins and tokenized gold,
                                      with composition set by a
                                      transparent adaptive methodology."

                                      purchasing power, independent of
                                      any single fiat currency."

                                      testing. It is not yet
                                      production-authorized."

  Sharia Status                       "The protocol is designed for
                                      Sharia review. Independent
                                      certification is pending."

  Security                            "The protocol uses a multi-source
                                      oracle architecture with timestamp,
                                      confidence, and deviation checks."

  Composition (v2.0)                  "The published percentages are the
                                      starting prior; the live weights
                                      are computed by the published
                                      adaptive methodology and change
                                      with conditions."
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

  Review rule: if a proposed statement cannot be traced to Section 25.2
  or Section 25.6.1, it does not pass review. Statements about future
  validation results are permitted only in the conditional voice ("will
  be validated in production"), never in the assertive voice.

25.7 Smart Contract Implementation — Honest Status

The declarations of this chapter are enforced on-chain by a dedicated
contract implementing invariant I10 (Section 2.6): the honest status
string, the validation-gate list and the production-authorization flag
are public state, callable by anyone at any time. The design extends the
getHonestStatus() pattern introduced in Listing 1 (Section 2.7): the
status string is updated to state the adaptive-methodology rule
explicitly — live weights are validation-stage values produced by a
published, reproducible methodology, and the four weight states of
Section 2.3 are distinct — so that a query of the contract refutes both
v1.2-era fixed-weight misreadings and performance-marketing claims in
one call. Gate state is mutable only by the Constitutional Council, with
an evidence hash committing each gate to its published audit or
validation report [BP §15.7].

Listing 15 — Honest status declaration (adapts BP §15.7)

  // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT

  pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

  /// @title MTQSigmaHonestStatus — on-chain honesty architecture
  (Listing 15)

  /// @notice Implements constitutional invariant I10: the protocol must
  be

  /// honest about its status, and validation-gate status is published

  /// on-chain (Section 2.6). All read functions are public.

  contract MTQSigmaHonestStatus {

  // ---- Governance (Chapter 22) ----

  address public constitutionalCouncil; // 7/7 Multi-Sig — final gate
  authority

  address public riskCouncil; // 4/7 Multi-Sig — evidence attestation

  // ---- The 11 validation gates (Section 25.5) ----

  string[11] public validationGates = [

  "Smart Contract Audit",

  "Independent Model Validation",

  "Sharia Certification",

  "Legal Opinion",

  "Public Testnet Deployment",

  "Penetration Testing",

  "Institutional Review",

  "Liquidity Bootstrapping",

  "Governance Launch",

  "Community Stress Test",

  "Mainnet Deployment Approval"

  ];

  bool[11] public gatePassed;

  uint256[11] public gatePassedAt;

  bytes32[11] public gateEvidenceHash;

  // ---- Events ----

  event GateMarkedPassed(uint256 indexed gateId, string gate, bytes32
  evidenceHash, uint256 timestamp);

  event GateRevoked(uint256 indexed gateId, string reason, uint256
  timestamp);

  modifier onlyConstitutionalCouncil() {

  require(msg.sender == constitutionalCouncil, "Only Constitutional
  Council");

  _;

  }

  /// @notice Returns the honest status of the protocol
  (testnet-specific).

  /// @dev Public and callable by anyone. Extends the getHonestStatus()

  /// pattern of Listing 1 (Section 2.7) for the adaptive v2.0

  /// architecture: it states the methodology rule, the simulated

  /// testnet environment, and the four-state weight distinction.

  function getHonestStatus() public pure returns (string memory) {

  return "MTQ Sigma v2.0-testnet — ADAPTIVE METHODOLOGY, NOT FIXED
  WEIGHTS. "

  "DEPLOYED ON ARC / MONAD / SOLANA DEVNET. NOT PRODUCTION-AUTHORIZED. "

  "All oracles and assets are SIMULATED for testing purposes. Live "

  "weights are validation-stage values computed by the published "

  "MASE/MARP methodology and reproducible from published data. The "

  "strategic prior is a starting anchor, not the live composition: "

  "W-Prior, W-Target, W-Smooth and W-Execution are four distinct "

  "states. No percentage is a final optimal weight until all 11 "

  "validation gates have passed.";

  }

  /// @notice Returns the list of validation gates that must be passed

  /// before mainnet deployment can be considered (Section 25.5).

  function getValidationGates() public view returns (string[11] memory)
  {

  return validationGates;

  }

  /// @notice Returns a boolean indicating whether all validation gates

  /// have been passed.

  /// @dev As of v2.0-testnet this always returns false, because no gate

  /// has been attested. Authorization derives from gate state, not

  /// from a hard-coded constant, so it degrades honestly if a gate

  /// is revoked.

  function isProductionAuthorized() public view returns (bool) {

  for (uint256 i = 0; i < 11; i++) {

  if (!gatePassed[i]) return false;

  }

  return true;

  }

  /// @notice Returns the status of one validation gate.

  function getGateStatus(uint256 gateId)

  public view returns (bool passed, uint256 passedAt, bytes32
  evidenceHash)

  {

  require(gateId < 11, "Invalid gate");

  return (gatePassed[gateId], gatePassedAt[gateId],
  gateEvidenceHash[gateId]);

  }

  /// @notice Marks a validation gate as passed with an evidence
  commitment.

  /// @dev Only the Constitutional Council (7/7) may attest a gate. The

  /// evidence hash commits to the audit or validation report, so the

  /// on-chain status is checkable against the published document

  /// (Chapter 24 publication layer).

  function markGatePassed(uint256 gateId, bytes32 evidenceHash)

  external onlyConstitutionalCouncil

  {

  require(gateId < 11, "Invalid gate");

  require(!gatePassed[gateId], "Gate already passed");

  gatePassed[gateId] = true;

  gatePassedAt[gateId] = block.timestamp;

  gateEvidenceHash[gateId] = evidenceHash;

  emit GateMarkedPassed(gateId, validationGates[gateId], evidenceHash,
  block.timestamp);

  }

  /// @notice Revokes a gate if its evidence is later invalidated.

  /// @dev Revocation re-opens the path to production automatically,

  /// because isProductionAuthorized() recomputes from gate state.

  function revokeGate(uint256 gateId, string calldata reason)

  external onlyConstitutionalCouncil

  {

  require(gateId < 11, "Invalid gate");

  require(gatePassed[gateId], "Gate not passed");

  gatePassed[gateId] = false;

  gatePassedAt[gateId] = 0;

  gateEvidenceHash[gateId] = bytes32(0);

  emit GateRevoked(gateId, reason, block.timestamp);

  }

  /// @notice One-call summary for integrators and dashboards.

  function getStatusSummary()

  external view returns (string memory status, bool authorized)

  {

  return (getHonestStatus(), isProductionAuthorized());

  }

  }

  Fidelity note: the three functions of BP §15.7 — getHonestStatus(),
  getValidationGates() and isProductionAuthorized() — are carried
  substantially intact; the gate-state machinery (markGatePassed,
  revokeGate, getGateStatus) is added so that invariant I10 is enforced
  rather than merely asserted, and isProductionAuthorized() derives from
  gate state while returning false in exactly the states the blueprint
  hard-coded. The status string extends the Listing 1 pattern (Section
  2.7) to state the adaptive-methodology rule and the four-state
  distinction. The 11-gate array is unchanged from BP §15.5/§16.3.

26. Final Declarations and Architecture Summary

26.1 The Final Declaration

  MTQ Σ v2.0 is a closed-loop monetary architecture: every obligation
  the unit creates is defined, measured, collateralized, executed,
  verified and published inside one constitutionally governed loop [BP
  §16.1].

The declaration below restates the whole architecture in one place. It
is carried from the blueprint's final declaration with the v1.2
fixed-quantity references replaced by methodology references, because
under invariant I3 the reference value is defined by a fixed, published,
auditable methodology — not by fixed weights:

-   The adaptive reference index — a methodology-defined, chain-linked
    basket of eligible currencies and gold — defines the reference value
    (Chapters 3 and 9).

-   The Reserve NAV collateralizes the token (Chapter 14).

-   Minting is priced against the reference index, breaking the 1:1 USD
    peg (Chapter 19).

-   Redemption is priced against NAV and releases the full proportional
    basket (Chapter 19).

-   The weighting layer (MASE) computes live targets through a
    constrained, robust, multi-model ensemble (Chapters 5–7).

-   The execution layer (MARP) uses dynamic cost-benefit optimization
    with liquidity-aware sizing (Chapters 10–11).

-   The Buffer has three distinct layers (BASE, STRESS, EMERGENCY) with
    smooth ramping (Chapter 16).

-   The Oracle Architecture uses three independent sources with
    timestamp, confidence and deviation checks (Chapter 17).

-   The Geopolitical Eject system uses staged liquidation (10% → 25% →
    50% → 100%) with a reintegration score (Chapter 21).

-   The Risk State Machine has six states (NORMAL, CAUTION, STRESS,
    DEFENSIVE, EMERGENCY, RECOVERY) with deterministic transitions
    (Chapter 18).

-   All 76 points of the independent monetary audit have been reconciled
    in the v1.2 lineage, and the v2.0 modification program closes the
    architectural gaps identified in the COO review (Section 26.6.3).

This is not yet production-authorized. It is a candidate for testnet
validation.

The reference methodology defines what one MTQ Σ is intended to
represent. The reserve portfolio exists to collateralize that
obligation.

The last sentence is the v2.0 form of the blueprint's closing statement:
where v1.2 said the GFB Index defines the obligation, v2.0 says the
methodology defines it. The obligation is unchanged — one unit of global
purchasing-power-representative value, fully collateralized — but its
definition is now an auditable process rather than a static quantity
table, which is precisely what makes the unit adaptive without making it
discretionary.

26.2 The Critical Distinction — Four Weight States

The single most load-bearing distinction of the entire architecture
receives a final restatement here; the full treatment, including the
producing subsystem for each state, is Section 2.3 [MS §97]. The four
states are not equivalent:

W^(Prior) ≠ W^(Target) ≠ W^(Smooth) ≠ W^(Execution)

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  State                               One-Line Definition
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  Strategic Prior (W^{Prior})         Long-term starting preference — a
                                      soft stabilizing anchor, never a
                                      target to be hit.

  Target (W^{Target})                 Current mathematical optimum,
                                      before constitutional constraints
                                      are applied.

  Smoothed (W^{Smooth})               Risk-controlled transition toward
                                      the constrained target, paced by
                                      the stress-adaptive smoothing rule.

  Execution (W^{Execution})           Actual trade decision after cost,
                                      liquidity and reserve testing by
                                      MARP.
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

This distinction eliminates the danger of accidentally creating a
fixed-weight basket. Even if the target happens to equal the prior for
many consecutive days — and in calm regimes it may — the system remains
adaptive, because what is constitutional is the path from data to
decision, not the destination. The COO review identifies this inequality
as the safeguard that makes MTQ Σ dynamic rather than fixed-weight
[COO-28], and it is echoed on-chain by the honest status string of
Listing 15. Every downstream system — index valuation (Chapter 9),
execution (Chapters 10–11), publication (Chapter 24) — consumes exactly
one of these states, and conflating them in any implementation is a
specification defect.

26.3 The Final Conceptual Flow

Two diagrams summarize the complete architecture. They are deliberately
complementary: the first is the data-flow view — what happens to the
data, in order, from global inputs to published audit; the second is the
two-engine hierarchy view — how the Reference Index and the Reserve run
as parallel constitutional systems that merge at execution [MS §98;
COO-28].

26.3.1 The End-to-End Data-Flow View

  GLOBAL DATA

  │

  ├── Reserve data

  ├── Trade data

  ├── FX data

  ├── Gold data

  ├── Liquidity data

  ├── Inflation data

  └── Stress data

  │

  ▼

  CONSTITUENCY ENGINE

  ▼

  │

  ▼

  AUDIT / PUBLISH

The pipeline is strictly one-directional: data determines eligibility,
eligibility bounds the optimizer, the optimizer proposes, constraints
dispose, smoothing paces, MARP decides, and verification plus
publication close the loop. The complete live-weight equation that
formalizes this pipeline — including the projection and smoothing
operators — is specified in Chapter 24, where deterministic
reproducibility makes every arrow auditable.

26.3.2 The Two-Engine Hierarchy View (COO-Approved Final Architecture)

The COO-approved merged architecture shows the same pipeline organized
as two parallel engines under one unit, merging at MARP and separating
again only at the audit layer [COO-28]:

  MTQ Σ

  │

  ┌───────────────────┴───────────────────────┐

  │ │

  REFERENCE INDEX RESERVE

  │ │

  CONSTITUENCY ENGINE RESERVE ENGINE

  │ │

  ELIGIBLE UNIVERSE Coverage / Liquidity

  │ Custody / Gold

  ▼ │

  MASE │

  │ │

  ├── Institutional │

  ├── Stability │

  └── Purchasing Power │

  │ │

  ▼ │

  ROBUST ENSEMBLE │

  │ │

  CONSTRAINT PROJECTION │

  │ │

  DYNAMIC BOUNDS │

  │ │

  │ │

  │ │

  │

  MARP

  │

  ┌───────────────────┼───────────────────────┐

  │ │ │

  CASH FLOW NO-TRADE ZONE RISK TRIGGER

  │ │ │

  └───────────────────┼───────────────────────┘

  │

  COST-BENEFIT GATE

  │

  PARTIAL EXECUTION

  │

  VERIFICATION

  │

  AUDIT / PUBLICATION

26.3.3 How the Two Views Relate

The two diagrams are the same architecture seen from two angles. The
data-flow view (Section 26.3.1) reads vertically: it answers "what
happens next to the data" and makes the sequence of mathematical stages
explicit, including the full MASE candidate fan-out (ten model families)
and the five MARP decision questions. The hierarchy view (Section
26.3.2) reads horizontally: it answers "which constitutional system owns
each stage" — the Reference Index column (constituency, MASE, ensemble,
constraints, smoothing, daily target) and the Reserve column (coverage,
liquidity, custody, gold), which run in parallel under the single
identity MTQ Σ and converge only at MARP. The convergence is the
architectural point: the reserve engine's coverage, liquidity, custody
and gold state feed the cash-flow, no-trade-zone and risk-trigger inputs
of MARP, so execution is priced against reserve reality, not against the
index alone. Downstream of MARP the views re-merge completely —
cost-benefit gate, partial execution, verification, audit and
publication are shared obligations of both engines. The COO review
presents the hierarchy view as the final merged architecture precisely
because it shows, in one picture, that index mathematics and reserve
economics are separate until the moment a trade must be justified.

26.4 The Final Strategic Basket Statement

The current starting prior remains [MS §99]:

  W^(StrategicPrior) = [USD 27%, EUR 20%, JPY 9%, GBP 8%, CNY 5%, CHF
  5%, Gold 26%]

but this is NOT the live weighting. The published prior is a research
snapshot — the genesis anchor and the soft reference of the
StrategicDrift penalty (Section 7.1) — and quoting it as the composition
of MTQ Σ is the most common and most serious misstatement of the
protocol (Sections 25.3 and 25.6.2). The live weight vector is:

  W_(t) = [w_(USD,t), w_(EUR,t), w_(JPY,t), w_(GBP,t), w_(CNY,t),
  w_(CHF,t), w_(Gold,t)] with ∑_(i) w_(i,t) = 1

with every component changing according to the MASE/MARP architecture,
and therefore:

w_(Gold,t) ≠ 26% and w_(USD,t) ≠ 27% in general

The same applies to every other component. Only the published W_t series
(Section 24.3) describes the composition of MTQ Σ at any time, and only
for the effective period it covers. When the research program revises
the prior (Chapter 23), the change flows through governance as a new
methodology version and is re-published — the definition of the unit,
which is the methodology, never wavers.

26.5 The COO-Approved Principles

26.5.1 The Rebalancing Principle

  MTQ Σ shall be continuously monitored and mathematically recalculated,
  but shall not be continuously traded. The system shall rebalance only
  when predefined economic, risk, structural, reserve, liquidity or
  emergency conditions justify the expected execution cost. Natural cash
  flows shall be preferred, deviations shall normally be corrected
  partially, and every trade shall be subject to transaction-cost,
  liquidity, turnover and reserve constraints.

This is the operating constitution of the execution layer, and every
mechanism of Chapters 10–13 exists to enforce one of its clauses [MS
§100]. Continuously monitored and recalculated is the daily MASE
computation (Chapter 7); shall not be continuously traded is the
no-trade zone (Chapter 10) and invariant I11; justification of expected
execution cost is the cost-benefit gate (Chapter 11); preference for
natural cash flows is the flow-redirection rule that lets mints and
redptions do the rebalancing work before any external trade (Appendix
C.3); partial correction is the β-coefficient rule (Appendix C.2); and
the final clause binds every trade to transaction-cost, liquidity,
turnover and reserve constraints simultaneously, which is why MARP
consumes four independent gate families rather than a single threshold.
The principle converts a philosophical commitment — stability without
churn — into checkable rules.

26.5.2 The Monetary Principle

  MTQ Σ does not promise a fixed composition. It promises a fixed
  methodology.

This is the correct distinction, and it is the one-sentence answer to
every question of the form "what does MTQ Σ hold?" [MS §101]. The
weights may change. The currencies may change. The gold allocation may
change. The optimization model may adapt. But the rules governing how
those changes occur remain transparent, bounded, auditable and
constitutionally controlled. The distinction matters because a fixed
composition and a fixed methodology fail differently: a fixed
composition fails catastrophically when the world it assumed moves on,
while a fixed methodology fails visibly — a constraint is breached, a
gate rejects a trade, a weight leaves its envelope — and the failure is
correctable through the same published rules. Promising the methodology
is also what makes the promise verifiable: anyone can reproduce the
weights from published data (Chapter 24), whereas no one can verify a
promise about future composition.

26.6 Final Architecture Status — Locked and Not Locked

The final architecture status distinguishes concepts that are locked —
constitutional, part of the definition of the system — from quantities
that are not locked and must be quantitatively validated before they can
be called final [MS §102]. Both lists are carried in full.

26.6.1 Locked Concepts

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  #                                   Locked Concept (constitutional)
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  1                                   MTQ Σ is the project identity.

  2                                   Gold is an explicit
                                      reference-basket component.

  3                                   Gold is not merely a weighting
                                      multiplier.

  4                                   Gold reference allocation and gold
                                      reserve allocation are separate.

  5                                   Currency selection and currency
                                      weighting are separate.

  6                                   USD/EUR/JPY/GBP/CNY/CHF are the
                                      initial core candidate universe.

  7                                   CAD/AUD/SGD and other currencies
                                      remain eligible candidates.

  8                                   Strategic percentages are priors,
                                      not fixed live weights.

  9                                   MASE is the adaptive weighting
                                      engine.

  10                                  Multiple optimizers are combined.

  11                                  Robust covariance is used.

  12                                  CVaR is included.

  13                                  Purchasing-power stability is
                                      included.

  14                                  Regime analysis is included.

  15                                  Hard constraints exist.

  16                                  Strategic priors are soft anchors.

  17                                  Turnover is penalized.

  18                                  Rebalancing is cost-aware.

  19                                  Daily calculation does not mean
                                      daily trading.

  20                                  No-trade zones are used.

  21                                  Bands can adapt.

  22                                  Partial rebalancing is preferred.

  23                                  Natural cash flow is preferred.

  24                                  Reserve and index management are
                                      separate.

  25                                  Multi-source gold valuation is
                                      required.

  26                                  Tokenized gold is an eligible
                                      instrument, not the definition of
                                      gold.

  27                                  No artificial price support.

  28                                  No discretionary buybacks.

  29                                  Crisis controls protect system
                                      integrity rather than market price.

  30                                  Backtests must be rebuilt for MTQ
                                      Σ.

  31                                  Walk-forward and out-of-sample
                                      validation are mandatory.

  32                                  Model robustness matters more than
                                      maximum historical return.

  33                                  Every live weight must be
                                      reproducible from published data
                                      and rules.
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

26.6.2 Not Locked — Must Be Quantitatively Validated

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  #                                   Not Locked — Must Be Quantitatively
                                      Validated
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  1                                   Exact final currency weights.

  2                                   Exact final gold weight.

  3                                   Exact gold corridor.

  4                                   Exact currency corridors.

  5                                   Exact MASE model coefficients.

  6                                   Exact ensemble coefficients.

  7                                   Exact no-trade thresholds.

  8                                   Exact smoothing factor.

  9                                   Exact partial-rebalance
                                      coefficient.

  10                                  Exact turnover penalty.

  11                                  Exact crisis thresholds.

  12                                  Exact reserve gold allocation.

  13                                  Exact reserve liquidity buffer.

  14                                  Exact review frequency where data
                                      quality suggests an alternative.
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

This distinction is deliberate. No numerical weighting percentage should
be described as "the final optimal weight" until it survives the
complete MTQ Σ research program (Chapter 23) and Gate 2 of the
validation sequence (Section 25.5). Every number in this document is
therefore either a constitutional envelope, a validation-stage research
value, or a genesis snapshot — never a final optimum.

26.6.3 The Second Mathematical Layer (COO Final Conclusion)

The COO review's final conclusion frames the status of this document
relative to its predecessor [COO-28]: the consolidated modification
specification was not wrong — it was an excellent consolidated
architectural document — but compared with the final architecture it was
missing a second mathematical layer that must not be omitted before MTQ
Σ is called production-ready. The missing pieces were principally: index
valuation, chain-linking, risk mathematics, adaptive ensemble
mathematics, execution economics, liquidity mathematics, stress
mathematics and audit mathematics.

That second layer is now part of the locked architecture: index
valuation and chain-linking (Chapter 9), risk mathematics (Chapter 6),
adaptive ensemble mathematics (Chapters 5 and 7), execution economics
(Chapters 10–12), liquidity mathematics (Chapter 13), stress mathematics
(Chapters 18 and 23) and audit mathematics (Chapter 24). And one
conceptual point remains absolutely explicit, as the COO review
requires:

W^(Prior) ≠ W^(Target) ≠ W^(Smooth) ≠ W^(Execution)

That is the safeguard that makes MTQ Σ dynamic rather than fixed-weight.
With the second layer locked, the honest status of the architecture is:
conceptually complete and specified end-to-end, quantitatively
unvalidated — which is exactly the state the validation gates of Section
25.5 exist to resolve.

26.7 The Final One-Sentence Definition

The definition of the unit, in one sentence [MS §103]:

  MTQ Σ is a transparent, dynamically weighted, multi-currency-and-gold
  monetary reference architecture in which the live composition is
  continuously calculated by a constrained, robust, multi-model
  stability engine and changed only through cost-aware, risk-aware,
  liquidity-aware rebalancing rules.

Every clause of the sentence is load-bearing and traceable: transparent
(publication layer, Chapter 24); dynamically weighted (invariant I3);
multi-currency-and-gold (Chapters 3–4); continuously calculated (daily
MASE, Chapter 7); constrained, robust, multi-model (Chapters 6–8); and
changed only through cost-aware, risk-aware, liquidity-aware rules
(MARP, Chapters 10–13). If a summary of MTQ Σ ever contradicts this
sentence, the summary is wrong. The sentence is also the boundary of
legitimate paraphrase: shorter descriptions may drop clauses (an
elevator pitch need not mention liquidity-awareness), but no description
may add a clause the architecture does not have — a fixed weight, a peg,
a guarantee — without becoming a misrepresentation. In any conflict
between a derived summary and this definition, the definition governs,
exactly as this document governs its sources (Section 26.8.3).

26.8.3 The Single Source of Truth

This document — MTQ Σ — Master Monetary Architecture & Quantitative
Specification v1.0 — is the single authoritative source for the MTQ Σ
protocol [BP §16.5; COO-27].

-   Supersedes: the Source of Truth Blueprint v1.2 (Final Closed-Loop
    Monetary Architecture, Testnet Edition), the Consolidated Final
    Modification Specification — Baseline v1.0, and the COO review, all
    of which are fully folded into this edition; earlier versions and
    internal working documents are historical only.

-   Authority: this document is the canonical reference for developers,
    auditors, validators and governance bodies. Where any other material
    — including the on-chain honest status strings — disagrees with this
    specification, this specification governs until a governed version
    change.

-   Amendments: any future change must be made through the governance
    process of Chapter 22 and documented as a new version (v1.1, v1.2,
    etc.); the parameter registry and methodology versioning of Chapters
    22 and 24 are the machine-readable counterparts of this rule.

The title itself is a COO decision carried into this edition [COO-27]:
the working title "Consolidated Final Modification Specification" was
correct while the work modified the historical architecture, but the
next master document is titled "Master Monetary Architecture &
Quantitative Specification" because the project is no longer merely
modifying the historical architecture — it is now defining an original
mathematical system. This document is that master document.

The contact field is intentionally a placeholder in this edition:
point-of-contact information is operational metadata, not specification
content, and it is maintained by governance so that it survives
personnel changes. The repository and license rows are carried from the
blueprint so that code provenance and reuse terms travel with the
document [BP §16.6]. With this table the specification closes: 26
chapters, five appendices, one architecture, one methodology, one honest
status — and one next step, which is not a design task but a validation
one: pass the gates of Section 25.5 in order, honestly, and only then
update the declarations this chapter froze in the candidate state.

                          absorb losses before    
                          the core reserve (BASE  
                          first, then STRESS,     
                          then EMERGENCY).        

  Staged liquidation      Eject ladder 10% → 25%  Chapter 21
                          → 50% → 100% that       
                          minimizes market impact 
                          when an asset must be   
                          removed.                

  Reintegration score     Anti-gaming score       Chapter 21
                          (threshold R ≥ 0.80)    
                          gating re-admission of  
                          an ejected asset.       

  Parameter registry      Governed, versioned     §22.3
                          store of all            
                          methodology and risk    
                          parameters; changes     
                          require the appropriate 
                          council.                

  Decision log            Per-trade record        Chapter 24
                          (DecisionID, target     
                          weight, actual weight,  
                          drift, cost, outcome)   
                          published for every     
                          executed or rejected    
                          rebalance.              

  Strategic prior         The starting            §3.2
                          soft-anchor vector      
                          [27/20/9/8/5/5/26];     
                          research data, not a    
                          definition of the unit. 
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Appendix B — Deployment Checklist

B.1 Phase 0 — Environment and Prerequisites

The checklist below compiles the developer deployment sequence from the
blueprint's genesis and deployment modules into five phases [BP §13.7.4;
BP §2.6.4]. It targets the testnet edition; every phase that differs on
mainnet is governed by the validation gates of Section 25.5 and must not
be executed before those gates pass.

-   □ Foundry installed (forge / cast); RPC endpoints configured for
    Arc, Monad and Solana Devnet (Appendix D).

-   □ Deployer key secured; the onlyDeployer modifier protects the
    genesis function [BP §13.7.4].

-   □ Governance modifiers (onlyConstitutionalCouncil, onlyMultiSig)
    implemented via the governance contracts of Chapter 22.

-   □ Mock ERC-20 assets and mock oracle feeds prepared (Appendix D.3);
    native USDC on Arc identified.

-   □ Genesis deposit funded in testnet assets (Chapter 20); sweep
    threshold configured.

-   □ The honest-status contract (Listing 15) compiled and ready to
    deploy alongside the core contracts.

B.2 Phase 1 — Roles and Addresses

Set the governance and operational addresses before any state-changing
deployment step. On testnet the following values apply [BP §13.7.4]:

-   □ dao — monetary-parameter authority (Chapter 22).

-   □ riskCouncil — 4/7 Multi-Sig; approves MASE submitters and risk
    parameters.

-   □ emergencyCouncil — 4/7 Multi-Sig; emergency controls.

-   □ constitutionalCouncil — 7/7 Multi-Sig; envelopes, gates and
    constitutional changes.

-   □ genesisReserveAddress = 0xE71869C662733642bfBb262B8c6bad8B0fBfA7D0
    (4/7 Safe) on Arc and Monad testnet.

-   □ coldTreasury = same as the Genesis Reserve on testnet; a separate
    address on mainnet.

-   □ keeper = deployer or a dedicated bot address (MASE submission,
    sweeps).

  On Solana Devnet the EVM-style genesis reserve address is not
  applicable; use the chain-native equivalent mechanism and record it in
  the deployment documentation [BP §13.7.4].

B.3 Phase 2 — Oracle Configuration

Configure the multi-source oracle layer before genesis so that the first
index valuation already reads validated prices (Chapter 17; addresses in
Appendix D.4) [BP §9.5]:

-   □ Deploy the OracleFeed mapping with Chainlink, Pyth and Chronicle
    addresses per asset pair (EUR/USD, GBP/USD, JPY/USD, CNY/USD (CNH),
    PAXG, VIX, DXY).

-   □ Set stalenessThreshold = 60 seconds; confidence bound < 1%;
    deviation bound < 2.5% (invariant I9).

-   □ Set minPrice / maxPrice sanity bounds per feed.

-   □ Enable the canonical gold price quorum of independent sources
    (Chapter 17).

-   □ On devnet, point every feed at its mock oracle and verify the
    heartbeat.

B.4 Phase 3 — Contract Deployment and Genesis

Execute the genesis sequence exactly as specified; the base-fixing
verification steps are the audit trail for the index denominator [BP
§2.6.4; BP §13.7.4]:

-   □ 1. Ensure verifyGFBBase() is included in the reference-index
    contract.

-   □ 2. Call genesis() with the genesis deposit; confirm the initial
    supply is minted to the Genesis Reserve and circulating supply is
    zero (Chapter 20).

-   □ 3. Call _emitGenesisVerification() at the end of genesis() —
    mandatory on testnets so the event is present in the logs for audit
    testing (Appendix D.2).

-   □ 4. After deployment, call verifyGFBBase() to confirm the
    denominator is consistent with the hard-coded base fixings.

-   □ 5. Record the emitted GenesisVerification event in the deployment
    documentation for the audit trail.

-   □ 6. Confirm the treasury sweep threshold ($10,000) and the Cold
    Treasury destination (Chapter 20).

B.5 Phase 4 — Weight Registry Initialization

Initialize the adaptive weight registry to the genesis snapshot (Section
2.7; Listing 2):

-   □ Set live weights to the strategic prior: [0.27, 0.20, 0.09, 0.08,
    0.05, 0.05, 0.26] × 1e18, summing to exactly 1e18.

-   □ Set methodologyVersion = keccak256 of the v2.0 methodology
    document hash; dataVersion = hash of the genesis data snapshot.

-   □ Load the admissibility envelopes LOWER_BOUND / UPPER_BOUND and the
    velocity limits MAX_VELOCITY as validation-stage values (Section
    8.1, Section 8.3).

-   □ Confirm setEnvelopes() is guarded by onlyConstitutionalCouncil
    with the 90-day timelock.

-   □ Approve the MASE submitter address via the Risk Council; record
    the approval.

B.6 Phase 5 — Post-Deployment Verification

Verify the deployment with the Foundry commands below (environment:
MTQ_CONTRACT, GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS, COLD_TREASURY, RPC) [BP §13.7.4],
then close out:

  # Verify the reference-index base denominator

  cast call $MTQ_CONTRACT "verifyGFBBase()" --rpc-url $RPC

  # Check the Genesis Reserve token balance

  cast call $MTQ_CONTRACT "balanceOf(address)" $GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS
  --rpc-url $RPC

  # Check the operational wallet balance (the contract itself)

  cast balance $MTQ_CONTRACT --rpc-url $RPC

  # Check the Cold Treasury balance

  cast balance $COLD_TREASURY --rpc-url $RPC

  # Honest-status checks (v2.0 additions; Listing 15)

  cast call $STATUS_CONTRACT "getHonestStatus()" --rpc-url $RPC

  cast call $STATUS_CONTRACT "getValidationGates()" --rpc-url $RPC

  cast call $STATUS_CONTRACT "isProductionAuthorized()" --rpc-url $RPC

  # isProductionAuthorized() must return false on every testnet
  deployment.

-   □ Confirm getHonestStatus() returns the adaptive-methodology status
    string (Listing 15).

-   □ Confirm isProductionAuthorized() returns false and all gates read
    as not passed.

-   □ Record all deployment events (GenesisVerification, WeightsAccepted
    or initialization, gate state) in the deployment documentation and
    publish via the Chapter 24 layer.

Appendix C — Worked Examples

C.1 Example 1 — Normal Operating Day (No-Trade Zone)

Scenario. MASE completes its daily calculation and posts a USD target of
28.2%, while the actual USD weight in the basket is 28.5% [MS §68]. The
system is running in a calm regime; the USD no-trade zone half-width is
θ_{USD} = 1.0%.

Math walkthrough. The deviation is:

D = |W_(USD)^(Target) - W_(USD)^(Actual)| = |28.2% - 28.5%| = 0.3%

The MARP trigger test compares the deviation to the no-trade band:

D = 0.3% < θ_(USD) = 1.0% ⇒ Trade = 0

Decision outcome. No trade is executed. The deviation is recorded in the
decision log with the trigger evaluation and the null trade (Chapter
24), the actual weight is carried forward, and tomorrow's calculation
re-evaluates from the new state. No money is wasted: the cost of closing
a 0.3% deviation — spread, slippage and gas — would exceed any risk or
tracking benefit, which is exactly what the no-trade zone encodes
(Chapter 10; invariant I11). A day like this is the expected common
case, not an anomaly: daily calculation does not mean daily trading.

C.2 Example 2 — Moderate Deviation (Partial Rebalancing)

Scenario. MASE posts a gold target of 27.0% while the actual gold weight
has drifted to 29.4% — for example because gold appreciated against the
currency components. The gold no-trade zone half-width is θ_{Gold} =
2.0% and the partial-rebalance coefficient is β = 0.40 [MS §69].

Math walkthrough. The deviation is:

D = |W_(Gold)^(Target) - W_(Gold)^(Actual)| = |27.0% - 29.4%| = 2.4%

The deviation exceeds the band, so the trade is triggered:

D = 2.4% > θ_(Gold) = 2.0% ⇒ trigger

The executed size is the partial correction:

Trade = β (W_(Gold)^(Target) - W_(Gold)^(Actual)) = 0.40 × (27.0% -
29.4%) = -0.96%

so 0.96 percentage points of portfolio allocation are sold from gold
initially, rather than the entire 2.4-point deviation. After execution
the actual gold weight is 28.44%, leaving a residual deviation of 1.44%
— back inside the no-trade zone.

Decision outcome. Only a fraction of the deviation is corrected per
trigger. Partial rebalancing (Chapter 10) trades urgency against market
impact: if the deviation is persistent, consecutive daily triggers walk
the basket toward the target in approximately 40% steps, each sized to
remain liquidity-friendly; if the deviation reverses, the system has not
over-traded. The decision log records target, actual, drift, executed
size and cost estimate (Chapter 24).

C.3 Example 3 — Natural-Flow Rebalancing

Scenario. A currency component is underweight and MARP determines it
requires +1.5% of portfolio allocation. Before any external trade is
considered, the natural-flow test examines incoming reserve flows —
mints and redemptions that can be directed into the underweight
component (Chapter 19) — and finds they contribute +0.9% [MS §70].

Math walkthrough. The external trade requirement is the residual after
natural flows:

TradeNeeded = 1.5% - 0.9% = 0.6%

Decision outcome. Only 0.6% requires external execution. The
natural-flow preference (locked concept 23; Section 26.5.1) means
directed mints and redemptions do the rebalancing work at zero marginal
execution cost — the flow would have arrived anyway, so using it to
close the deviation converts an unavoidable balance-sheet event into
free rebalancing. The executed external trade, if any, then passes
through the same cost-benefit gate as Example 4 before it is sent. The
treasury sweep (Chapter 20) composes with this mechanism by keeping
operational balances lean so that flows route through the reserve rather
than accumulating idle cash.

C.4 Example 4 — Cost-Gate Rejection

Scenario. A component is technically outside its preferred target, so a
trade is eligible by the no-trade-zone test. MARP now runs the
cost-benefit gate: the estimated benefit of the correction — risk
reduction, tracking improvement — is B = 0.12% of portfolio value, while
the expected execution cost — spread, slippage, fees — is C = 0.17% [MS
§71].

Math walkthrough. The gate compares benefit to cost:

B - C = 0.12% - 0.17% = -0.05% < 0

Decision outcome. The net value of the trade is negative, so:

Trade = 0

even though the portfolio is technically outside its preferred target.
This is exactly the behavior the architecture wants: the system refuses
to execute value-destructive corrections and carries the deviation
instead, re-testing every day as conditions — and costs — evolve. Two
overrides exist and both are deliberate: a risk trigger (for example a
crisis-score breach, Section 5.5) can justify a solvency-protecting
trade on risk grounds rather than economic grounds, and the emergency
path (Chapter 18) supersedes normal gating entirely. Absent those, a
deviation that is cheaper to keep than to fix is kept, and the decision
log records the rejection with its economics so the choice is auditable.

C.5 Example 5 — Gold Adaptive Shift

Scenario. The strategic prior for gold is W_{Gold}^{Prior} = 26%. On a
given review, MASE calculates a gold target of 29.2% because:
purchasing-power protection improved at the higher weight,
diversification improved, crisis probability increased, and the marginal
risk contribution of the additional gold remained acceptable [MS §72].

Math walkthrough. The target is the optimizer's output, not the prior:

W_(Gold)^(Target) = 29.2% ≠ W_(Gold)^(Prior) = 26%

The proposed target is checked against the gold admissibility envelope
(20–32%, Section 8.1) — 29.2% is admissible — and against the velocity
limit; the StrategicDrift penalty (Section 7.1) has already priced the
3.2-point departure from the prior inside the objective, so the
deviation is paid for in the optimization, not forbidden.

Decision outcome. The live target becomes 29.2%, not 26%. Conversely, if
conditions change — crisis probability falls, gold correlations shift —
MASE could calculate 23.8% without violating the design. Thus 26% is not
fixed: it is the starting prior, and the same architecture that moved
gold to 29.2% can move it back. The executed path then runs through
smoothing (Section 8.4) and the MARP gates of Examples 1–4, so even a
large target shift lands as a sequence of partial, cost-justified
corrections. This example is the operational proof of the monetary
principle of Section 26.5.2: fixed methodology, adaptive composition.

Appendix D — Testnet Deployment Specifics

D.1 Target Chains

The v2.0 testnet edition is deployed on three development networks: Arc,
Monad and Solana Devnet [BP §15.7; BP §13.7.4]. Arc and Monad are
EVM-compatible and share the deployment sequence of Appendix B,
including the EVM-style Genesis Reserve Safe; Solana Devnet requires the
chain-native equivalent mechanism for the genesis reserve, and the
honest status string of Listing 15 names all three networks. The
public-testnet validation gate (Gate 5, Section 25.5) additionally
contemplates a public testnet such as Arbitrum Sepolia with 100+
simulated users, which is a distinct, later stage than the devnet
deployments described here. Chain selection is deliberate rather than
convenient: deploying on three heterogeneous networks exercises the
portability of the oracle layer (Chapter 17), the multi-chain deployment
discipline of the genesis sequence (Phase 3), and the invariant that
honest status is chain-independent — the same query must return the same
truth on every network the contracts inhabit.

D.2 Genesis Verification on Testnet (Manual Emission)

On testnets the deployer must emit the GenesisVerification event
manually after the genesis transaction by calling an internal
_emitGenesisVerification() function, so that the event is present in the
testnet logs for audit testing [BP §2.6.3]. The call is placed inside
genesis() after the base denominator is set:

  // Inside the genesis() function, after setting the base denominator:

  _emitGenesisVerification();

  function _emitGenesisVerification() internal {

  emit GenesisVerification(

  GFB_BASE_DENOMINATOR,

  BASE_EUR_USD,

  BASE_GBP_USD,

  BASE_JPY_USD,

  BASE_CNY_USD,

  BASE_CHF_USD, // v2.0: seven-component prior includes CHF

  BASE_GOLD_USD, // v2.0: gold base fixing

  block.timestamp

  );

  }

  Fidelity note: the v1.2 snippet emitted five currency fixings; the
  v2.0 genesis snapshot has seven components (six currencies plus gold,
  Section 3.4), so CHF and the gold fixing are added to the event. The
  developer checklist for this step is Phase 3 of Appendix B [BP
  §2.6.4].

D.3 Mock Oracles, Mock Assets and the Mock Aggregator

Everything on the devnets is simulated, and the honest-status
declaration states it (Section 25.7; Section 26.8.2) [BP §16.4]:

-   Oracles — MOCKED. All price feeds (FX pairs, gold, VIX, DXY) are
    simulated: mock feeds are deployed behind the same OracleFeed
    interface, staleness/confidence/deviation checks are enforced
    against the mock heartbeat, and the canonical gold price quorum
    logic runs unchanged against mock sources, so the full code path of
    Chapter 17 is exercised.

-   Assets — MOCKED. All ERC-20 reserve assets are testnet mocks, except
    native USDC on Arc. Mock assets exercise the Asset Admission
    Registry, haircut application and the staged-liquidation ladder of
    Chapter 21 without touching real value.

-   Aggregator — MOCK. The multi-source aggregator consumes mock feeds
    exactly as it would consume production feeds, including timestamp,
    confidence and deviation rejection paths, so oracle-failure behavior
    is testable on demand.

The mock environment is a feature, not a compromise: because the
honest-status contract always reports NOT PRODUCTION-AUTHORIZED while
any gate is open, the devnet deployment can be used for audit rehearsal,
incident-response drills and community familiarization without any risk
of the testnet state being mistaken for production state.

D.4 Asset-Specific Oracle Address Table

The contract must be deployed with the correct oracle addresses for the
target chain. The table below carries the reference configuration; the
placeholder addresses are Arbitrum-Mainnet-style feed slots to be
replaced with the correct addresses for the target chain (devnet feeds
point at the mocks of Section D.3) [BP §9.5]:

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Asset / Pair      Chainlink Feed    Pyth Feed ID      Chronicle Feed
  ----------------- ----------------- ----------------- ---------------------
  EUR/USD           0x…EURUSD         0x…EURUSD_Pyth    0x…EURUSD_Chronicle

  GBP/USD           0x…GBPUSD         0x…GBPUSD_Pyth    0x…GBPUSD_Chronicle

  JPY/USD           0x…JPYUSD         0x…JPYUSD_Pyth    0x…JPYUSD_Chronicle

  CNY/USD (CNH)     0x…CNYUSD         0x…CNYUSD_Pyth    0x…CNYUSD_Chronicle

  PAXG (Gold)       0x…PAXG           0x…PAXG_Pyth      0x…PAXG_Chronicle

  VIX               0x…VIX            0x…VIX_Pyth       0x…VIX_Chronicle

  DXY               0x…DXY            0x…DXY_Pyth       0x…DXY_Chronicle
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

Feeds are registered through the configuration map:

  struct OracleFeed {

  address chainlink;

  bytes32 pyth;

  address chronicle;

  uint256 stalenessThreshold; // in seconds

  uint256 minPrice;

  uint256 maxPrice;

  }

  mapping(bytes32 => OracleFeed) public oracleFeeds;

D.5 Testnet Honest-Status Declaration

The testnet deployment must include the honest-status contract of
Listing 15, and its outputs are part of the deployment verification
(Appendix B, Phase 5). The declaration in force on the devnets is:

-   getHonestStatus() returns the adaptive-methodology status string:
    deployed on Arc / Monad / Solana Devnet; not production-authorized;
    all oracles and assets simulated; live weights are validation-stage
    values; the strategic prior is a starting anchor; no percentage is a
    final optimal weight until all 11 gates pass.

-   getValidationGates() returns the 11-gate list of Section 25.5; all
    gates read as not passed.

-   isProductionAuthorized() returns false, deriving from gate state
    rather than a constant.

Any dashboard, block explorer integration or documentation describing
the devnet deployment must quote these functions rather than
paraphrasing them — the on-chain string is the canonical statement, and
the messaging guide of Section 25.6 governs every other description.

D.6 Testnet Verification Sequence

The full Foundry command set for deployment verification is Phase 5 of
Appendix B; the testnet-specific sequence adds the genesis-verification
and honest-status steps in order [BP §13.7.4]:

1.  1. Verify the base denominator: cast call $MTQ_CONTRACT
    "verifyGFBBase()" --rpc-url $RPC — the returned denominator must
    match the base fixings of the genesis snapshot (Section 3.4).

2.  2. Confirm the GenesisVerification event is present in the genesis
    transaction logs (manual emission, Section D.2).

3.  3. Check the Genesis Reserve token balance and circulating supply:
    cast call $MTQ_CONTRACT "balanceOf(address)"
    $GENESIS_RESERVE_ADDRESS --rpc-url $RPC — circulating supply must be
    zero at genesis.

4.  4. Check the operational and treasury balances: cast balance
    $MTQ_CONTRACT --rpc-url $RPC and cast balance $COLD_TREASURY
    --rpc-url $RPC.

5.  5. Query the honest-status contract: getHonestStatus(),
    getValidationGates(), isProductionAuthorized() — the last must
    return false.

6.  6. Record all results in the deployment documentation and publish
    via the transparency layer (Chapter 24).

E.2 Using the Lineage Map

The map serves three purposes. First, audit routing: an auditor who
wants to verify a claim against its origin can jump from any chapter to
the exact source sections it carries — for example, the no-trade-zone
mechanics of Chapter 10 trace to MS §35–43 and COO-14/15, and the
honest-status declarations of Chapter 25 trace to BP §15 in full.
Second, change control: when a source document is revised (a new
blueprint version, a new COO review round), the map identifies precisely
which chapters of the master document are affected, so a version bump
touches only the chapters whose lineage moved. Third, provenance of
adaptations: where this document departs from a source — fixed
quantities replaced by methodology references (Chapters 3, 9, 25, 26),
fixed gold percentages replaced by adaptive allocation (Chapter 3), the
five-currency genesis extended to seven components (Appendix D.2) — the
departure is always an application of the Modification Specification's
supersession rules, never an untracked edit. The single-source-of-truth
declaration of Section 26.8.3 governs any conflict between this document
and its sources: this document wins, and the map documents why.
